# Implementation Plan: Mission Status Read API, mission detail, work packages and artifacts (Java service)

**Branch**: `issue-957-mission-status-read-api-mission-detail` | **Date**: 2026-10-13 | **Spec**: [spec.md](spec.md)
**Input**: Mission specification `kitty-specs/mission-status-read-api-mission-detail-01M4S6D5/spec.md` (closed; binding, including the operator rulings of 2026-10-13 rounds 1 and 2 and the seven plan obligations of review round 3; the operator rulings of round 3 (section 14, E-1 to E-9, D-148 to D-156) bind this plan).

**Branch contract.** Current branch at plan start: `issue-957-mission-status-read-api-mission-detail`. Planning/base branch: the same (`branch_matches_target` true). Pull request shape (operator ruling E-8, 2026-10-13, D-152; it supersedes the one-PR part of D-54, whose stacking on #5529 stands): TWO DRAFT pull requests. **PR A = P0 to P8** (ports, primitives, mission and Work Package detail groundwork; no route is served) stacks on the branch that carries #5529's last slice. **PR B = P9 to P13** (the route phases, then CI, native, nightly, README, perf and full-flow tests) stacks on PR A. Each shows only its own commits and each is self-contained and green at its own tip (section 11, "Two pull requests"). The operator retargets PR A to `main` after #5529 lands and PR B after PR A lands. No agent pushes, opens or retargets a pull request. #957 cannot merge before #5529 (C-005), and PR B cannot merge before PR A. #5533's Java work stacks on PR A.

Companion artifacts: the three tracer files `traces/approach.md`, `traces/design-decisions.md` (this plan's decisions are D-68 onward) and `traces/tooling-friction.md`; `traces/baseline-red.yaml` is written by the baseline precondition P0 and `traces/nfr009-base.md` by phase P3 (section 6, items 1 to 5). No `research.md` or `data-model.md` is written by the plan phase: P2 creates `research.md` (P6 appends to it) and P3a creates `data-model.md` (P4 and P4b append to it), each as its first commit. Section 14 lists the spec amendments and escalations this plan cannot decide. **Section 15 is the operator's binding ruling A3 ("operator 2026-10-13, #5529 landing finding A3"): the inbound port projects.** It extends P1 and inserts P1a and P1b before any functional phase; it is implemented, not weighed. **Section 1.1 is the operator's binding ruling of 2026-10-13, "CQRS side-first layout, query side now" (D-131):** it supersedes the package-convention ruling (D-130) except its `dto/` and `exceptions/` subpackages, and every package path in this plan is a path of that layout; the mapping table of section 1.1 is the one place that relates the flat checkout to it.

In this plan "phase" (P0 to P13, with the lettered insertions P1a, P1b, P3a and P4b) names a unit of work. **P0 is not a work-package candidate** (C-011 and FR-021 call the baseline "a recorded precondition, not a Work Package"): the orchestrator executes it before any Work Package is claimed, and the tasks stage starts at P1, which is the first Work Package (the campsite). P1 to P13 and the lettered phases are work-package candidates; the tasks stage maps them to WP files and keeps their write scopes (section 11). Paths are relative to the repository root unless marked: `S` = `services/mission-status-api`, `J` = `S/src/main/java/org/speckitty/missionstatus`, `T` = `S/src/test/java/org/speckitty/missionstatus`, `R` = `S/src/test/resources`, `M` = `kitty-specs/mission-status-read-api-mission-detail-01M4S6D5`.

## Summary

The Java service of #5529 (`S`, Java 25, Spring Boot 4.1 MVC on virtual threads, GraalVM native on four targets, hexagonal and ArchUnit-enforced) answers three operations. This mission adds the **detail half**, five operations of the frozen contract: `getMission`, `getWorkPackage`, `getWorkPackageDetail`, `listArtifacts`, `getArtifactContent`. It does so without editing the contract (C-001), without a Python `src/` change (C-007) and without a new workflow file.

The design in one paragraph. A new **capped, allowlisted text-read port** (`application.query.port.out.TextSource`, implemented only in `adapter.out.filesystem`) is the single reader for every new route and, later, for #5533. The read fence of #5529 is amended **atomically in one phase**: ten new `Allowlist.PathClass` values, eleven new `Allowlist.ENTRIES` lines (twenty-two in all), a **read scope** (AUTHORED or ARTIFACT) that is an input of the classification, per-route access sets and a positive control for every class. A second outbound port decodes Work Package frontmatter (YAML stays out of `application`), a third decodes `lanes.json`. A shared **Work Package index** (`application.query.index.WorkPackageIndex`) defines what a Work Package is for three routes. The domain reduction grows the resolved Work Package state (FR-020). Four inbound interfaces in `application.query.port.in` carry every rule; the controllers in `adapter.in.http.detail` only parse a transport value, call the port, map a typed outcome and shape JSON (rule 15), and a test-only plain-Java caller proves no HTTP type is needed (AC-13.3). An `AdmissionGate` behind the port bounds the heavy operations. Python-side the existing conformance generator is **extended, not duplicated**, with the detail and artifact fixture classes; goldens are produced by the existing detail and artifact oracles. The work starts with a recorded baseline (FR-021), a behaviour-preserving campsite commit (C-011) and the FR-020 reduction, and the primitives phase lands before any route phase because #5533 imports it. The operator's ruling A3 (section 15) adds one more structural step in front of all of that: the live-stream projection, the admission cap and the Mission head projection move behind the inbound port (P1 for the head, P1a for the stream, P1b for the ArchUnit gates), so that the detail routes and a later MCP adapter (#6116) cannot bypass them. The operator's ruling of the same day (section 1.1) fixes where every class of the mission sits: the side-first tree `application.query`, `domain.readmodel`, `domain.redaction`, query side only (`application.command` is reserved and is not created).

## Technical Context

**Language/Version**: Java 25 for the service; Python 3.11+ (the checkout's `.venv`) only for the oracle extension and the pin tests
**Primary Dependencies**: unchanged from #5529 (Spring Boot 4.1.1, Jackson 3, snakeyaml from the version catalog, ArchUnit, networknt json-schema-validator, JaCoCo, openapi-generator 7.25.0). **No new dependency** is planned (AC-19.4); if the YAML spike (P6) finds snakeyaml unusable for the YAML 1.2 subset the corpus needs, that is an escalation, not a quiet addition (section 14, E-6)
**Storage**: none written; reads files only, through `TextSource` and the existing `MissionEventSource`
**Testing**: JUnit 5 + ArchUnit + contract validation + golden suites on the JVM leg (the CI job `jvm` runs `./gradlew check`; phases run the targeted Gate G of section 11); on each native leg `nativeTest` runs only the `@Tag("native")` subset plus the smoke replay (CI only); Python `pytest` for the golden-honesty test, the baseline record and the CI pins
**Target Platform**: Linux x64, Linux arm64, macOS arm64, Windows x64 native binaries; loopback only
**Project Type**: single standalone service (existing tree)
**Performance Goals**: NFR-001 (`getMission` 40 files p95 under 100 ms, `getWorkPackageDetail` under 100 ms, `listArtifacts` of 1000 files under 1500 ms, `getArtifactContent` of 262144 bytes under 100 ms), NFR-002 (bounded memory), NFR-009 (cache growth at most 64 MB). All INITIAL and PROVISIONAL (ADR `2026-10-05-2`): ratio to a same-run floor is the primary assertion, the figure is the ceiling beside it, a miss is classified, never retried
**Constraints**: read-only, no process, no lock, no write mode on any path; loopback, Host and Origin guard unchanged; no secret or host path in a response or log except `promptMarkdown` when `includePrompt=true`; byte-identical on four OS targets; nothing in the wheel
**Scale/Scope**: 609 tracked Missions in this repository's corpus (`git ls-files 'kitty-specs/*/meta.json'` at the design base; 607 on `origin/main`) (the nightly corpus job compares all of them); a fixture project of 500 Missions for NFR-001

## Charter Check

Charter read (`.kittify/charter/charter.md`, version 1.5.0, 2026-10-06), then `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` (byte-identical, 93834 bytes each), then `SPEC-KITTY-LEDGER.md` (entries SK-310 (two headings carry the id; both were read, see "Ledger entries in force"), SK-351, SK-353, SK-355, SK-363, and for the tasks and approval stages SK-300, SK-356, SK-357, SK-361: see "Ledger entries in force" below). The charter wins on any disagreement. **Drift found**: (1) the charter's Testing Requirements say "pytest with 90%+ coverage" and "mypy --strict must pass"; no CI job runs mypy and no charter text names Java (the same silence #5529 recorded); this plan holds the Java tree to its own per-class JaCoCo floors and changes no charter text (C-010). (2) `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` describe `docs/` and `src/` structure for the Python CLI and are silent on `services/`; the service README is the local authority there.

| Charter rule | Verdict |
|---|---|
| Single canonical authority; reconcile, do not duplicate | Pass. One head derivation (`OverviewReader`, extracted by the campsite, serves `/missions` and `getMission`: AC-1.2), one text-read port (one strict UTF-8 decode for every read this mission adds; the three older copies in #5529 are named and left, section 9, D-116), one redaction function (`TextScreen` and `Projector` over one `LeakClasses`), one Work Package index for three routes, one extended conformance generator. The Python oracle stays the existing test-side helpers; no third oracle |
| Architectural alignment; DDD with tiered rigour | Pass. Hexagonal, side-first layout (section 1.1), ArchUnit rules 1 to 12 keep their ids (rules 3 and 4 are re-scoped to `application.query.port.in` and `.out` by #5529's re-cut; rule 3 is generalised to every `adapter.in.<x>` and gains a controller-confinement part, rules 1 and 2 gain a reflection part, by the operator's ruling A3), rule 13 (ports are interfaces only, data in `dto/`, exceptions in `exceptions/`) and rule 14 (`application.query` never depends on `application.command`) added at P1b, rules 15 and 16 (the detail controllers, the inbound interfaces) added at P8; JaCoCo class floors (line 0.90 domain and application, branch 0.80 domain) on every new domain and application class |
| ATDD-first | Pass. Each phase states its own red-first form in section 11: red on the phase base and green on its tip for P3 and P5 to P13; for P1 "every existing test unchanged and green on base and tip"; for P0, P4 and P4b "the record and golden tests are red on a copy with a row, module or byte removed"; for P2 the drift-probe and skip-report mutations; P3a has no behaviour. The reviewer verifies the form of the phase's own row |

**Ledger entries in force for the tasks and approval stages** (verified in the ledger, which lives one directory above the repository): **SK-357** (open): `finalize-tasks` puts any overlapping pair of `owned_files` in one lane and can report `LANE_DEPENDENCY_CYCLE`, so section 11 gives every shared file one owner and names the other writers; **SK-300**: the approval gate demands issue-matrix rows that `finalize-tasks` never scaffolded and reveals its gates one failure at a time, so the orchestrator computes the cited `#NN` set across `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `tasks/` before the first approval; **SK-361**: a `#NN` that is not an issue (this plan cites `#5838`, `#5927`, `#6116`, `#5528`, and standards-style references would too) is classed as a gating issue, so the workaround is `agent issue-verdict --verdict not-applicable` with an evidence reference for each false positive; **SK-356**: `wps.yaml` `plan_concern_refs` must match `IC-##`, so this plan uses no `IC-##a` headings (it has no plan-concern headings at all); **SK-353** is carried as AC-6.4 and decided in D-97; **SK-310, the `finalize-tasks` entry** ("does not re-sync `owned_files` from `wps.yaml` once a WP is bootstrapped"): an `owned_files` edit after bootstrap is inert and a mid-mission scope widening is a hand edit of frontmatter, so the shared-file table of section 11 must be right before `finalize-tasks` runs. The other SK-310 heading ("plan completion guard reads the coordination status surface instead of the primary planning artifact surface") bites the completion of this plan stage, is fixed in `v4.0.0rc1+`, and is noted, not relied on.

Post-design re-check: no new violation. Two observations for the charter owner, recorded and not acted on: the charter's "Programme PR Workflow" says nothing on GitHub enforces required checks, consistent with section 5 (whether `Mission Status API gate` is a required check is unverifiable from the checkout); and the Standing Orders table says "nine" practices while `AGENTS.md` says "eight".

| JSON writer | `MissionStatusApplicationJson.mapper()` sorts properties alphabetically and map entries by key (`ORDER_MAP_ENTRIES_BY_KEYS`), writes nulls, and `CanonicalJsonMessageConverter.canWrite` accepts only classes under `org.speckitty.missionstatus.adapter.in.http.` (so a hand DTO in `adapter.in.http.dto` or `.detail` is written canonically). The generated models write required-nullable members as `null`; `ProblemBody` (NON_NULL) is the error side |
| Existing readers and their limits | `SafeFiles.readRegular(file, max, refused, unreadable)` reads in 64 KiB chunks and tests `out.size() > max` after each chunk, so it can hold up to one chunk beyond the cap (the spec's "at most cap + 1 bytes" is not true of it: section 14, S-17). `NoUncappedReadTest` allows `openRead` calls only in `SafeFiles`, the ledger source, `AllowlistPassThrough` and `AllowlistAccess` (its `MAY_OPEN_FOR_READ`) |
| Meta reads per request, existing source | `MissionStatusService.resolve` (checkout name) calls `readMeta` for each listed Mission until the match and then `probed`, whose `source.stampLedger` resolves the read directory, which reads the matched Mission's `meta.json` a second time: **2 reads of the matched Mission on a stream route**. `overview(ref)` (the `/missions` item) calls `readMeta`, then `stampLedger` and (cache miss) `readLedger`, each of which resolves the read directory again: 2 warm, 3 cold. A detail ledger route that goes `MissionLookup.find` (1) and then `OverviewReader.overview(ref)` (`readMeta` 2, the ledger stamp 3, on a miss `readLedger` 4) and **never calls `probed()`** reads the matched Mission 3 times warm and 4 cold (obligation FRESH3-002, D-104); with a separate probe step it would be 4 and 5 |
| `lastActivityAt` | `OverviewDeriver.lastActivityAt` runs `Instants.parse` on every Work Package's `lastTransitionAt`; a non-instant there already refuses the whole head as `ledger_unparseable` (M3) before any Work Package is projected (used by FRESH3-003) |
| Package layout at the checkout | Flat and pre-re-cut: `application/{MissionStatusService,ProjectionCache,ChangeFeed,StreamSubscriber,Resume,StreamSettings}`, `application/port/in` and `application/port/out` hold interfaces, records and exceptions together (`Change`, `ChangeBatch`, `StreamSignal`, `StreamRefused`, `SourceFault`, `MissionRef` and the others), `domain/{cursor,model,overview,paging,projection,reducer}`. There is no `application/query`, no `dto/` or `exceptions/` subpackage, no `domain/readmodel`, no `domain/redaction` and no `MissionStatusQueryService`. The re-cut is #5529's and is not in this checkout, so **no path of the side-first layout is observable here** (R-35, Unverified 18); `git ls-files 'services/mission-status-api/src/**' \| grep -c 'application/query'` prints 0 |
| Class names held as string literals (invisible to the compiler during a move) | `testsupport/FilesystemFixtureFactory#fromTreeWithOrdering` loads the package-private `ProjectionCache` with `Class.forName("org.speckitty.missionstatus.application.ProjectionCache")` and reflects the package-private seven-argument `MissionStatusService` constructor `(MissionEventSource, Clock, Consumer, ScheduledExecutorService, StreamSettings, LeakClasses, ProjectionCache)`; `architecture/ArchitectureRulesTest` names `...application.MissionStatusService` in two `contain` assertions (the `isFalse` one passes vacuously on a stale name) and `architecture/PortLevelClassSetTest` names it in `haveFullyQualifiedName`; `build.gradle.kts` holds the two JaCoCo patterns `...domain.*` and `...application.*`. These are the sites the C-005 re-read of section 1.1 checks by grep and by `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest`. The one non-Java site is the pin `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py`: its `NEW_FILES_IN_OUT_PACKAGES` probes the flat directories `application/port` and `architecture/fixture/application/port` by path, and `grep -rnE 'application/port\|domain/(overview\|reducer\|projection\|paging\|cursor)' tests scripts .github` finds exactly the three lines of that tuple at the checkout (PLAN-FRESH4-007) |

## 1. Seam: where the change lands

### 1.1 The layout: operator 2026-10-13, "CQRS side-first layout, query side now" (D-131)

**Attribution (quoted for the decision record): "operator 2026-10-13: CQRS side-first layout, query side now".** The service is going fully CQRS once a write module arrives, so #5529 is getting a side-first layout now. The ruling is **binding on this plan: it is implemented, not weighed**. It **supersedes the package-convention ruling of the same date (D-130) except that ruling's `dto/` and `exceptions/` subpackages**, which it keeps. The target tree (package root `org.speckitty.missionstatus`; `J` = its directory):

```text
application.query/                  MissionStatusQueryService (renamed from MissionStatusService), ProjectionCache
application.query.stream/           ChangeFeed, StreamSubscriber, Resume, StreamSettings
application.query.port.in/          interfaces only: MissionStatusQuery, Subscription
application.query.port.in.dto/      application.query.port.in.exceptions/
application.query.port.out/         interfaces only: MissionEventSource
application.query.port.out.dto/     application.query.port.out.exceptions/
application.command/                reserved for the future write side. NOT created by this mission:
                                    no class, no package, no directory, no empty placeholder
domain.readmodel/                   overview/, reducer/, paging/, cursor/ (moved from domain/*)
domain.model/                       shared events and value objects (unchanged)
domain.redaction/                   renamed from domain.projection: LeakClasses, Projector (and ProjectionError)
adapter.in.http, adapter.out.filesystem   unchanged
```

ArchUnit pins three things: **ports are interfaces only** (rule 13), **`application.query` never depends on `application.command`** (rule 14), and **the existing layering** (rules 1 to 12, re-scoped to the new packages). The exact rule texts, subjects and planted controls are in section 13.

**Where this mission's classes go (the placement rule, from the first commit of each).**

- **Services of the new routes** (`MissionDetailQueryService`, `WorkPackageQueryService`, `WorkPackageDetailQueryService`, `ArtifactQueryService`, named after the ports they implement as `MissionStatusQueryService` is named after `MissionStatusQuery`) and `AdmissionGate`, `MissionLookup`, `OverviewReader`, `MissionReadModel`, `MissionHeadProjection`, `MissionHead` live **directly in `application.query`**, beside `ProjectionCache`. That is deliberate: `ProjectionCache` and the extracted classes are package-private, and the detail services use them by package visibility (D-93); a sub-package such as `application.query.detail` for the services would force a public `ProjectionCache`. Helpers that need no cache access sit in sub-packages: `application.query.index` (the Work Package index), `application.query.detail` (the `getWorkPackageDetail` readers), `application.query.artifact` (the walk and the reader).
- **Stream additions of the ruling A3** (`EventProjection`, `ProjectedSubscription`, `StreamAdmission`, `FeedSubscription`, `FeedSignal`, and the moved `Change` and `ChangeBatch`) live in `application.query.stream` with `ChangeFeed`, `StreamSubscriber`, `Resume` and `StreamSettings`. The service in the parent package constructs them, so the classes P1a adds are `public`; that is application-internal visibility only (rules 3 and 4 let no adapter depend on `application.query.stream`). The visibility of the #5529 classes the re-cut moves into that package (`ChangeFeed` and its nested records are package-private at the checkout) is the re-cut's; P1a's first step reads it from the tip and, if a moved class stayed package-private while the service needs it, widens it as a named edit.
- **Ports.** The four new inbound interfaces in `application.query.port.in`; the three new outbound interfaces in `application.query.port.out`; everything else a port exchanges in `dto/`, every contract exception in `exceptions/` (list in section 13).
- **New read models** in `domain.readmodel.<x>`: the result records and enums in `domain.readmodel.detail`, the framework-free derivations in `domain.readmodel.derive`, the reducer additions in `domain.readmodel.reducer`. Work Package records shared by the reduction, the decoders and the index stay in `domain.model`. The text screen is `domain.redaction.TextScreen` beside `LeakClasses` and `Projector`.
- **`application.command` is not created.** No class of this mission belongs to the write side. The future write module adds its own package, its own JaCoCo count and its own ArchUnit subject; rule 14 is what keeps this side clean until then.

**Reconciling "every data type a port exchanges lives in `dto/`" with the domain read models the ports return (PLAN-FRESH3-011).** The sentence is about the port's own vocabulary: requests, stream values, the file-system view (`FileRef`, `TextRead`, ...) and the port-local refusals. **A result that is a domain read model is not a port data type**: `MissionStatusQuery` has always returned `MissionOverview`, `MissionPage` and `ProjectHeader` of `domain.readmodel.overview`, and the four new interfaces return the records of `domain.readmodel.detail` in the same way, as the operator's tree prescribes (`domain.readmodel/` holds the read models; `domain.model/` the shared events and value objects). So read models and `domain.model` values stay in the domain and the port's `dto/` packages hold only what the port itself defines. Rule 13 checks the package layout of `application.query.port..` and is silent about the domain on purpose; rule 15 bounds what the controllers may take from the domain. If the operator meant that the detail results move into `port/in/dto`, rule 15 and P8's scope change and nothing else (E-10).

**The mapping table: checkout path (flat, today) to the path used in this plan.** `J`-relative; the re-cut is #5529's landing refactor (D-130, D-131) and **no phase of this mission performs it**. Rows 1 to 11 are the single place where old and new are related; a path elsewhere in the plan is always a new one, except where a section states that it quotes the checkout (the readiness facts, FRESH3-002 in section 8, 15.1).

| # | Class or group (#5529) | Checkout path | Path in this plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the query service | `application/MissionStatusService` | `application/query/MissionStatusQueryService` |
| 2 | the reduction cache (package-private) | `application/ProjectionCache` | `application/query/ProjectionCache` (package-private) |
| 3 | the raw feed and the stream settings | `application/{ChangeFeed,StreamSubscriber,Resume,StreamSettings}` | `application/query/stream/{ChangeFeed,StreamSubscriber,Resume,StreamSettings}` |
| 4 | inbound interfaces | `application/port/in/{MissionStatusQuery,Subscription}` | `application/query/port/in/{MissionStatusQuery,Subscription}` |
| 5 | inbound data | `application/port/in/{Change,ChangeBatch,Closure,PageRequest,StreamCursorText,StreamScope,StreamSignal}` | `application/query/port/in/dto/{...}`; `Change` and `ChangeBatch` leave it again for `application/query/stream/` in P1a |
| 6 | inbound exceptions | `application/port/in/{MissionStatusUnavailable,StreamRefused}` | `application/query/port/in/exceptions/{MissionStatusUnavailable,StreamRefused}` |
| 7 | outbound interface | `application/port/out/MissionEventSource` | `application/query/port/out/MissionEventSource` |
| 8 | outbound data | `application/port/out/{LedgerRead,LedgerStamp,LedgerWindow,MissionRef,ProjectInputs}` | `application/query/port/out/dto/{...}` |
| 9 | outbound exception | `application/port/out/SourceFault` | `application/query/port/out/exceptions/SourceFault` |
| 10 | read models, reduction, paging, cursors | `domain/{overview,reducer,paging,cursor}/` | `domain/readmodel/{overview,reducer,paging,cursor}/` |
| 11 | redaction | `domain/projection/{LeakClasses,Projector,ProjectionError}` | `domain/redaction/{LeakClasses,Projector,ProjectionError}` |
| 12 | unchanged | `domain/model/`, `adapter/**`, `config/**` | same |
| 13 | tests and fixtures | `T/application/**`, `T/domain/{overview,reducer,paging,cursor,projection}/**`, `T/architecture/fixture/application/**` | the test package mirrors the main package of the class it tests (`T/application/query/**`, `T/application/query/stream/**`, `T/domain/readmodel/**`, `T/domain/redaction/**`); the ArchUnit fixtures sit under `T/architecture/fixture/application/query/**`, so the conforming skeleton (`Service`, `Query`, `Source`) and the planted `A2*`, `A6`, `A8`, `A9` classes are re-pathed with the rules that read them |

**The C-005 re-read procedure (replaces "re-read after the re-cut").** (1) **P1 cannot start before the orchestrator has merged #5529's re-cut tip into this branch**; until then none of the paths of this plan exists (Unverified 18). (2) The first step of P1, and of every phase after an orchestrator-run merge of a #5529 tip, is `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` (section 5). It is written against the table above: for every row 1 to 11 it loads the class by its new name (`Class.forName`) and asserts that the checkout name does not load, it asserts the shape facts of the readiness table (the package-private `ProjectionCache`, the public six-argument and the package-private seven-argument `MissionStatusQueryService` constructors, `SourceFault.Kind` with its five values, the lower bounds), and that every production class sits under `adapter`, `application`, `config` or `domain` (no sibling top-level package can escape the JaCoCo patterns and the rules). It is red on the flat checkout by design, and P1a edits its two row-5 assertions for `Change` and `ChangeBatch` (which leave `port/in/dto` for `application/query/stream/`) in the commit that moves them. (3) The string-literal sites of the readiness table are checked by the grep of section 5 (`"org.speckitty.missionstatus.(application|domain)` in `S/src/test` and `S/build.gradle.kts`): each hit must name a class of the table's right-hand column or one of the JaCoCo wildcards. (3a) **The one non-Java string site is the pin's probe list (PLAN-FRESH4-007, D-143).** `NEW_FILES_IN_OUT_PACKAGES` in `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` builds `src/<tree>/java/org/speckitty/missionstatus/<package>/out/NewFile.java` for the flat packages `application/port` (main and test) and `architecture/fixture/application/port`, and asks `git check-ignore` about each. After the re-cut those directories do not exist, the probes stay green, and the guard behind the naming hygiene of 15.7 (a new file in an `out` package, now `application/query/port/out` with its `dto/` and `exceptions/` subpackages, must not be ignored) quietly stops testing the packages this mission fills. **P1 makes that edit as a named edit (it is the first phase on the re-cut tree and runs the pin in its gate; P13 stays the owner of the file):** the tuple becomes twelve probe paths `src/<tree>/java/org/speckitty/missionstatus/<package>/NewFile.java`, with `<package>` in `application/query/port/out`, `application/query/port/out/dto`, `application/query/port/out/exceptions` and `adapter/out` for the `main` and the `test` tree, and in `architecture/fixture/application/query/port/out`, `.../out/dto`, `.../out/exceptions` and `architecture/fixture/adapter/out` for the `test` tree; a pure function `out_probe_violations(entries)` returns a violation for any entry that still holds a flat `application/port/` or `fixture/application/port/` segment and for a missing probe, and a control in the same file passes it the six old flat entries and requires violations, so a list edited back, or never edited, is red and not vacuously green. The grep of section 5 (`application/port` and the old `domain/...` directories over `tests`, `scripts` and `.github`) is empty from the end of P1.

(4) A difference between the tip and the table is an orchestrator item: the table is amended by a decision note in both missions' records, never by a phase on its own authority. The path of any #5529 symbol cited in this plan (the coupled list of C-005 included) is the right-hand column; nothing else needs re-reading.

**ArchUnit scopes, JaCoCo patterns and write scopes, re-derived for the new packages.**

- *ArchUnit.* `ArchitectureRules` builds each rule from the root plus a package suffix. The suffixes that change are rule 3 (`application.port.in..` becomes `application.query.port.in..`) and rule 4 (`application.port.out..` becomes `application.query.port.out..`), both by the re-cut (#5529 owns `ArchitectureRules` until P1b); the rest (`domain..`, `application..`, `adapter.in.http..`, `adapter.out.filesystem..`, `.codec..`, `config..`) are prefix-stable under the new layout, because every new package sits below them. Rules 13 to 16 are written for the new names from their first commit (section 13). The conforming skeleton and the planted fixtures follow the mirror rule of row 13.
- *JaCoCo.* `jacocoScopePatterns` and the three `element = "CLASS"` rules use `org.speckitty.missionstatus.domain.*` and `org.speckitty.missionstatus.application.*`; **a JaCoCo `*` crosses package separators** (`jacocoPatternRegex` in `S/build.gradle.kts`), so both patterns cover `domain.readmodel..`, `domain.redaction`, `application.query..` and any later `application.command..` unchanged; the keys `domain` and `application` of `jacoco-class-floor.properties` stay; the moves and the rename change no class count; the count a phase writes is still the number its own command prints (section 2). The 0.80 branch floor on `domain.*` now also covers the read-model packages, as it covered their flat predecessors.
- *Write scopes.* Every `J/...` and `T/...` path of section 11 and of the shared-file table is the new path. The concurrent-set and one-owner checks were re-run on the new paths (section 11, last paragraph).

### 1.2 Layers: the classes this mission adds or changes

**Port package convention (operator 2026-10-13, D-130, kept by D-131):** `application/query/port/in` and `application/query/port/out` hold only interfaces (the ports and port-returned handles such as `Subscription`); every data type a port itself defines (records, sealed types, enums, static decoding helpers) lives in `port/{in,out}/dto`, every exception that is part of a port contract in `port/{in,out}/exceptions`. A new type of this mission is placed by this rule from its first commit; ArchUnit rule 13 (P1b) pins it. Exact classes are in section 13; the contract of each new port is in section 7 for the shared primitives.

| Layer | New (N) or changed (C) classes |
|---|---|
| `domain.model` | N: `WpId` (with a lenient `parse(String)` returning `Optional<WpId>`: P3, E-5, D-150), `WpMetadata`, `FrontmatterResult`, `LanesManifest`, `Assignment`, `ReviewResultRecord`, `ReviewOverrideRecord`, `HistoryRow`, `WpResolved`, `ReviewCycleMeta`, `LanesResult` (a sealed result with `Manifest`, `Corrupt` and `Legacy`); C: `MissionId` (+ a lenient `parse(String)` returning `Optional<MissionId>`: P8, E-5, D-150), `LedgerLine` (+ a nullable `eventType`, D-98), `DiagnosticCode` (+9 values), `StatusEvent`, `StatusSnapshot` (a parallel `resolved` map and `lifecycleTypes`; the 4-argument constructor stays) |
| `domain.readmodel.detail` (records and enums only: what the ports return) | N: `MissionDetail`, `MissionPhase`, `PhaseName`, `PhaseStatus`, `PhaseBasis`, `WorkPackageSummary`, `WorkPackageView`, `Readiness`, `SubtaskProgress`, `ReviewView`, `WorkPackageDetail`, `SubtaskEntry`, `DependencyEntry`, `ReviewCycleEntry`, `WorkspaceView`, `OwnedFileEntry`, `ArtifactReferences`, `ArtifactReference`, `ArtifactListing`, `ArtifactEntry`, `ArtifactKind`, `ArtifactContent` |
| `domain.readmodel.derive` (framework-free derivations; barred to the controllers by rule 15) | N: `PhaseDeriver`, `ReadinessDeriver` (port of `dependency_readiness_for_wp`), `LaneNames` (ports of `lane_branch_name` and `worktree_dir_name`), `OwnedFilePatterns` (`is_glob_pattern`), `TitleRows` (`title_rows`, with `PyText`), `ArtifactKinds` (the ordered classifier), `ArtifactPaths` (the artifact-path rule), `CycleNames`, `HistoryOrder` |

| `application.query.port.in` (interfaces only) | N: `MissionDetailQuery`, `WorkPackageQuery`, `WorkPackageDetailQuery`, `ArtifactQuery`; **C (P1a)**: `MissionStatusQuery` (`changesSince` returns `StreamBatch`; `+ endStreams()`) |
| `application.query.port.in.dto` (data types) | N: `ArtifactPathInput`; **N (ruling A3, P1a)**: `StreamEvent` (sealed, projected values only), `StreamBatch`, `StreamRequest`; **C (P1a)**: `StreamSignal` (`Events` replaces `Forwarded` and `Truncated`), `StreamScope` (`+ parse`), `StreamCursorText` (the unparseable detail); **removed from the port**: `Change`, `ChangeBatch` (moved into `application.query.stream`) |

| Java test-side (golden coupling, D-109) | C (paired with the manifest change in P4): `T/testsupport/ConformanceTree.java` (a `surface` partition), `T/conformance/golden/GoldenSuiteTest.java` (one assertion); N: `T/conformance/golden/DetailManifestCensusTest.java` (P4); `ServedRoute`, `ServedRoutes`, `DetailGoldenSuiteTest`, `ResolvedStateGoldenTest` (P7); one `ServedRoute` provider per route phase; `ServedRoutesCompleteTest` (P13) |
| CI | C: `.github/workflows/mission-status-api.yml` (native `--floor`, `--request-floor`, the nightly job and its `LC_ALL`), `scripts/ci/mission_status_corpus_compare.py`; C (one line each, only if required): `.github/workflows/ci-router.yml`, `tests/ci/test_mission_status_*.py` pins; **P2 edits three clauses of `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` (the models list, its generation needle and the hand-DTO directory clause), which C-004 does not admit as written (operator-approved exception: S-23, E-9, D-154)**; no `.gitattributes` edit is planned, because the root rule `services/mission-status-api/src/test/resources/conformance/** -text` already covers every new fixture directory (verified in `.gitattributes`), so the C-004 slot for it stays unused; `.github/workflows/packs.yml` and `tests/architectural/test_ci_corpus_trigger_completeness.py` are **not written** (E-2 ruled 2026-10-13: the default stands, D-149) |

**No CLI command is added or changed. Nothing reaches into kernel internals** (no Python `src/` import is added; the generator imports only `src` files that the `contract_tools` router group already names, and AC-14.6 derives that claim). **No core-loop to sync coupling**: the service never reads `config.toml` or any hosted key, spawns nothing and takes no lock (FR-016, AC-16.3).

**The inbound ports stay transport-agnostic** (issue #6116 will add Spring AI MCP resource adapters over `application.query.port.in`). No type in `application.query.port.in` or in the domain read models references Spring, `jakarta.servlet`, `org.openapitools` or a generated DTO (rules 2a, 9, 16). **What the claim covers, precisely (PLAN-FRESH4-009, D-146):** (1) *type references*, enforced by rules 2a, 9 and 16 (clause a of rule 16 includes the stream vocabulary) and proved by `PlainCaller` and `PlainStreamCallerTest`; (2) *the outcome vocabulary of the new operations*: `DetailRefusal`, `TextRefusal` and the typed results carry a kind and the contract's wire word and **no HTTP status**, and the kind-to-status mapping exists once, in `ProblemMapper` and `ProblemAdvice` of the HTTP adapter, so an MCP adapter maps the same closed set of kinds to its own error codes and duplicates no status table; (3) **a stated limit, not covered:** `StreamRefused.Kind`, a #5529 type that ruling A3 retypes around but does not redesign, carries an HTTP status per value (`status()`: 400, 409, 404, beside its wire word), which `ProblemMapper` reads and `SubscribeConnectTest` asserts five times; moving it into the adapter would change those assertions, which the behaviour-preserving contract of 15.2 forbids for P1a. A non-HTTP adapter reads `kind()` and `code()` and ignores `status()`, so it duplicates no status mapping but does meet one HTTP number on the port. P1a marks the accessor in its Javadoc as the HTTP contract's value; moving it (an exhaustive switch over `Kind` in `ProblemMapper`, the five assertions read through `kind()`) is owned by the plan of the first non-HTTP adapter, #6116, and recorded as E-11 and R-37. A controller holds only: (a) raw-query parsing (`path` strict percent decoding, `includePrompt` exact `true`/`false`), (b) one port call, (c) the mapping of a typed outcome or `DetailRefusal` to a status (`ProblemMapper`), (d) shaping JSON (`DetailMapper`). Everything else is behind the port: identifier validity (a malformed `missionId` or `wpId` is a 404, never a 400: the adapter calls `MissionId.parse(String)` / `WpId.parse(String)`, which return an `Optional`, and an empty result becomes `DetailRefusal.notFound()` (rows C2 and W1), so no adapter makes the 400-versus-404 call itself: E-5, D-150; rule 15 admits both ids because they are records of `domain.model`), the artifact-path rule and its count check, the lookup, the index, the caps, the order of decision, the substitution, the credential refusal, the diagnostics and the admission gate (FR-013, FR-022). An MCP adapter therefore parses the two identifiers with the same factories, passes a boolean and a one-element list and duplicates no rule. **The same holds for the live stream, by the operator's ruling A3 (section 15):** the port emits only a projected, sealed `StreamEvent`, refuses on capacity through `StreamCapacityReached`, decodes its own scope and cursor text (`StreamRequest`), and ends every stream on `endStreams()`; the one cap and the one shutdown registry serve every inbound adapter. **The Mission head is an application-internal seam, not an adapter-facing one (PLAN-FRESH3-008):** every inbound port derives its head through `MissionReadModel#head` (enforced by `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest`), so a second adapter gets the same projection by calling the ports; rule 3 bars an adapter from naming `MissionReadModel`, and an adapter never needs to.

## 4. Upgrade/migration chain

**Untouched.** No migration, no `.kittify` metadata change, no template, skill, pack or agent-surface change, no CLI schema version bump: `spec-kitty upgrade` has nothing to deploy and `spec-kitty regen --check` has no new input. No `src/` file changes (C-007).

**Reflexivity (Missions in flight, this one included).** The service is read-only and detached: not started by any CLI command, writes no ledger, takes no lock (FR-016, C-008). What it reads of a Mission in flight is the per-route access set of FR-011; landing adds and edits only the paths in C-004 (`packs.yml` and the corpus registry test are not written: E-2 ruled). Missions in flight therefore see no change to event logs, lanes or commands. Verified, not assumed: AC-16.1 to AC-16.3, AC-11.1 (the three existing routes still touch no new class), the fingerprint test, and a review diff check that no `src/` path, no `kitty-specs` path other than `M/` and no file outside the C-004 write set (as ruled under E-2) changed. The shared edits are listed in section 11 (chokepoints) and section 14 (S-11).

Required status checks are repository settings and not in the checkout, so whether any check is "required" is unverified. Per the charter, ruff check and ruff format are hard-enforced; nothing else in this list is.

**Which Gradle tasks are phase gates and which belong to CI (`NO_FULL_HEAVY_SUITES_IN_MISSION`, D-88; verified in `S/build.gradle.kts`).** The charter forbids implementers, reviewers and the orchestrator from running a performance, stress or timing suite or an e2e or full-integration suite during mission work; they run the targeted files, the named gate classes and the owning module's fast tier. For this module the fast tier is the `test` task. `check` is **not** a phase gate, because it depends on `perfTest` (tag `perf`), `kitTest` (TestKit nested builds) and `nativeTagJvmTest` besides `test` and the two JaCoCo tasks. **Operator exception for this mission only (E-7, ruled 2026-10-13, D-151):** before review a WP agent MAY additionally run a local `./gradlew --no-daemon check`, `nativeCompile nativeTest`, `nativeSmoke -PnativeBinary=<binary>` and the named perf measurements (the NFR-009 base figure at P3 and AC-20.5; the P13 ratio tests `Nfr001DetailLatencyTest` and `Nfr002BoundedMemoryScenarioTest`). The plan expects such runs at P6 (the first native-visible decoder), P8 (the PR A tip: `check`, then `nativeCompile nativeTest nativeSmoke`) and P13 (the PR B tip). CI remains the authority: a local green replaces no CI leg and a local red is classified under section 6, never retried to green. The exception does not name `nativePerf`, `native-tag-floor`, the `digests` comparison or the one local full-corpus nightly run, which stay CI-only / outside it.

| Task | Where it runs | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `./gradlew --no-daemon test --tests '<class>'` | every phase, red-first loop | targeted file |
| `./gradlew --no-daemon test jacocoTestCoverageVerification jacocoScopeCheck` | **every Java phase exit gate** | `test` is the module's fast tier (it excludes the tags `perf` and `native-binary`; the nested-build classes are in the separate `kit` source set); the two JaCoCo tasks run after it and are the class-floor and non-vacuity gates |
| `./gradlew --no-daemon kitTest --tests '<named nested-build class>'` | P2 only (`ContractDriftNestedBuildTest`, `VerificationTamperTest`, the skip-report class of D-101, `PlantedClassCoverageNestedBuildTest` of AC-19.3) | named files, not the nested-build suite |
| `./gradlew --no-daemon nativeTagJvmTest` | P13 only | the JVM twin of the `@Tag("native")` tests (few tests) |
| `./gradlew --no-daemon perfTest --tests 'org.speckitty.missionstatus.perf.Nfr009CacheGrowthTest'` | P3 only (the base figure and AC-20.5); **the operator's word is given (E-7, 2026-10-13)** | one named class of the `perf` tag; `S/build.gradle.kts` makes `test` exclude `perf` (`excludeTags("native-binary", "perf")`) and `perfTest` include only it, so `test --tests` would find nothing |
| `./gradlew --no-daemon --dependency-verification strict bootJar testClasses` | P2 and any phase that touches `S/build.gradle.kts` or the version catalog | the freshness check of `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` beside `VerificationTamperTest` |
| `nativePerf`, `native-tag-floor`, `digests`; and, as the authority, `check`, `nativeCompile nativeTest`, `nativeSmoke` | **CI is the authority** (`jvm`, `native` x4, `mission-status-api-gate`); `check`, `nativeCompile nativeTest` and `nativeSmoke` may also be run locally before review under the E-7 exception, `nativePerf`, `native-tag-floor` and `digests` may not | performance suite, nested builds, a GraalVM image build and replay. A local run is evidence for the reviewer and never a substitute for the CI leg |

**Gate G preconditions (PLAN-FRESH2-008, D-128).** The forked test JVM must run under a **UTF-8 file-name locale**: `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` (or any installed UTF-8 locale; `locale -a` lists them) must be set for Gate G and for every targeted `test --tests` run. Tests that create or list a non-ASCII file name (AC-6.6 `WP01-é.md`, the artifact sort corpus, the UTF-8 byte-order fixtures) depend on `sun.jnu.encoding`; under a POSIX locale the file creation throws before the service is reached, which would make Gate G red for a reason that is neither the mission's nor a baseline red. CI runs on UTF-8 runners (Linux; the native legs run only the tagged subset and the smoke binary, which sets its own locale: D-90). Defence in depth: those tests call `Utf8Host.assumeUtf8()` (P7, `T/testsupport/Utf8Host.java`), which, when `sun.jnu.encoding` is not UTF-8, appends `not run: precondition unmet: <test id>` to `build/reports/not-run.txt` (the D-101 hook, copied to the job summary) and skips the test by assumption, so a mis-set shell shows as a recorded skip and never as a red; the cases that inject the `NameEncoding` stub (AC-6.6 stub leg, AC-4.8 stub twin) run under any locale and are not skipped.

**Exact local commands per gate** (all from the repository root checkout; `.venv/bin/...`, never a bare `uv run`, which re-syncs the environment):

```text
# Python hygiene (every phase that touches a Python file)
.venv/bin/ruff check . && .venv/bin/ruff format --check .
make format-check-files FILES="<the changed .py paths>"      # per-file, honours the format-exclude ratchet
.venv/bin/ruff check --select TID251 .
.venv/bin/spec-kitty regen --check
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/architectural/test_no_legacy_terminology.py -q
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/architectural/test_layer_rules.py tests/architectural/test_pyproject_shape.py -q
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/architectural/test_archive_root_byte_identical.py -q
.venv/bin/python scripts/generate_contextive_glossaries.py check
# the Windows-filename guard reads `git ls-files`: stage or commit the new files first, or it passes vacuously
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/architectural/test_no_invalid_windows_filenames.py -q
grep -rnE '(^|[^A-Za-z])[Ff]eatures?([^A-Za-z]|$)|feature_' services/mission-status-api/src/main services/mission-status-api/README.md | grep -v 'non-numeric-number feature'    # expect empty today; later only the marked legacy literal `feature_slug` of the legacy-lanes detection (D-115). The pattern excludes Jackson's `*Feature` identifiers (`JsonReadFeature`, `MapperFeature`, `SerializationFeature`, `DateTimeFeature`), and the filter drops the two Javadoc lines about Jackson's non-numeric-number feature (`ReadDirResolverJson`, `PythonJson`); both verified on the checkout
# the oracle, the goldens and this mission's contract modules (targeted; corpus-marked, so the marker selector is required)
.venv/bin/python -m tests.contract._mission_status_conformance --check
PWHEADLESS=1 .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/contract/test_mission_status_conformance.py tests/contract/test_mission_status_detail.py tests/contract/test_mission_status_artifacts.py tests/contract/test_mission_status_examples.py -m "corpus and not windows_ci" -q
# the CI pins and the router
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ci/test_contracts_routing.py tests/ci/test_contracts_workflows.py tests/ci/test_fork_guard.py tests/ci/test_workflow_script_import_guard.py tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py tests/ci/test_mission_status_routing.py tests/ci/test_mission_status_corpus_compare.py tests/ci/test_mission_status_gate_checks.py -q
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/architectural/test_ci_corpus_trigger_completeness.py tests/architectural/test_no_duplicate_suite_execution.py tests/architectural/test_workflow_coherence.py tests/architectural/test_module_shard_registry.py -q
.venv/bin/python scripts/ci/local_gate_parity.py --base-ref origin/main      # what a diff selects
# the Contracts workflow's python-checks scripts at their defaults (smoke; this diff edits tests/contract/**)
.venv/bin/python contracts/tools/layout_check.py && .venv/bin/python contracts/tools/leak_scan.py && .venv/bin/python contracts/tools/structure_check.py
# the Mission-state audit (before pushing any M/** change)
.venv/bin/spec-kitty doctor mission-state --audit --fail-on teamspace-blocker --json > "<scratch>/audit.json"; echo "exit $?"
# baseline for ordinary changes (CLAUDE.md "Test policy"); heavy suites stay excluded
make test-fast

# Java (JDK 25 on PATH; a UTF-8 file-name locale is a precondition of Gate G: export LC_ALL=C.UTF-8, or any installed UTF-8 locale, first; <scratch> is a directory OUTSIDE the repository, bundle.py refuses one inside)
.venv/bin/python contracts/tools/bundle.py --root contracts --out "<scratch>/bundle-out" --module mission-status --bundle-only
export CONTRACT_BUNDLE="<scratch>/bundle-out/bundle/mission-status/openapi.yaml"
export CONTRACT_BUNDLE_SHA256="$(sha256sum "$CONTRACT_BUNDLE" | cut -d' ' -f1)"
cd services/mission-status-api
./gradlew --no-daemon test --tests 'org.speckitty.missionstatus.<package>.<Class>'     # targeted, red-first loop (UTF-8 locale, see Gate G preconditions)
./gradlew --no-daemon test jacocoTestCoverageVerification jacocoScopeCheck             # PHASE EXIT GATE of every Java phase (not `check`)
./gradlew --no-daemon kitTest --tests '*ContractDriftNestedBuildTest' --tests '*VerificationTamperTest' --tests '*SkipReportNestedBuildTest' --tests '*PlantedClassCoverageNestedBuildTest'   # P2 only
.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py -q                  # Gate P: the exit gate of P2 and of every phase that names Gate G (P1 to P13 but P3a); what the pin reads and who writes it is the readiness row "Everything the pin reads"

./gradlew --no-daemon nativeTagJvmTest                                                  # P13 only
# E-7 (the operator's word is given, 2026-10-13): the P3 base-commit figure and its comparison; AC-20.5 itself is asserted by CI's perfTest against the same-run floor, D-127: the plain `test` task excludes the tag `perf`, so this uses perfTest
./gradlew --no-daemon perfTest --tests 'org.speckitty.missionstatus.perf.Nfr009CacheGrowthTest'
# C-005 pre-flight, first step of every phase (D-107): the shape facts this plan relies on and the old-to-new class names of section 1.1 (red on a tree that is not the re-cut layout, by design), then the #5529 tip it ran against
./gradlew --no-daemon test --tests 'org.speckitty.missionstatus.architecture.CoupledSymbolsShapeTest'
git log -1 --format=%H <the ref that carries #5529: today refs/remotes/m5529/lane-b = 4dc35c7d5d771cca96f83ef5789f115ca8209b61>   # record this in the phase's first commit message
# C-005 string-literal sites (section 1.1): a class name held in a string is invisible to the compiler during a move; every hit must name a class of the right-hand column of the mapping table, or be one of the two JaCoCo wildcards

grep -rnE 'application/port|domain/(overview|reducer|projection|paging|cursor)' tests scripts .github      # the pin's flat probe paths (section 1.1, step 3a): three hits in the tuple at the checkout, empty from the end of P1

# CI is the authority; listed so that nobody runs them as a phase gate. Under the E-7 exception (D-151) check, nativeCompile nativeTest and nativeSmoke may also be run locally before review (P6, P8, P13); nativePerf, native-tag-floor and digests stay CI only:

cd services/mission-status-api && ./gradlew --no-daemon bootJar && cd ../..
LC_ALL=C.UTF-8 java -jar services/mission-status-api/build/libs/mission-status-api.jar --mission-status.project-root="<repo>" --mission-status.port=8787 > "<scratch>/service.log" 2>&1 &
```

The phase-specific subset of the above is the **targeted test surface** each WP prompt must declare (charter, Testing Requirements); section 11 names it per phase. The commands, counts and the recorded elapsed time and compared totals of the nightly run go in the PR's "Tests run" section.


## 6. Baseline: pre-existing red versus introduced red (FR-021, AC-21.1 to AC-21.3)

**Known pre-existing red: #5927** (`tests/contract/test_mission_status_reality.py` is red on `main`: 240-second timeouts, `KeyError: 'findings'`, a drift read answering 500). It is an **open GitHub issue** (`gh issue view 5927` on 2026-10-13: `OPEN`). **#3284 is closed (2026-09-08) and is not a live baseline issue; no row cites it.** The charter's Pre-existing Failure Reporting Rule binds: a pre-existing failure found later is reported to the orchestrator, who files the issue with the operator's OK; until the issue exists the failure is unclassified, is not accepted as baseline, blocks the claim that depends on it, and is never attributed to this mission nor hidden.

**Procedure, run once as the P0 precondition, executed by the orchestrator before any Work Package is claimed and outside the Work Package list (C-011, FR-021, D-114; the tasks stage starts at P1)** (the orchestrator runs the capture with a Sonnet implementer writing the test; no scratch checkout moves the HEAD of a working checkout: use `git worktree add <scratch>/base-a <sha>` and `<scratch>/base-b <sha>`):

1. **Two bases.** A = the first parent of merge commit `58cd71ec9` (current `main`), recorded as the **literal 40-hex sha** `d082b18106d284666581bfa3ad171fb58fe06625` (verified an ancestor of `origin/main`), never as "first parent of 58cd71ec9": that merge commit is a commit of this branch and does not survive compaction. B = the design-base tip at capture, not earlier than merge `fd866831a` (today HEAD `62a5caec4` carries it). B is a commit of this unpushed branch, which charter compaction before each draft PR and the later retarget onto `main` both rewrite, so the record stores for B its sha, its tree hash (`git rev-parse <B>^{tree}`), `ephemeral: true` and a description (the #5529 tip it carried, today `4dc35c7d5d771cca96f83ef5789f115ca8209b61`). The rule that keeps AC-21.1 green and non-vacuous across the rewrite is below.
2. **Targeted modules** (the module list committed in the test: every `tests/contract/test_mission_status_*.py` and `tests/ci/test_mission_status_*.py` present at the capture sha, plus the Gradle `test` task of the service). **Existence is decided per base, before any run, by `git cat-file -e <base>:<path>`** (running `pytest` on a missing path errors, which would be counted as a red or the module silently dropped: the vacuity the record exists to prevent). Verified against base A (`d082b18106d284666581bfa3ad171fb58fe06625`): **absent on A** are the Gradle module (the service tree does not exist), the four `tests/ci` modules (`test_mission_status_api_build.py`, `test_mission_status_corpus_compare.py`, `test_mission_status_gate_checks.py`, `test_mission_status_routing.py`) and `tests/contract/test_mission_status_conformance.py`; each is recorded `absent_on_base` with `executed: 0`, never as green. Also absent on A, and recorded in a `files_absent_on_base` list without a run: the generator `tests/contract/_mission_status_conformance.py`, `scripts/ci/mission_status_corpus_compare.py` and `scripts/ci/mission_status_gate_checks.py`. **Present on A**: `test_mission_status_artifacts.py`, `_contract_1_1.py`, `_detail.py`, `_drift.py`, `_examples.py`, `_ops.py`, `_payloads.py`, `_project.py` and `_reality.py` (the last under item 3). Record-test controls beyond section 6's list: a module present on B and absent on A recorded without `absent_on_base`, and a module present on both bases recorded `absent_on_base`, each fail. Per module and base: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest <module> -q -p no:cacheprovider -rf` with the corpus selector for corpus-marked modules, and for Java `./gradlew --no-daemon test` (needs the bundle variables of section 5). `executed` and `failed` are taken from the runner's own summary of that run.
3. **`test_mission_status_reality.py` and `test_mission_status_payloads.py`.** The reality module is recorded `not run: known timeout`, issue #5927, `attribution: unclassified`, **zero rows** (two runs would cost two ten-minute caps, #5838). It is not executed on either base (obligation FRESH3-005). The payloads module is not run by `tests (contract tools)`; it runs in `tests (corpus-blocking)`; it is captured like any other module.
4. **Rows.** Each red test: test id, base sha, normalised signature (exception class plus first message line with digits, absolute paths, ULIDs and hexadecimal runs replaced by placeholders, AC-21.2), and the open `issue`. `redCount` per base = sum of `failed` and number of rows; `issueState: OPEN` and the date checked are recorded per issue (`gh issue view`). `executed` has a floor committed in the test beside the record.
5. **NFR-009 base measurement (owned by P3, not by P0; D-112).** It needs a Java class and a corpus shape that no existing harness provides: `perf/MissionCorpus#ledger` builds 1 to 12 Work Packages with 1 to 5 transitions each (plus 120 annotation rows on every seventh Mission), and no heap-after-collection class exists in `perf/`. P3's **first commit** therefore adds `T/perf/Nfr009CacheGrowthTest.java` (tag `perf`) and a corpus option in `MissionCorpus` that generates the specified shape (500 Missions, 10 Work Packages each, 100 transition rows each: ten rows per Work Package, namely `planned` to `claimed`, `claimed` to `in_progress`, three rework rounds of `in_progress` to `for_review` to `in_progress`, then `in_progress` to `for_review` to `approved`), pinned by a unit assertion on the generated counts, and no production line. The base figure is taken once at that commit in a scratch worktree (`git worktree add <scratch>/nfr009-base <sha of that commit>`; the working checkout does not move) with `cd S && ./gradlew --no-daemon perfTest --tests 'org.speckitty.missionstatus.perf.Nfr009CacheGrowthTest'` (the plain `test` task excludes the tag `perf`; `perfTest` includes only it: `S/build.gradle.kts`). The figure, the command, the date and the sha are written to `M/traces/nfr009-base.md` as a P3 output, and the base-commit comparison of P3's tip with it is made by the same class and the same shape. **The class also measures its own same-run floor (D-127, PLAN-FRESH2-007):** in one run it caches the same corpus twice, once through the real reducer (with the FR-020 resolved state and history) and once as history-less entries built through the `StatusSnapshot` four-argument constructor that P3 keeps (the pre-FR-020 projection), and asserts the growth between the two retained sizes against the 64 MB ceiling of NFR-009, so **CI's `perfTest` asserts AC-20.5 without a recorded base** and the criterion is never silently replaced by the absolute ceiling alone; if the history-less control cannot be built through public API, P3 records the alternative (a test-tree hook in package `application.query`, never a production change) in `design-decisions.md`. The base-commit figure stays as a corroboration (S-24). P1, P1a and P1b (all behaviour-preserving) lie between the design base and that commit and do not change what the cache holds. Running the class locally is one named measurement that the operator's E-7 ruling (2026-10-13) allows; the base-commit figure and the local run are therefore taken at P3. It is a measurement, not a retry.
6. **CI-level reds on the PR.** `M/**` edits select the at-budget `tests (corpus-blocking)` job (#5838), so a red or cancelled check is likely before any assertion fails. For every red PR check: read the real conclusion (`gh run view`, `gh pr checks` reports `cancelled` as fail and a zero-second "failure" is usually a cancelled job); remember PR CI tests the head merged with current `main` and one red shard cancels the rest; compare the same job on the latest `main` run; cite the existing issue instead of opening a duplicate; one diagnosed rerun, never retry-to-green; record run ids and conclusions in the PR body.

**Record shape and the reachability rule (D-89, S-12).** The test calls a pure function `base_violations(record, objects)` where `objects` is an injected resolver (`exists(sha)`, `tree_of(sha)`); the real run injects `git cat-file -e` and `git rev-parse <sha>^{tree}`, the controls inject fakes. Rules: (1) both shas and B's tree hash are 40-hex and B differs from A; (2) A must exist (`git cat-file -e A^{commit}`; the CI checkout has full history and A is on `main`); (3) if B exists its tree must equal the recorded tree hash (a different tree is a violation: someone edited the record or recorded another commit); (4) if B does not exist the outcome is the typed `unreachable_after_rewrite`, accepted only when the record says `ephemeral: true`; (5) the module list of the record equals a module list committed in the test beside the executed floors, and the test fails when a listed module is missing from the live glob (a superset is allowed: this mission adds modules). Non-vacuity controls (each fails): a copy with a malformed sha, a copy with B's tree hash changed while B is present, a copy with `ephemeral` removed while B is absent, a copy with A absent, a copy with a module removed from the record, and a copy with a row removed. The pure function is the unit under test, so the controls do not depend on what the CI checkout happens to contain.

**How a failure is told apart (FR-021, Baseline and verification honesty):** a failure is this mission's if its test id is not in the recorded set of a module that was run, or if it is in the set with a different signature. **A red in a module recorded `not run` has no signature: it is unclassified, reported to the orchestrator under AC-21.3, attributed neither to this mission nor to the baseline, and it never gates this mission's per-PR checks** (those are the golden suites, the Java build and the fence tests; the nightly corpus comparison is the full-corpus check). A red that is not this mission's is reported against `baseline-red.yaml` in the PR and accepted as baseline only when it carries an open issue. Category-1 reds (known P0) are never "fixed" here; categories 2 to 4 (CI-environment, stale install, stale venv) are re-checked with `uv sync --frozen --all-extras` before being called pre-existing.

**Ordering is a review item, not an AC** (D-44), and it is checked **before** the history compaction: the review brief of the first Work Package verifies `git merge-base --is-ancestor <commit adding baseline-red.yaml> <first WP commit>`; compaction rewrites both commits, so the check cannot be repeated afterwards and the record test does not depend on it. The record test keeps reading the file after merge, when `M/` is frozen, and after the compaction and the retarget (rule 4 above). Closeout: after the compaction, re-run the record tests (`PWHEADLESS=1 .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/contract/test_mission_status_conformance.py -k baseline_record -m "corpus and not windows_ci" -q`) and the generator `--check` on the compacted tip (section 11, "Compact the history").

No test suite was run while authoring this plan (`java` is absent on the planning host); the read-only checks that were run are `spec-kitty regen --check`, the token-free Sonar helper, `gh pr list`/`gh pr view`/`gh issue view`, and `git` queries.


## 7. Reusable primitives for #5533

#5533 (`rehome-dashboard-read-surface-01M4S6D0`, designed in parallel) builds its governance routes on the primitives of this mission and adds **no artifact route**. Its Java work packages need the primitives before any route of this mission matters: the seams below are delivered by phases **P5** (rows 1 to 10, and the recorder of row 11), **P7** (the test doubles of row 11) and **P8** (row 12), and "primitives complete" means the **P8 commit** (section 11; D-94). The "Lands in" column of 7.1 is the seam-to-phase map. Section 7.5 lists the seams the operator's ruling A3 adds for #6116 (the live-stream projection, the admission cap, the head projection), which land earlier (P1, P1a) and are not primitives #5533 imports.

**Stability promise, restated for the two-draft-PR shape (E-8, D-152).** The primitives (P5, P7, P8) are all in PR A, which is stacked on #5529's last slice and cannot merge before it (C-005); #5533's Java work stacks on PR A, so "primitives complete" is the PR A tip, and nothing "merges" at P5, P7 or P8 on its own. The promise is therefore bound to commits: **a change to a name in 7.1 after the commit that lands its phase on this branch needs a note in both missions' `traces/design-decisions.md` and the operator's word.** The commits are recorded in this mission's `design-decisions.md` (an append-only entry naming the commit of P5, of P7 and of P8) **after the history compaction**, because compaction rewrites every phase sha; #5533 imports a seam by that recorded commit once this mission's stack is on the #5529 base. No agent pushes, opens or retargets a pull request; publishing any ref of this branch is an operator act, the compaction happens before any such ref exists, and a ref once published is frozen (a later change to a name in 7.1 goes through the decision note and the operator). The split into PR A (P0 to P8) and PR B (P9 to P13) is the operator's ruling E-8 (2026-10-13, D-152): a name in 7.1 is frozen from the PR A commit that lands its phase, and PR B (P9 to P13) is a user of the seams, never an editor of them.

The #5533 identifiers cited in this section were checked against the #5533 specification at its workspace commit `cdce434a3` (branch `issue-5533-rehome-dashboard-read-surface`, `kitty-specs/rehome-dashboard-read-surface-01M4S6D0/spec.md`), which is not on any ref of this repository; a #5533 change to those identifiers needs the note both missions' decision records already require.

### 7.1 The seams (exact names, layers and signatures as designed)

| # | Fully-qualified name | Layer | Contract as designed | Lands in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `application.query.port.out.TextSource` | outbound port | `DirListing list(FileRef dir, int nameLimit, ReadScope scope)`; `EntryStat stat(FileRef path, ReadScope scope)`; `BytesRead readBytes(FileRef file, int cap, ReadScope scope)`; `TextRead readText(FileRef file, int cap, ReadScope scope)`. No `Path`, no `IOException`, no route vocabulary, and **`MissionRef` (inside `FileRef.MissionFile`) is the only Mission-status vocabulary on the port** (its fault type is the port's own `TextFault`, row 6). Implemented only by `adapter.out.filesystem.FilesystemTextSource` (and by the test double `testsupport.InMemoryTextSource`) | P5 |
| 2 | `application.query.port.out.dto.FileRef` | port vocabulary | sealed: `FileRef.MissionFile(MissionRef mission, RelativePath path)` (relative to that Mission's **primary** directory) and `FileRef.ProjectFile(RelativePath path)` (relative to the project root). #5533 uses only `ProjectFile` | P5 |
| 3 | `application.query.port.out.dto.RelativePath` | port vocabulary | record over `List<String> segments`; `RelativePath.of("a/b")` rejects an empty segment, `.`, `..`, a backslash, a NUL and a leading `/`; `child(String)`, `parent()`, `last()` | P5 |
| 4 | `application.query.port.out.dto.ReadScope` | port vocabulary | enum `AUTHORED`, `ARTIFACT`. **#5533 adds exactly one value, `GOVERNANCE`**, and its own `PathClass` values; `TextSource` itself does not change | P5 |
| 5 | result types `application.query.port.out.dto.DirListing`, `EntryStat`, `BytesRead`, `TextRead` | port vocabulary | `DirListing`: `Names(List<String> names, boolean limitReached, boolean trustworthy)` \| `Absent` \| `NotADirectory`. `EntryStat`: `Absent` \| `Present(EntryKind kind, long sizeBytes, long modifiedEpochSecond)` with `EntryKind` FILE, DIRECTORY, SYMLINK, OTHER, a **nested enum of `EntryStat`** (so it is in P5's scope through that file and adds no class to section 13's tree beyond the nested one; an `lstat`: a link is never followed). `BytesRead`: `Bytes(byte[])` \| `Absent` \| `NotRegular` \| `OverCap`. `TextRead`: `Text(String text, long sizeBytes)` \| `Absent` \| `NotRegular` \| `OverCap` \| `NotText`. The cap is enforced **twice, in this order**: first by the size of the **open handle** (no byte is read when it exceeds the cap: AC-5.4 records 0 bytes read for 262145), then by the **bytes actually read** (at most `cap + 1`) as the race guard (AC-7.1: a size 100 at stat and 300000 at read is `OverCap`); never by a size read before the open | P5 |
| 6 | `application.query.port.out.exceptions.TextFault` and `application.query.port.out.dto.TextRefusal` (+ the pure function `TextRefusals.of`) | outbound port | `TextFault` is thrown, never returned (unchecked, no message): `Kind` = `PATH_REFUSED` (a link in any component, or a path that leaves the root), `VANISHED`, `DIR_UNREADABLE` (a listing or stat failed), `FILE_UNREADABLE` (a read failed). It is a **new type, not a new constant of `SourceFault.Kind`**, so no existing exhaustive switch over `SourceFault.Kind` changes (the four sites are `ChangeFeed#metaStageCode`, `ChangeFeed#ledgerStageCode`, `MissionStatusQueryService#ledgerFault` and `MissionStatusQueryService#omitted`; D-91). `TextRefusal` is the closed enum of 7.2 | P5 |
| 7 | `domain.redaction.TextScreen` | domain | `new TextScreen(LeakClasses)`; `Screened screen(String text)` where `Screened` is sealed: `Clean(String text, boolean redacted)` (host paths replaced by `[path]` together with the following non-space characters, addresses by `[email]`; `redacted` true exactly when the text changed) \| `SecretFound` (no text). One function for every route. Uses `LeakClasses.credential(String)` (new, over the same compiled `secrets` the `Projector` uses) | P5 |
| 8 | `domain.redaction.Projector`, `LeakClasses`, `ProjectionError` | domain | unchanged (`strict`, `human`, the leak classes generated into `leak-patterns.json`); the entry point for projected human and strict fields | existing |

(`application.query.artifact.ArtifactLimits` is this mission's artifact constant, lands in P12 and is **not a seam**: #5533 does not import it) | P5 |
