### 7.2 The closed set of refusal kinds #5533 imports

The set is a **type**, not a table: `application.query.port.out.dto.TextRefusal` (enum, the seven rows below) and the pure function `TextRefusals.of(...)` from every outcome that can refuse (`BytesRead`/`TextRead` results, a `TextFault.Kind`, a `TextScreen.Screened.SecretFound`) to one value. `ArtifactQueryService` maps `TextRefusal` to `DetailRefusal` (the application's own route vocabulary): `DetailRefusal` **adds `INVALID_ARTIFACT_PATH` and `SOURCE_UNREADABLE` to that mapping** (both are application decisions, not port kinds; `ARTIFACT_LISTING_UNREADABLE` is the listing form of `IO_FAILURE`). `TextRefusalTableTest` is the reflection test that pins it: the enum's names equal the first column below, the function is total over the inputs listed in the second column, and a planted eighth value turns it red.

| Kind | Originates as | Meaning at the port | Status and code here (the contract's) |
|---|---|---|---|
| NOT_FOUND | `Absent`, **`TextFault.Kind.VANISHED`** (the file or the Mission directory disappeared between the lookup and the read), or an application decision that nothing eligible exists | nothing at the path | 404 `not_found` on content; a vanished file is omitted from a listing (AC-4.7); no diagnostic for a vanished file below the Mission directory |
| NOT_REGULAR | `NotRegular` | a directory or another non-file | 404 `not_found` (artifact eligibility), a governance code for #5533 |
| PATH_REFUSED | `TextFault.Kind.PATH_REFUSED` | a symbolic link in any component, a reparse point, or a path outside the root | 404 `not_found` here with no diagnostic for a link below the Mission directory (row A3, AC-5.3); the diagnostic `path_refused` is written only when the Mission directory itself is a link (L9, rows C3); #5533's G4b gives it its own 4xx code |
| OVER_CAP | `OverCap` | more than `cap` bytes were read, or the open handle was larger | 413 `artifact_too_large` |
| NOT_TEXT | `NotText`, or a NUL byte (the application's check) | invalid strict UTF-8 or a NUL | 415 `artifact_not_text` |
| SECRET_REFUSED | `TextScreen.Screened.SecretFound` | a credential pattern in the decoded text | 422 `artifact_secret`, no content |
| IO_FAILURE | `TextFault.Kind.FILE_UNREADABLE` / `DIR_UNREADABLE` | the operating system refused or failed | 500 `artifact_unreadable` (content) or `artifact_listing_unreadable` (listing) |

Path validity (`ArtifactPaths`, 400 `invalid_artifact_path`) is an artifact rule and is not a port kind.

### 7.3 Decision D-68: over-cap and an I/O failure are DISTINCT kinds (answers #5533's question 1)

**Recommendation and decision: distinct.** Over-cap is a *returned* outcome (`OverCap`), an I/O failure is a *thrown* `TextFault` (D-91: the text-read port has its own fault type; the first draft added a constant to `SourceFault.Kind`, which forced edits to four exhaustive switches that no phase owned). Reasons, against the spec's status table (FR-005, FR-012 rows A4 and A5) and the contract: `ArtifactRefusalCode` has **both** `artifact_too_large` (413) and `artifact_unreadable` (500) and says "a consumer branches on the code"; collapsing them would turn a file the user can shrink into a server fault the user cannot act on. #5533's own table makes the same distinction (G3a 413 class, G4a 500 class), and its SPEC-FRESH3-010 asks for distinct kinds for over-cap, not-text, credential and symlink, which a Mission-status-shaped fault enum would serve badly. The existing `SafeFiles.readRegular` collapses the two into one `unreadable` kind; P5 extends it with `readCapped` (a distinct `OverCap` result, bounded to exactly `cap + 1` bytes) and makes `readRegular` delegate, so no second whole-file reader exists and the behaviour of every existing caller is unchanged. `FilesystemTextSource` translates the `SourceFault` that `SafeFiles` throws into a `TextFault` in one private method (`VANISHED`, `PATH_REFUSED` and `DIR_UNREADABLE` map to their namesakes, `DIR_UNREADABLE` from a file read to `FILE_UNREADABLE`; `META_UNPARSEABLE` and `LEDGER_UNREADABLE` cannot come from these calls and map to `FILE_UNREADABLE`); `SourceFaultKindsTest` pins that `SourceFault.Kind` still has exactly its five values, so a later sixth constant forces a review of the four switches.

### 7.4 Decision D-69: the primitives STAY in the `missionstatus` bounded context (answers #5533's question 2)

**Recommendation and decision: stay, with no shared kernel package now.** The primitives are part of the same Gradle project, root package, JaCoCo scope and ArchUnit rule set as #5529's code, and four of the coupled #5529 symbols (`Allowlist`, `AllowlistAccess`, `SafeFiles`, `ArchitectureRules`) are the very files a move would rewrite while #5529 is unmerged. #5533's FR-017 already plans a sibling context (`org.speckitty.governance`) with an explicit **allow-list of class names** for `governance` to `missionstatus` edges (PD-7 names this option); the table in 7.1 is that allow-list.

| Consequence | Stay (decided) | Extract a shared package now (rejected) |
|---|---|---|
| ArchUnit | No rule changes for this decision, **provided the governance context takes the same side-first tree (PLAN-FRESH4-003, D-142)**. Rules are rooted at `ArchitectureRules.ROOT` and built from the root plus a fixed suffix (rules 3 and 4: `application.query.port.in..` and `application.query.port.out..`; rule 13: `application.query.port..`; rule 14: `application.query..`; the others `domain..`, `application..`, `adapter.in.http..`, `adapter.out.filesystem..`, `config..`), so "run the existing rules once per root" (#5533's FR-017) holds only if a governance root is laid out as `org.speckitty.governance.application.query` (with `port.in` and `port.out`, each with `dto/` and `exceptions/`), `domain.readmodel` and `domain.redaction`: the layout the operator's CQRS ruling (D-131) fixes for the service, not for one context. A governance `adapter.in.http` that depended on a flat `governance.application.port.in` would turn rule 3 red, and rules 13 and 14 would scan nothing in that root (the non-vacuity count per root catches it). #5533 runs the existing rules once per root, adds the cross-context rules and raises `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` itself. The alternative, making the rule suffixes parameters of `ArchitectureRules.all`, is not taken: it rewrites the builders P1 and P1b write, and is #5533's escalation to the operator if it wants flat names for governance. This mission's rule 15 subject is `adapter.in.http.detail` and rule 16's subject the new inbound interfaces: neither pre-empts #5533 | Every rule's package strings and the `landscape` of rules 3, 4, 12 (`root + ".."`) are rewritten; `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` and the non-vacuity counts change in unmerged #5529 test code |
| JaCoCo class floors | Patterns `...missionstatus.domain.*` and `...application.*` and their two counts are unchanged; the primitives are counted in them (P5 raises the counts). #5533 adds its own patterns and floor lines | Two patterns re-pointed, `jacocoScopeCheck` keys and `jacoco-class-floor.properties` recounted, in a file #5529 owns |
| Native hints | None for classes (Spring AOT); resource patterns unchanged | A package move changes no hint but forces a native re-validation on four legs |
| Stacked PR | The diff is additive: new classes plus small edits to `Allowlist`, `SafeFiles`, `AllowlistAccess`. #5533 imports the seam commits of 7.1, not a move | A move of coupled #5529 classes inside a stack that has not merged: the highest conflict risk of the whole stack |
| Cost of changing our mind | Cheap: the allow-list already names the classes, so a later extraction is a rename | Hard to reverse |

**Reconsider when** a consumer outside this Gradle project exists or a third bounded context needs the primitives. **Coordination (section 14, E-3):** #5533's plan author should confirm PD-7 as "stay and import on the allow-list of 7.1". **#5533's Java work stacks on PR A (operator ruling E-8, D-152)**, which carries every primitive (P5, P7, P8); the PR A base is #5529's last slice, so #5533 imports the seams of 7.1 from the PR A tip, recorded after compaction. What this plan commits to is the commit-bound stability promise above. **The strings of #5533's text that this re-cut supersedes (PLAN-FRESH4-003):** its FR-017 names `application.port.in` and `application.port.out`; its PD-7 names `SourceFault` in `application.port.out` and `LeakClasses` and `Projector` in `domain.projection`. After the re-cut they are `application.query.port.in`, `application.query.port.out`, `application.query.port.out.exceptions.SourceFault` and `domain.redaction.{LeakClasses,Projector}` (the mapping table of section 1.1, rows 4, 7, 9 and 11), and the allow-list of 7.1 is stated in those names. This plan edits only this mission's directory, so the matching decision note in #5533's own `traces/design-decisions.md` is an orchestrator item (D-142, E-3).

### 7.5 Seams the operator's ruling A3 adds (for #6116, not for #5533)

The ruling (section 15) makes three more things reusable by a second inbound adapter. They are named here so the stability promise above binds them too; they land in P1 and P1a, i.e. **before** P5, and a change to a name after the commit that lands its phase needs the same note in both missions' decision records and the operator's word. They are **not** primitives #5533 imports (its governance routes read no live stream and no Mission head), so "primitives complete" for #5533 stays the P8 commit.

| # | Fully-qualified name | Layer | Contract as designed | Lands in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | `application.query.port.in.dto.StreamEvent` (+ `StreamSignal.Events`, `StreamBatch`) | inbound port | sealed `StatusTransition`, `Lifecycle`, `LogTruncated`; projected values only (strings, a boolean, `StreamCursor`); no raw ledger row and no member named `reason`, `reviewRef`, `reasonSource` or `claimAgent` (the truncation's wire word `reason` is the component `truncationCause`, which `EventData` writes under the key `reason`: PLAN-FRESH3-003) | P1a |
| 14 | `application.query.port.in.dto.{StreamRequest, StreamScope#parse, StreamCursorText}`, `application.query.port.in.exceptions.{StreamRefused (+ fixed detail), StreamCapacityReached}`, `application.query.port.in.MissionStatusQuery#subscribe` and `#endStreams` | inbound port | decode scope and cursor text once, in the legacy order; refuse on capacity in the subscribe path (one cap, one shutdown registry for every adapter) | P1a |
| 15 | `application.query.MissionReadModel#find` and `#head` (over `OverviewReader#head` and `MissionHeadProjection`) | application (package visibility: the route services live in the same package) | **an application-internal seam, not an adapter seam (PLAN-FRESH3-008):** the only way for an implementation of an inbound port to obtain a projected Mission head together with the reduction it came from, in one pass; an adapter reaches a head only through the inbound ports, and rule 3 bars it from naming this class. The stability promise binds the application-side names only | P1 |

The transport-agnostic claim of section 1 ("an MCP adapter parses the two identifiers with the same factories and passes typed values") is therefore true of the stream's type references as well (the HTTP status inside the stream refusal is the stated limit of section 1, E-11), and `PlainStreamCallerTest` (P1a) and `PlainCaller` (P8) are the two proofs.

## 8. Plan obligations resolved

Each of the seven obligations of the spec section "Plan obligations (carried from spec review round 3)" is resolved below: the decision, the exact spec text it changes or pins with the wording the amendment must carry (the amendments are collected in section 14 and applied by the orchestrator in one spec-fold commit before the tasks stage; this plan never edits `spec.md`), the test that fails when the decision is reverted, and the owning phase. The plan squad verifies each.

### FRESH3-001. Strict UTF-8 decode placement under rules 1b and 2b

**Decision (D-70).** Typed port result with the strict decode in `adapter.out.filesystem.codec` (class `StrictUtf8`, a `CharsetDecoder` with `CodingErrorAction.REPORT` on both malformed input and unmappable characters). Not a hand-rolled validator in `domain`, and not a round-trip trick. **Why:** rules 1b and 2b allow `java.nio.charset..` but not `java.nio` (`ByteBuffer`), and `CharsetDecoder.decode` needs a `ByteBuffer`, so neither the domain nor the application can decode strictly with the JDK primitive; a hand-written validator would duplicate a JDK primitive and add a second implementation to keep in parity with Python's strict UTF-8, and `new String(bytes, UTF_8)` followed by a re-encode comparison is a correct-by-accident check that doubles the allocation NFR-002 bounds. The codec package is the right home: rule 12 lets it use any JDK class except a file API and depend only on `domain` (rule 12a), rule 12c lets `adapter.out.filesystem` and `testsupport` use it. Every consumer of decoded text goes through it: `FilesystemTextSource.readText` (artifact content, `tasks.md`), `WpFrontmatterCodec` and `ReviewCycleCodec` (their bytes arrive through `readBytes`). The application never sees bytes of a text read; the NUL decision (415) stays an application decision over the decoded `String`. `StrictUtf8` returns its own nested sealed result (`StrictUtf8.Decoded`: `Text(String)` or `Rejected`), a type of the codec package that depends on nothing outside the JDK; `FilesystemTextSource` maps it to the port's `TextRead` (the codec may not name `TextRead.NotText`, rule 12a).

**Spec text and wording.**
- FR-007: add after the third bullet: "The port also offers a decoded read, `readText`: the bytes of the file, read under the caller's cap, are decoded as strict UTF-8 in `adapter.out.filesystem.codec` (a malformed, overlong, surrogate or truncated sequence, or a code point above U+10FFFF, is never replaced and never repaired; a leading byte order mark stays in the text) and returned as a typed result: `Text(text, sizeBytes)`, `Absent`, `NotRegular`, `OverCap` or `NotText`. `readBytes` returns the raw bytes under the same cap for callers whose decoder lives behind its own port."
- FR-005 order of decision, step (5): replace "bytes that are not strict UTF-8" by "the port's `NotText` result, or a NUL byte in the decoded text".
- AC-7.3: append: "The strict UTF-8 decode of every text read is done by one class in `adapter.out.filesystem.codec` and returned through the port as a typed result; a unit control feeds it an overlong sequence (C0 AF), an encoded surrogate (ED A0 80), a code point above U+10FFFF (F4 90 80 80), a truncated sequence (E2 82) and a lone lead byte (C3), requires `NotText` for each, and requires a valid four-byte character, a byte order mark and an empty input to decode. A planted application class that wraps bytes in a `ByteBuffer` turns rule 2b red."

**Reconciliation with the existing strict decoders (D-116).** Three inline strict UTF-8 decodes already exist in #5529 (`LedgerLineDecoder#strictUtf8`, `ReadDirResolverJson#object`, `FilesystemMissionEventSourceProject#utf8`; `FilesystemMissionEventSourceMeta` calls `ReadDirResolverJson#object` and has none of its own: checked by grepping `newDecoder`), so `StrictUtf8` is a fourth copy of the same four-line JDK idiom, not a new rule. They are **left alone** (section 9 row): consolidating means editing three coupled #5529 files for no behaviour this mission needs (Locality of Change, DIRECTIVE_024), one of them (`LedgerLineDecoder`) in P3's scope, in a stack whose base is unmerged (D-69's argument). The single-owner rule is stated for **new** reads: every text read or Work Package file decode this mission adds goes through `StrictUtf8`, and `ArchitectureControlsTest` gains no rule for it. The three #5529 sites are a consolidation candidate for #5529's owner once it has merged (they can delegate unchanged in behaviour); it is recorded in `traces/approach.md` and is not a gate allowlist, so no priced-debt entry applies.

**Test and revert.** `StrictUtf8Test` (the control above) and AC-5.5 / AC-3.4 / AC-6.1 cases 24 to 26 through the port: revert `REPORT` to `REPLACE` (or move the decode into a lenient `new String`) and `StrictUtf8Test`, the AC-5.5 fixture and `wpm-24..26` go red; `ArchitectureControlsTest` gains the planted `A2cByteBuffer` fixture class. **Owner: P5** (`StrictUtf8`, `readText`), used by P6.

### FRESH3-002. AC-1.8(c): the true meta.json read count

**Decision (D-71, call sequence fixed by D-104).** **Reword AC-1.8(c) to the true count; do not make single-read a campsite change.** Verified in the code (checkout name `MissionStatusService`, `MissionStatusQueryService` after the re-cut): `MissionStatusService.resolve` reads each listed Mission's `meta.json` once up to the match and then calls `probed`, whose `source.stampLedger` resolves the read directory (`FilesystemMissionEventSource.ledgerPath` calls `ReadDirResolver.resolve`, which reads `meta.json`): the stream routes therefore read the matched Mission twice. The head derivation `overview(ref)` reads it again (`readMeta`), then `stampLedger` and, on a cache miss, `readLedger` each resolve the read directory again.

**The call sequence of the three ledger routes is fixed:** `MissionLookup.find(id)` (the probe-free lookup), the duplicate continuation (it reads only the Missions that sort after the match), then `OverviewReader.head(ref)`; nothing else. **`head(ref)` is the one accessor that gives the detail services both the projected head and the reduction it came from (PLAN-FRESH2-003, D-123):** it returns `MissionHead(overview, entry)` from a single pass (`readMeta`, the ledger stamp, on a miss the ledger read), the existing `overview(ref)` delegates to it, and the per-Work-Package snapshot (`StatusSnapshot` resolved map, `lifecycleTypes`, lane per Work Package) is read from `MissionHead#entry`; no detail service ever calls `OverviewReader#ledger(ref, mid8)`, which would add a second stamp and so a fourth `meta.json` read (4 warm, 5 cold). **No route calls `probed()`, `stampLedger` or `readLedger` itself.** The "ledger probe" of FR-001 and D-58 *is* the `stampLedger` inside `OverviewReader`'s ledger step, whose faults are already mapped by rows M1 and M2 (a note and an empty snapshot, a 200, never the 500 that `probed` throws). So the matched Mission is read **3 times on a warm cache and 4 on a cold one** on the three ledger routes (the lookup, the head's `readMeta`, the ledger stamp and, on a miss, the ledger read), and **once** on the two artifact routes (lookup only, no head). A route that added its own probe step before the head derivation would read it 4 and 5 times and the pinned count would fail by one. Every non-matching Mission is read exactly once per request (before the match by the lookup, after it by the duplicate scan). Removing the extra reads needs `MissionEventSource` to accept an already-resolved handle: a change to a coupled #5529 port (C-005) to save three reads of a file capped at 1 MiB and a few hundred bytes in practice. NFR-001's figures already include the scan, and the AC pins the counts so growth turns red.

**Spec text.** AC-1.8(c) becomes: "(c) on the 500-Mission fixture of NFR-001, with the requested Mission sorted first (the worst case: every later `meta.json` is read for the duplicate scan), the p95 figures of NFR-001 hold, and the instrumented port records per request exactly one read of the `meta.json` of every Mission other than the requested one, and for the requested Mission: on `getMission`, `getWorkPackage` and `getWorkPackageDetail` three reads on a warm ledger cache and four on a cold one (the lookup, the head derivation, the ledger stamp and, on a miss, the ledger read); on `listArtifacts` and `getArtifactContent` exactly one. The ledger probe of FR-001 is the stamp inside the head derivation, not an extra call. Fails if any other Mission is read twice, if the requested Mission's count rises, or if the figures are missed with the scan included. Controls: a request for the last Mission in name order records the same totals; a stream request (`resolve`, which does probe) records two reads of the matched Mission before the feed starts, so that the difference between the two paths is visible." The sentence of the spec's Risks table / D-58 that says "exactly one read of each Mission's `meta.json`" is not repeated elsewhere.

**Test and revert.** Four per-route classes over one helper, so that no test class is written by several phases (D-113): `MissionDetailMetaReadCountTest` (P9, including the stream-route control), `WorkPackageMetaReadCountTest` (P10), `WorkPackageDetailMetaReadCountTest` (P11) and `ArtifactMetaReadCountTest` (P12, count 1); each uses the `MetaReadCounter` helper (instrumented port counts `MISSION_META` READ per Mission, per route, warm and cold, first-sorted and last-sorted requested Mission) and asserts a discovered-row floor so an empty class fails. Revert: make `OverviewReader` re-read the meta once more, add a `probed()` call, or make the duplicate scan restart from the beginning, and the equality fails. The helper `MetaReadCounter` is a `testsupport` class of P7. **Owner: P9** (`getMission`), **P10** (`getWorkPackage`) and **P11** (`getWorkPackageDetail`), each with the class of its own route in its exit gate **on the real service** (`MissionDetailQueryService` over `MissionReadModel`, not only on the extracted `/missions` path), and **P12** (artifact routes, count 1). P1 holds the matching control on the extracted path (`OverviewReaderTest`/`MissionReadModelTest`: `head` records what `overview` recorded, 2 warm and 3 cold; `find` then `head` records 3 warm and 4 cold), so a regression of the accessor is red one phase before any route exists.

### FRESH3-003. Rows M7 and V18 against the nullable rulings of #5529

**Decision (D-72).** Derive the table from the contract schemas and the oracle's `Projector` use; **restrict M7 and V18 to the non-nullable fields**; add row **M14** (a nullable member with an unrepresentable value is served as `null`, 200, **no diagnostic**, as #5529's ruling 1 and its `lastEventId` do) with a fixture class; add a `knownDivergence` entry where the oracle serves the value unprojected. A fact that shapes the table: `OverviewDeriver.lastActivityAt` already refuses the whole head (M3, `ledger_unparseable`) when any Work Package's `lastTransitionAt` is not an instant, so that member can never reach a per-member null; a junk date is an M3 case, not an M7 or M14 case.

Per new-route member (schemas under `contracts/mission-status/schemas/`; oracle = `Projector` in `tests/contract/_mission_status_payloads.py`, `_mission_status_detail.py`). **Row ids T1 to T18 are part of the amended FR-008 table** (they key the `@ProjectionRow` cases of AC-8.4):

**Spec text and wording.** FR-008: insert the table above (all rows) after the three field classes. Row M7: "a strict **non-nullable** field cannot be shown, or a human field of FR-008 class 3 holds a credential or a leak that substitution cannot remove, or `history[].at` is not an instant | 500 `projection_refused` (`source_unreadable` on detail) | `projection_refused`". V18: "a non-nullable strict field, a class-3 human field or a history stamp that cannot be shown (M7) | injected (Java-only) | stored text served, or `ProjectionError` | 500 `projection_refused`". New V19: "a nullable head date that is not an instant (`acceptedAt`, `mergedAt`, `discardedAt`, #5529 ruling 1) | `projection-nulls`, fixture `head-dates-nulled` | stored text served, and the lifecycle status derived from it | `null`, and the lifecycle status derived without it". FR-014 table: add class `projection-nulls` (Count 6: `head-dates-nulled`, `merge-target-branch-path`, `planning-branch-too-long`, `override-partial-junk-at`, `lane-id-fails-pattern`, `reviewed-at-junk`; golden files: getMission, getWorkPackage, getWorkPackageDetail as each fixture needs; `head-dates-nulled` is a `knownDivergence` fixture) and the Java-only class `projection-refusals` (Count 4: `history-at-not-instant`, `history-event-id-leak`, `owned-file-pattern-leak`, `dependency-id-leak`; no golden; V18). AC-14.4's first clause reads "each row V1 to V19" and its row-to-entry map gains M7 to V18 and M14 to V19 (S-3). New AC-8.4 (the first draft demanded a fixture per row, which the ten named fixtures cannot cover and one row, T11, cannot even reach): "every (row, route) pair of the member-to-route list of `data-model.md` (a row on each route whose mapper carries one of its members) has a mapper-level case, annotated with its row id and its route; the discovered case count equals the number of pairs, 26, which cover all 18 rows T1 to T18, and a short discovery fails; in addition each row whose outcome is 200 with a `null` and that names a fixture has a route fixture of class `projection-nulls`, asserting the member is `null`, the status is 200 and zero diagnostics are written, and each M7 row that names a fixture has one of class `projection-refusals`, asserting 500 `projection_refused` and one diagnostic; a row marked 'not separately reachable' (T11) is pinned by its mapper case only. Fails if a pair has no case, if a nullable member refuses, or if a non-nullable member is nulled."

**Test and revert.** The `projection-nulls` and `projection-refusals` suites and the `@ProjectionRow(row, route)` cases (the annotation lives in P7's `testsupport`; a case belongs to a (row, route) pair and is written by the phase of that route; `ProjectionRowCoverageTest` in P13 asserts all 26 pairs of the member-to-route list given in this paragraph and, through them, all 18 rows). Revert: make `mergeTargetBranch` non-nullable in the mapper (it then refuses) or null `history[].at`, and the respective fixture goes red; delete the V19 entry and AC-14.4 goes red; make the entry's two outcomes equal and AC-14.4 goes red (stale entry). **Owner: P4** (fixtures, manifest entries V18 and V19), with the route phases owning the fixtures and cases of their members and naming them in their exit gates. **The member-to-route list** (checked against `MissionHead`, `WorkPackageSummary`, `WorkPackage`, `WorkPackageDetail` and `Readiness` in `contracts/mission-status/schemas/`; it is part of `data-model.md`) gives each route's pairs. **P9** (`MissionDetailMapper`, 7 pairs): T1, T2, T3 and T4 for the head and, for the `WorkPackageSummary` the route lists, T5 (`wpId`, `dependencies[]`, `readiness.unsatisfied[]`), T6 (`title`, `phaseLabel`) and T10 (`lastTransitionAt`). **P10** (`WorkPackageMapper`, 10 pairs): T5, T6, T7 (`authored.subtasks[]`, `ownedFiles[]`, `requirementRefs[]`, `trackerRefs[]`, the `subtasks` keys, verified in `WorkPackage.yaml`), T8 (`mergeTargetBranch`), T10, T11, T12, T13, T14 and T17 (`assignment.*`, `implementerOfRecord`, `actor.*`, `latestResult.reviewer`, `override.actor`, `history[].actor`). **P11** (`WorkPackageDetailMapper`, 9 pairs): T1 (`missionId`), T5 (`wpId`, `DependencyEntry.wpId`), T7 (`SubtaskEntry.id`, `OwnedFileEntry.pattern`), T8 (`workspace.planningBranch`), T9, T15, T16, T17 (`reviewCycles[].reviewer`) and T18. 7 + 10 + 9 = 26 pairs over 18 rows. **The rule is per pair, not per row** (PLAN-FRESH4-002, D-141): the first draft gave a row's case to the first route that carries it and listed the cases of T5 and T6 under P10, so the summary members that `getMission` (P9) lists had no case until a later phase, and the actor handles of `getWorkPackage` (T17, P10) had none until P11; a summary that nulled or let through a leaking title passed every gate of P9. A row carried by two routes now has a case on each. The route fixtures follow the same rule: **P9** `head-dates-nulled` (T4) and `dependency-id-leak` (T5, the summary's `dependencies[]`); **P10** `merge-target-branch-path` (T8), `override-partial-junk-at` (T14), `history-at-not-instant` (T13), `history-event-id-leak` (T12), `owned-file-pattern-leak` (T7) and `dependency-id-leak` again; **P11** `planning-branch-too-long` (T8), `lane-id-fails-pattern` (T9), `reviewed-at-junk` (T15), and `owned-file-pattern-leak` (`OwnedFileEntry.pattern`) and `dependency-id-leak` (`DependencyEntry.wpId`) again. T1 to T3, T6, T10, T11, T17 and T18 name no route fixture and are pinned by their mapper cases (T6's M7 needs a leak that substitution cannot remove, which the mapper case plants directly). **P13** closes the count (`ProjectionRowCoverageTest`).

### FRESH3-004. The non-UTF-8 locale refusal of the Work Package index

**Decision (D-73).** Add row **M13** for the three index routes, restricted to **candidate names** (a name that starts with `WP` and ends with `.md`: those are ASCII at both ends, so a mangled middle is detectable and the file is unreachable by its mangled name) rather than every non-ASCII name of `tasks/`. A non-candidate such as `tasks/notes-é.md` is not refused. The probe is the same host check the artifact routes use: `adapter.out.filesystem.NameEncoding` (non-Windows host whose `sun.jnu.encoding` is not UTF-8), surfaced to the application as `DirListing.Names.trustworthy` (false on such a host); the application refuses a candidate with a non-ASCII character (or U+FFFD) when `trustworthy` is false. Under a UTF-8 locale a candidate holding U+FFFD is an ineligible path and is skipped (FR-006 item 2).

**Spec text and wording.** FR-012 table: "M13 | getMission, getWorkPackage, getWorkPackageDetail | a non-UTF-8 `sun.jnu.encoding` on a non-Windows host and a Work Package file candidate in `tasks/` (a name starting `WP` and ending `.md`) whose name is not pure ASCII (D-31) | 500 (the diagnostic code `source_unreadable` on `getMission` and `getWorkPackage`; `source_unreadable` on detail) | `source_unreadable`". V15: "... (A8) **and (M13) on the three index routes** | injected (Java-only, under `LC_ALL=C`) | the oracle lists and reads the name | listing 500 `artifact_listing_unreadable`; content 500 `artifact_unreadable`; index routes 500 `source_unreadable`". New AC-6.6 under FR-006: "(file-name encoding) a fixture generated at test time holds `tasks/WP01-é.md`, `tasks/WP02-x.md` and `tasks/notes-é.md`; under a UTF-8 locale `getMission`, `getWorkPackage` and `getWorkPackageDetail` serve both Work Packages; in a child JVM started with `LC_ALL=C` each of the three answers 500 `source_unreadable` with exactly one path-free `source_unreadable` diagnostic and none omits `WP01` silently, while an ASCII-only fixture is served under both locales and the non-candidate `notes-é.md` does not refuse. A unit leg injects the host probe (`NameEncoding` stub) so the case runs under any locale. Fails if a non-ASCII candidate is omitted, a partial index is served, or a non-candidate refuses." AC-4.8 gains: "The Work Package index uses the same host probe (AC-6.6)." AC-12.1's row list and AC-14.4's map gain M13 to V15. The FR-004 "File names" bullet, which still says the Work Package index answers 500 for any non-ASCII name, is narrowed to the same candidate-only rule (S-4, exact wording there); both ends of a candidate are ASCII, so a mangled middle cannot change whether a name is a candidate.

**Test and revert (D-103).** `WorkPackageIndexLocaleTest` (the stub leg: the `NameEncoding` stub, any locale; **P6**) and one stub-leg case of the same fixture through each route's own service test (**P9, P10, P11**). `WorkPackageIndexLocaleProcessTest` is the three-route leg (a child JVM started with `LC_ALL=C`, each of the three answering 500 `source_unreadable` with one diagnostic): it needs all three routes, so it is owned by **P13**; `config.AppProcess` is package-private in the test package `config` and already passes an environment (`launch(Path, Path, Map<String,String> environment, List, List)`), so the test lives in `T/config/` and uses it as it is (the first draft's fallback branch is dropped). Revert: drop the `trustworthy` check in `WorkPackageIndex` and the stub leg turns red (a silent omission); start the child without `LC_ALL=C` and the process leg turns red. **Owner: P6** (index and stub leg), **P9 to P11** (route stub cases), **P13** (process leg), with `NameEncoding` and `DirListing.Names.trustworthy` from **P5**; the nightly job sets `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` and the native smoke harness sets it for the binary it launches (S-16, D-90).

### FRESH3-005. Baseline item 2 and the not-run reality module

**Decision (D-74).** **Reword; do not require a bounded run.** A red in a module recorded `not run` has no recorded signature, so it is unclassified (reported to the orchestrator, AC-21.3) and attributed neither to this mission nor to the baseline; the per-PR gate is independent of that module. Reason: the reality module is at its ten-minute cap and red (#5838, #5927); a bounded run would capture timeouts whose normalised signature carries no information about a detail-route fault, so it would add cost and false confidence, and the operator's round-2 ruling already allows `not run: known timeout` with the issue.

**Spec text and wording.** "Baseline and verification honesty", item 2 becomes: "2. A failure is this Mission's if its test id is not in the recorded set of a module that was run, or if it is in the set with a different signature. For a module recorded as `not run` (the reality module, #5927) no signature exists: any red in it is unclassified, is reported to the orchestrator under AC-21.3, and is attributed neither to this Mission nor to the baseline; item 3 keeps the per-PR gate independent of it." The sentence "A test in `test_mission_status_reality.py` that was red with the #5927 signature and is now red with a detail-route signature is introduced" is deleted. AC-21.1 / AC-21.3 gain: "A module recorded `not run` carries `attribution: unclassified`, an `issue`, a `reason` and **zero rows**; the test fails on a `not run` module with rows or without `attribution`." (S-12 adds `absent_on_base` for the Gradle module on base A.)

**Test and revert.** `test_mission_status_conformance.py::test_baseline_record_a_not_run_module_has_no_rows_and_is_unclassified` plus the copy-with-a-row-added-to-the-reality-module control; revert by adding a row to it and the test is red. **Owner: P0.**

### FRESH3-006. `worktreePresent`: the `.git` symbolic link

**Decision (D-75).** The `.git` entry must be a regular file or a directory and **not a link**; a link is absent. Python's `ResolvedWorkspace.exists` uses `Path.exists()` (it follows a link), so this is a Java-side strictness recorded as V7 (both halves).

**Spec text and wording.** FR-010, second bullet: "... and `.worktrees/<recorded slug>-<lane id>` is a real directory (not a link) and its `.git` entry exists **as a regular file or a directory, not as a symbolic link or a reparse point** (OQ-3: a husk is absent, as in `ResolvedWorkspace.exists`; a `.git` that is a link is absent; neither entry is followed or read)." The WORKTREE_LANE_GIT row of FR-011 gains "STAT only, no follow". AC-10.3: add the row "directory present, `.git` a symbolic link (false, target unread)"; the `worktree-states` class is **Count 7** (the committed rows: directory and `.git` present, directory without `.git`, directory absent, directory a symbolic link, `.git` a symbolic link, planning lane, no lane); V7: "a `worktreePresent` directory **or its `.git` entry** that is a symbolic link | `worktree-states`, fixtures `directory-symlink` and `git-entry-symlink` | `Path.exists` follows it: true | false, target unread".

**Test and revert.** `WorktreeStatesTest` over the 7 fixtures (the symlinks are provisioned from `PROVISION_PLAN` `link` lines, D-42, never committed) asserting the instrumented port records only STAT with no-follow on the two paths; the manifest check that V7's two outcomes differ. Revert: stat with link-following and `git-entry-symlink` returns true: red. **Owner: P11**, fixtures P4, class `WORKTREE_LANE_GIT` P5.

### FRESH3-007. Does the index record hold the body?

**Decision (D-76).** The index record holds the **validated frontmatter fields and the file name only, never the body**. For `getWorkPackage` with `includePrompt=true` the body comes from **the same read and decode that indexed the winning file**: `WorkPackageIndex.build(mission, Optional<WpId> captureBodyOf)` keeps the body of the first valid file that holds the requested id and of no other file. This **deviates from the spec's preferred wording** (a second read of the winning file) and is chosen on purpose: (a) one read of one file means the title, the dependencies and the prompt of one response cannot come from two versions of a file that an author is rewriting (the class of defect AC-6.3 guards); (b) no second `WP_FILE` READ enters the access set, AC-2.1 or AC-7.4; (c) the first-valid-file-wins rule means the winning file for the requested id is known at the moment it is decoded. The invariant "one capped read buffer at a time" holds: each candidate is read, decoded, reduced to metadata (plus the one captured body) and its buffer dropped.

**Spec text and wording.** FR-006, after item 6: "The index record (`IndexedWorkPackage`) holds the validated frontmatter fields and the file name; it never holds the body. `getWorkPackage` with `includePrompt=true` receives the body from the same read and decode that indexed the winning file: the index builder is given the requested id and keeps the body of the first valid file that holds it, and of no other; there is no second read of the file." AC-2.1: append "The test records exactly one `WP_FILE` READ per candidate file for the request with `includePrompt=true`." NFR-002: "... `getMission` keeps no Work Package body; `getWorkPackage` with `includePrompt=true` holds the read buffer (1048577 bytes), the decoded text (up to 2 MiB when UTF-16), the captured body (up to 2 MiB) and the serialised JSON (up to about 6.3 MB when every character is escaped): about 11 MiB, and passes the admission gate (FR-022 is widened to name this call: S-13)."

**Test and revert.** `WorkPackageIndexTest`: a reflection test that no component of `IndexedWorkPackage` is a body (`String` named `body`/`prompt`, or a `byte[]`); the access-set test (one `WP_FILE` READ per file, none twice); a mutation test that writes a different second version of the winning file between the lookup and the response is unnecessary because there is no second read. Revert: re-read the file for the prompt and the "one READ per file" count is red. **Owner: P6** (builder) and **P10** (route).

## 9. Campsite-clean scope (C-011, charter Standing Order 2)

A distinct, behaviour-preserving FIRST work package (phase P1), in commits of its own with the prefix `refactor(campsite)`, **after** the baseline record (P0) and **before** any functional change. The review checks the commit diff: no `assert`, expected value or golden byte of an existing test changes, and every existing test passes unchanged; new tests for the extracted classes are allowed. It is domain-matched debt on surfaces this mission must edit anyway, nothing else, and it adds no production file beyond the application classes it extracts or introduces (`MissionLookup`, `OverviewReader`, `MissionReadModel`, and, by the operator's ruling A3 item 4, `MissionHeadProjection` and the record `MissionHead`); the test files it adds are the ones named under item 1 (`MissionLookupTest`, `OverviewReaderTest`, `MissionReadModelTest`, `ReadModelSingletonTest`, `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` of D-107, `MissionHeadProjectionTest`) and `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest` with its planted fixture.

**Folded (two campsite items, and the operator's stream seam as a third):**

1. **`MissionStatusQueryService` (433 lines at the checkout, where it is `MissionStatusService`; a god class that owns lookup, head derivation, projection, ledger reduction, diagnostics and the stream entry points).** Extract two package-private classes into `application.query` and add one public composition handle: `MissionLookup` (the scan of `resolve`, split as D-58 says into the probe-free lookup `find(MissionId)` that returns the first readable Mission while remembering the first fault and the optional continuation that detects a second readable directory with the id) and `OverviewReader` (`overview(ref)`, the new `head(ref)`, `ledger(ref, mid8)`, `derive`, `requireInstant`, the omission table) and, one class further, `MissionHeadProjection` (the extracted `projected(MissionOverview, mid8)`, `withProjectedDates(MissionMeta)` and `instantOrNull`: the head projection every detail route must go through, A3 item 4, D-123; `OverviewReader#head(ref)` returns `MissionHead(overview, entry)`, the projected head and the `ProjectionCache.Entry` it came from, in one pass, and `overview(ref)` delegates to it; `MissionHead` is a package-private record, which is why the route services sit in `application.query` itself: section 1.1). `MissionStatusQueryService.resolve` becomes `MissionLookup.find` followed by its own `probed`; `project()`, `missions()` and the stream methods keep their answers and their recorded accesses byte for byte. The package-private accessors `cache()` and `feed()` and the public constructor stay, so `MissionStatusQueryServiceTest`, `FailLoudMappingTest`, `SubscribeConnectTest` and `ProjectionCacheTest` compile and pass unchanged. `OverviewReader` owns the one `ProjectionCache`; this is what lets `getMission` use "the same derivation as the overview item" (AC-1.2) without a second implementation. **The wiring path (D-93).** `MissionLookup` and `OverviewReader` are package-private and the cache is built inside `MissionStatusQueryService`'s public constructor, so `config` (another package) could not hand the same cache to four new detail services. The campsite therefore adds one **public final class `MissionReadModel`** in `application.query`: it holds the `MissionLookup`, the `OverviewReader` and so the one `ProjectionCache`, and exposes the lookup and the head derivation to the detail services (which live in the same package). `ServiceWiring` builds it once, as one bean, and passes it to `MissionStatusQueryService` through a new public constructor; the existing six-argument public constructor stays and builds its own, so every existing test compiles unchanged. **The package-private seven-argument constructor `(MissionEventSource, Clock, Consumer<Diagnostic>, ScheduledExecutorService, StreamSettings, LeakClasses, ProjectionCache)` also stays, with its parameter types** (PLAN-OPERATOR-A3, secondary point): `FilesystemFixtureFactory#fromTreeWithOrdering` reflects it to inject a reducer ordering (AC-8a), and nothing in production calls it. `MissionReadModel` therefore has a public constructor that builds its own cache and a package-private one that takes a given `ProjectionCache`; the seven-argument service constructor delegates to the latter. `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` pins both constructors by reflection and the literal class name the factory loads. The detail services take a `MissionReadModel` in their public constructors. `/missions` and `getMission` therefore share one cache by construction (AC-1.2, NFR-009), and no phase widens a visibility ad hoc. `ServiceWiring` is edited here for the single `MissionReadModel` bean and once more by P1a (the `maxConnections` setting), both before P3 and behaviour-preserving, and never afterwards; `ProjectionCache` is left alone (P3 owns it). Focused tests added: `MissionLookupTest`, `OverviewReaderTest` (the meta-read counts of `/missions`: two reads of the matched Mission warm, three cold, which the later ledger routes build on), `MissionReadModelTest` (two consumers of one instance reduce a ledger once) and `ReadModelSingletonTest` (the Spring context holds exactly one `MissionReadModel` bean and the `MissionStatusQueryService` bean was built from it), and `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` (D-107): it asserts the old-to-new class names of the mapping table (section 1.1) and the shape facts the plan relies on (`ProjectionCache` package-private, the public six-argument and the package-private seven-argument `MissionStatusQueryService` constructors, no production class outside `adapter`, `application`, `config` and `domain`, at least the four methods of `AllowlistAccess` (`openRead`, `stat`, `realPath`, `listNames(Path)`: a presence check, because P5 adds `scoped(ReadScope)` and `listNames(Path, int)`), `SourceFault.Kind` with exactly its five values (the one exact assertion: no phase touches it, D-91), and the counts this mission itself raises as lower bounds at the #5529 values: 15 `PathClass` values, 11 `ENTRIES`, 8 `DiagnosticCode` values, `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` 12; every assertion about something this mission extends is a lower bound or a presence check, never an equality, so the test stays green after P5 and P8: D-117), so a renamed method or a changed visibility in a merged #5529 tip turns it red instead of waiting for a careful reader. `R/jacoco-class-floor.properties` `application` count: the five classes above plus their nested ones, by the command of section 2.
2. **`ArchitectureRules.all(root, rule9Packages)` (one method that builds the twelve rules).** Split into one private builder per rule (`rule1(root)` ... `rule12(root)`) returning `Rule`, `all()` composing them in order, so rules 13 to 16 are added as four more builders rather than four more screens of one method. `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` stays 12 in the campsite commit; `ArchitectureRulesTest` and `ArchitectureControlsTest` pass unchanged.

3. **The stream seam of the operator's ruling A3 (P1a) and its gates (P1b), section 15.** Not a campsite surface of C-011's list, and not required by Standing Order 2 for this mission's routes; placed here because it is the same kind of change (a behaviour-preserving structural change to #5529 code), because the operator ruled it, and because it must precede every functional phase so that rules 15 and 16 and the detail routes are built on the final shape of the port. Classification, the widening of C-011 and C-005 (S-22) and the list of named test edits are in section 15.2; the HTTP stream is preserved byte for byte and pinned by the unchanged stream suites.

**Surveyed and left alone, each with its reason (recorded in `M/traces/approach.md` by P1, as a named append: the tracer files are never `owned_files`). Every production surface and every pin that a phase changes functionally has a row, or is the campsite itself (Standing Order 2; PLAN-FRESH3-010, PLAN-FRESH4-006, D-144); the test classes a phase edits are the closed lists of 15.5 and section 11, and the test files it adds are new:**

| Surface | Reason |
|---|---|
| `FilesystemMissionEventSource` and its `Ledger`/`Meta`/`Project` helpers | Already decomposed into four files; the longest method is short. The new reads live in a new class (`FilesystemTextSource`), not in the event source, so the source is not touched |
| `Allowlist` (199 lines) | The change is additive (ten enum values, eleven entries, a scope input). No method is over 20 lines and the classifier list is data; a table-ising refactor would grow the file set and is not needed to add rows |
| `tests/contract/_mission_status_conformance.py` (781 lines) | Cohesive; the longest function is 32 lines (`materialise_resolver_case`), most are under 20; SonarCloud reports `total: 0` open issues for it and for its test; the mission adds data rows (`CLASS_SPECS`, `PROVISIONING_NOTES`) and per-class helpers, not control flow. Splitting the module would change what the Packs deselect and the router group name |
| `scripts/ci/mission_status_corpus_compare.py` | Edited functionally (AC-14.5); SonarCloud `total: 0`; no function near the complexity ceiling |
| `.github/workflows/mission-status-api.yml` (486 lines) | A YAML file has no Sonar finding class here; the edits are two literals and one environment line |
| `SafeFiles` | Extended (not cleaned): `readCapped` is a functional change (a distinct result and an exact byte bound) and goes in P5 with its tests; `readRegular` delegates to it |
| The strict UTF-8 decodes of #5529 (`LedgerLineDecoder#strictUtf8`, `ReadDirResolverJson#object`, `FilesystemMissionEventSourceProject#utf8`) | Left alone, D-116. `StrictUtf8` (P5) is the fourth copy of a four-line JDK idiom, and consolidating edits three coupled #5529 files (one in P3's scope) for no behaviour this mission needs. Single ownership holds for every read this mission adds; the three older sites are named here and in `traces/approach.md` as a consolidation candidate for #5529's owner after it merges |
| `application.query.stream.ChangeFeed` (513 lines at the checkout; the largest file of the module) | P1a retypes it to the raw feed vocabulary (`FeedSignal`, `FeedSubscription`, the moved `Change` and `ChangeBatch`) and changes no logic. Left alone: its poll, consume, deliver and fault methods work on one `ReentrantLock` and the `watches`, `known` and `subscribers` tables, a cohesion that a split would put across a class boundary in a class the re-cut has just moved, and P1a's change in it is a rename; `ChangeFeedTest` keeps its scenarios |
| `adapter.in.http.EventsController` (257 lines) and `EventData` (137 lines) | **Not left alone: they are the campsite of P1a.** The projection, the counter, the registry and the scope and cursor decoding leave them for the application (section 15), which is the extraction the scout would have chosen; what remains is transport (the pump, the framing, the wire shaping) |
| `domain.readmodel.reducer.StatusReducer` (176 lines) | P3 adds the fold of the resolved state. The existing methods are short single-purpose statics (`fold`, `project`, `shouldApply`, `isRollback`, `uniqueOfSorted`, `sortedUnique`), so no extraction would help P3; the new fold goes into small classes of the same package where a member needs more than a few lines, not into `StatusReducer` |
| `adapter.out.filesystem.codec.RowClassifier` (284 lines) and `LedgerLineDecoder` (144 lines) | P3 adds FR-020 members (a nullable `eventType`, the resolved-state members). `RowClassifier` is a flat table of small private statics (`classify`, `status`, `annotation` and the `valid*` predicates, one per row shape): a new member adds one predicate in the same style, and rule 12a keeps the class on `domain`. An extraction would move the table without reducing what P3 edits. `LedgerLineDecoder` is a thin decoder over it; the one thing a scout would take out, the strict UTF-8 helper, is the D-116 row above. Left alone |
| `adapter.out.filesystem.FilesystemMissionEventSourceProject` (341 lines) | No phase scope names it; left alone with the other `FilesystemMissionEventSource*` helpers (first row) |
| `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` (2737 lines) | Edited by P1 (the probe list and its control), P2 (three clauses) and P13 (the native floors and the stage floors), and run by every phase as Gate P. Sonar analyses it (`sonar.tests=tests`) and the token-free helper reports `total: 0` open issues for it (run for this round). It is a catalogue of independent pins, each a `*_violations` function with its test and the control that turns it red; every planned edit changes a literal or adds one such trio in the same style, so no function grows past its neighbours, and a split would move pins away from the file that the router group and the `ci` shard name. Left alone |
| `services/mission-status-api/build.gradle.kts` (452 lines) | Edited by P2 only: the explicit models list and the skipped-test report hook (one `doLast`). A Kotlin script lies outside `sonar.sources=src`, so there is no Sonar signal; it is already sectioned by concern (dependency verification, JaCoCo, the test source sets, native, the contract bundle) and the edit adds a literal list and one block. Left alone |
| `adapter.in.http.ProblemMapper` (71 lines) and `ProblemAdvice` (70 lines) | P1a edits `ProblemAdvice` (the `UnparseableCursor` handler goes, the `StreamRefused` handler reads the fixed detail, the capacity handler names the moved type) and P8 adds the `DetailRefusal` mapping to both. Each method is a one-expression factory or handler per status, so a new status is a new method in the same style; nothing is near the complexity ceiling. Left alone |
| `domain.redaction.LeakClasses` (224 lines; `domain.projection` at the checkout) and `domain.model.DiagnosticCode` (19 lines) | P5 adds `LeakClasses.credential(String)` over the compiled `secrets` that the `Projector` already uses, and nine `DiagnosticCode` constants. `LeakClasses` is the single owner of the leak classes (charter: single canonical authority; `leak-patterns.json` is generated for it), so splitting it would create a second place that knows them; the enum is data. Left alone |
| `AllowlistAccess` (33 lines) and `AllowlistPassThrough` (48 lines) | P5's edits are additive: two `default` methods on the interface (`scoped`, the bounded `listNames`) and one override of each in the pass-through and in the recorder; nothing existing changes shape (D-92, PLAN-FRESH2-006). Not beyond additive, so no extraction. Left alone |
| `config.ServiceWiring` (91 lines), `MissionStatusProperties` (24) and `StreamSettings` (30; `application/StreamSettings` at the checkout) | Edited by P1 and P1a by one bean, one record component and one setting each, and by each route phase through a wiring class of its own; wiring and data classes with no branching. Left alone |
| `StreamSubscriber` (138 lines) and `Resume` (68), `ProjectionCache` (81, expected unchanged), `StatusSnapshot` (31) and `StatusEvent` (38) | P1a retypes `StreamSubscriber` and `Resume` to the raw feed vocabulary, as it does `ChangeFeed` (the row above); P3 adds parallel members and a nullable component to the two small domain records. Small single-purpose classes whose edits are renames and additions. Left alone |
| `ArchitectureRules` (262 lines), `ArchitectureRulesTest` (87) and `ArchitectureControlsTest` (169) | `ArchitectureRules` is the campsite (item 2 above: one builder per rule, so rules 13 to 16 are added as builders in the same shape); the two test classes gain one control or one skeleton member per rule. Left alone beyond that |
| Java files in general | `services/` is outside `sonar.sources`, so there is **no Sonar signal for Java**; the scout used size and method length only, and says so |

**Observable.** Commit prefix `refactor(campsite)`; `git diff` of those commits shows no changed assertion, expected value or golden byte in an existing test (the review brief greps the diff for changed `assert` and expected lines); `./gradlew --no-daemon test jacocoTestCoverageVerification jacocoScopeCheck` (Gate G) green with the existing tests unchanged. **Order of work (D-44):** (1) baseline record P0 (a precondition, not a Work Package), (2) campsite P1 (the first Work Package), (3) FR-020 P3 as the first functional change. **Enforced by the Depends-on column (D-110, D-119):** P3a depends on P1b, which depends on P1a, which depends on P1; P3 depends on P3a; P2, P4 and P5, the phases that could otherwise run beside it, depend on P3; every other functional phase depends on one of those. The review check is `git merge-base --is-ancestor <P1 commit>, <P1a commit> and <P1b commit> <first commit of any later phase>`.

## 10. Tracer files

The three tracer files live at `kitty-specs/mission-status-read-api-mission-detail-01M4S6D5/traces/approach.md`, `.../traces/design-decisions.md` and `.../traces/tooling-friction.md`, in the dated-entry format of procedure `mission-tracer-files` (`- **[YYYY-MM-DD][phase] Short symptom.** 1-3 sentences; disposition.`; design decisions as `- **[date][phase] D-n Title.** Rationale; disposition.`). They were seeded by the spec phase (the spec phase calls them tracer files; D-1 to D-67). This plan phase appended entries to each: decisions **D-68 to D-86** (design-decisions; **D-87 to D-107** were appended when the plan was corrected after its review and each names the part of D-68 to D-86 it supersedes), the approach and the read order (approach), and the scaffold side effect and the stale-dispatch observations (tooling-friction). Implementation phases append as they go (P1 records the surfaces it declined in `approach.md`, as a named append); D-131 to D-138 were appended by the fourth plan-fix round and D-139 to D-147 by the fifth and D-148 to D-156 by the round-3 rulings; the close-out assesses them; unresolved friction is filed upstream, never worked around silently. The `.gitattributes` rule `kitty-specs/**/traces/*.md merge=spec-kitty-traces` covers exactly this path.

## 11. Phasing, work-package seams, traceability, tests

### Phasing (risk front-loaded; every phase leaves the branch green)

Order of work is fixed by the spec: **baseline (P0), campsite (P1), then the Java reduction (P3) as the first functional change** (D-44, amending D-22); between the campsite and the reduction sit the operator's two behaviour-preserving insertions P1a and P1b (section 15). P0 is the FR-021 precondition run by the orchestrator, not a Work Package (the tasks stage starts at P1). The graph below enforces D-44: P3 depends on P3a, which follows P1, P1a and P1b, and P2, P4 and P5 depend on P3, so no other functional change can land before it (D-110, D-119). The **primitives phase P5 lands before any route phase** because #5533's Java WPs import it (section 7). `S` = `services/mission-status-api`; `J`, `T`, `R` are as defined at the top. A phase may be split by the tasks stage, never merged across a dependency. **The Depends-on column is the only statement of order (D-87): the concurrent sets below are derived from it and from the write scopes, not the other way round.** Two phases carry a letter because they break a cycle that the first draft had: P4 generates the `resolved-state` goldens from a member-to-source mapping that P3 derives, and P3 is pinned by those goldens. So the mapping is its own first phase (**P3a**, documentation only), P4 generates everything except `resolved-state`, and the `resolved-state` class is **P4b** after both. **P1a and P1b are the operator's ruling A3 (section 15)**: P1a is the stream seam behind the inbound port, P1b the generalised ArchUnit gates; both are behaviour-preserving, both sit between P1 and P3a in the chain `P1 -> P1a -> P1b -> P3a -> P3`, so the order of D-44 (campsite, then the first functional change) holds.

"**Gate G**" below is `cd S && ./gradlew --no-daemon test jacocoTestCoverageVerification jacocoScopeCheck` (section 5: the module's fast tier plus the two JaCoCo gates; never `check`, never a perf, nested-build or native task), run under a UTF-8 file-name locale (section 5, Gate G preconditions). Every Java phase's exit gate is Gate G plus **Gate P** (the pin, `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py -q`) plus the targeted classes it names; CI runs the rest.

**Green at every tip (D-109, D-111).** A phase that adds a manifest class also changes everything that enumerates the manifest, in the same phase: P4 and P4b carry the paired Java census (their rows) and Gate G, and no Java test enumerates the new classes before P7 (the `surface` partition of `ConformanceTree` keeps every existing consumer on the 14 existing classes). From P7 on, a detail class is compared only for the fixtures a route phase has registered in its `ServedRoute` provider, so a route that does not exist yet turns nothing red, and P13's `ServedRoutesCompleteTest` fails if any detail fixture stays unserved. **No gate names a type of a later phase (PLAN-FRESH4-001, D-139, D-140):** a test of a helper that later phases use runs over test-local stand-ins and planted classes (`HttpHarnessDetailTest`, the test of `PlainCaller`, the coverage function of `PermissionCaseCoverageTest`), the floor over the later phases' own cases belongs to the P13 test that owns the last such case (`PermissionCaseFloorTest`, `ProjectionRowCoverageTest`, `ServedRoutesCompleteTest`), and a hook that a later phase's type would feed takes a plain `Class<?>` or `Supplier` (`HttpHarness#startWith`, the recorder's route). Dependencies are checked against what each phase *uses*, not against what it is about: `WpId` (P3) is used by P6, so P6 depends on P3; `ArtifactPaths`, `ArtifactKinds`, `ArtifactKind` and `Eligibility` are used by P6 (the index skips an ineligible candidate), P11 (`artifactPath` of a review cycle) and P12, so they are P5's; the `domain.readmodel.detail` records are returned by the P8 interfaces, so they are P8's; the hand-DTO records and their drift references are P2's. A change a route needs in a record it does not own is a named edit. **A pin that reads a file a phase edits is that phase's gate (PLAN-FRESH2-001):** P2 turns three clauses of `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` red by design (the models-list literal, its needle in `generation_violations`, the generated-mode rule that forbids a populated `dto` directory) and edits them in the same commit as the `build.gradle.kts` change, which keeps the branch green and is the operator-approved exception E-9 (ruled 2026-10-13, D-154); P1a keeps the five pinned `EventsController` needles and runs the file. **The complete list of what the pin reads and who writes it is the readiness row "Everything the pin reads" (PLAN-FRESH4-005, D-143), and its consequence is Gate P: every phase whose gate names Gate G (P1 to P13 but P3a, which writes only `M/data-model.md`) runs the pin, because its tracked-path scan reads `git ls-files` of `S` and each of those phases adds tracked paths there (stage the new files first, as for the Windows guard); its other clauses are named in the rows below where a phase touches them (P1: the probe list; P1a: the `EventsController` needles; P1b and P8: the rule-9 literals of `ArchitectureRulesTest`; P2: the models list, the `dto` clause and the drift probe; P3 and P13: the `@Tag("perf")` of the perf classes; P13: the native floors, `requests.json` and the workflow).**

**Spec-fold (S) before the tasks stage.** The orchestrator (or, if it prefers, a first WP) applies the amendments of section 14 to `spec.md` in one commit; this plan edits no spec text. Until then the plan's wording in section 8 and section 14 is the binding reading.

**P2's go/no-go rule (D-79).** Generated models are used for every new schema **unless** the spike shows any of: (a) a required-nullable member does not serialise as `null` through the canonical mapper; (b) member or map-entry order differs from the golden (note: `ORDER_MAP_ENTRIES_BY_KEYS` sorts by Java `String` natural order, UTF-16, while the golden sorts by code point; the open `subtasks` map is the one member where that can differ, so the mapper emits it through a code-point-ordered list serializer and fixture `unicode-subtask-ids` of class `detail-populated` pins it: S-15); (c) `WorkPackage.promptMarkdown` cannot be omitted when not requested (the generated model writes `null`; `ProblemBody`'s note says the same of `Problem`). On a miss, that model (certainly `WorkPackage`) is a hand-written record in `adapter.in.http.dto` (rule 9 already lists the package) with `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` on `promptMarkdown` only, covered by the response-validation test and a hand drift reference. The records are **owned by P2 (D-111): P2 writes every one that its `research.md` table names, with its hand drift reference and its mutation target, and a route phase adds nothing to `adapter/in/http/dto`**; the `tests/ci` pin follows the same set (`HAND_DTO_RECORDS`, E-9). Controllers are hand-written either way and implement no generated `*Api` interface, because a generated `@RequestParam` would put the framework's boolean converter on `includePrompt` (OQ-2) and a framework exception on `path` (D-28).

**Permission-denied fixtures (spec D-32, D-101).** Every such case (AC-4.3, AC-4.7, AC-10.3 and the permission rows of AC-12.1) is asserted twice: (a) by fault injection through the recorder (`failWhen` with an `AccessDeniedException`, any user, any OS) and (b) on the real file system behind `RealPermissionFixture.refusedForThisUser(path)` (P7), which opens the file as the test user and, when the operating system does not refuse it (a root runner, Windows), appends `not run: precondition unmet: <test id>` to `build/reports/not-run.txt` and skips the test with an assumption. Each route phase marks both legs of a case with `@PermissionCase("<id>")`; the coverage function of P7's `PermissionCaseCoverageTest` is shown red by planted test-local cases (a missing leg, a duplicate id), and P13's `PermissionCaseFloorTest` asserts over the production tests, with a named floor of four ids, that every id has exactly one injected-fault test and one real-permission test, so leg (a) cannot be missing (P11 and P12 write those tests, so no earlier tip can hold the floor). A JUnit skip is green in `check`, so the job-summary report is a hook in `S/build.gradle.kts` (P2, inside C-004): a `doLast` of the `test` task copies `build/reports/not-run.txt` to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` when that variable is set and to standard output otherwise; `SkipReportNestedBuildTest` (a TestKit nested build holding one such skipped case) fails without the hook. No workflow step is added. The Python generator never makes an unreadable file with `chmod` (P4): permission-denied rows are Java-only, like `projection-refusals`, so `--check` does not depend on the user who runs it.

**Shared files: one owner each (ledger SK-357, D-87).** `finalize-tasks` puts any overlapping pair of `owned_files` into one lane, even a dependency-ordered pair with an intermediate Work Package elsewhere (it then reports `LANE_DEPENDENCY_CYCLE`); the workaround is one owner per shared file in `owned_files` and a named out-of-map edit for every other writer. The tasks stage uses this table; "named edit" means: rebase, state the edit in the commit message, touch nothing else of the file.

**Concurrent sets (derived from the Depends-on column; every pair below has disjoint scopes after the shared-file table is applied).** P1 first (after the P0 record), then P1a, then P1b, then P3a, then P3 alone: nothing functional, and nothing else structural, runs beside the reduction (D-110, D-119). After P3: **P2 ‖ P4 ‖ P5**; after P4: **P4b** (‖ P2, P5). After P3, P4, P4b and P5: **P7**. After P5, P4, P3, P2 and P7: **P6** (D-126: P6 no longer runs beside P7; P6's provisioned cases and its non-ASCII cases need P7's `ProvisionPlan` and `Utf8Host`; no cycle arises because P7 depends on P3, P4, P4b and P5 only). **P8** after P2, P5 and P7, so **P6 ‖ P8** is a concurrent pair, with disjoint scopes. **P9** after P2, P3, P6, P7 and P8, then **P10 ‖ P11**. **P12** after P2, P5, P7 and P8 (it needs neither the ledger nor the index, and its eligibility rule is P5's) and may run beside P9 to P11. **P13** last. `P2` must be merged into the stack before P8 (the shared mappers and the hand-DTO decision depend on it). The scope check (a script over this table that expands the braces, derives the concurrent pairs from the Depends-on column and tests each pair, and over the section 13 tree that tests "owned by exactly one phase") was run after each correction round, last after round 3 (D-110, D-111, D-119, D-126); the authoritative run is `spec-kitty agent mission finalize-tasks --validate-only`, whose `validate_no_overlap` and lane computation must report one lane per concurrent phase (if it collapses lanes, SK-357 is the explanation: fix the manifest by this table, do not edit the table's disjointness). **Chokepoints (serialise; every phase touching one rebases and states its edit):** `S/build.gradle.kts` (P2 alone), `Allowlist`/`AllowlistAccess`/`AllowlistPassThrough` and the README allowlist fence (P5 alone: all ten classes and eleven entries land in one change so the fence amendment is atomic), the shared files above, and `ServiceWiring`/`MissionStatusApplicationHints` (`ServiceWiring` is edited twice, by P1 and by P1a, both before P3; each route phase adds a wiring class of its own; hints only on a proven miss). **Checked against open pull requests:** no open PR touches any path above (readiness facts); the shared surface is #5529's own branch (every `S/**` file), which this branch carries through `4dc35c7d5`; the orchestrator-run merge of any later #5529 tip into this branch is followed by the C-005 pre-flight of section 5 (D-107) at the start of each phase.

**Two pull requests, readability (operator ruling E-8, 2026-10-13, D-152; it supersedes the one-PR default of D-54, whose stacking on #5529 stands).** Both are DRAFTS. **PR A = P0, P1, P1a, P1b, P2, P3a, P3, P4, P4b, P5, P6, P7, P8**; its base is the branch that carries #5529's last slice. **PR B = P9, P10, P11, P12, P13**; its base is PR A's branch. The operator retargets PR A to `main` after #5529 lands and PR B after PR A lands (when PR A lands by squash or merge, the orchestrator rebases PR B `--onto` the new `main`, checks that the PR B tree is unchanged, and never runs tests under `git rebase -x`). #5533's Java work stacks on PR A, which carries every primitive. Hand-written Java will exceed what is readable in one sitting once tests are counted; generated, count and golden files (`R/conformance/**`, `jacoco-class-floor.properties`, `requests.json`) are reviewed by regeneration command, not by line. **Compact the history before each draft PR** (charter, `mission-wrap-up-sequence`), in this order: (1) run the ordering check of section 6 (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`) and the review items that name commits; (2) compact PR A's range (P0 to P8, cut at the P8 tip) and check that `git diff <pre-compaction P8 tip> <compacted tip>` is empty; (3) re-record the post-compaction commits of P5, P7 and P8 in `design-decisions.md` (section 7); (4) re-run the baseline-record test and the generator `--check` on the compacted PR A tip (section 6: base B is rewritten by design and the record's `unreachable_after_rewrite` rule absorbs it); (5) rebase PR B's commits (P9 to P13) `--onto` the compacted PR A tip, check the PR A part of the tree is unchanged, then compact PR B's range the same way before PR B is opened. A published ref is frozen: PR B's compaction never rewrites a PR A commit.

**Phase-to-PR map (tasks.md states the split and carries a `PR` column, A or B, for every Work Package; the WP numbers are the tasks stage's).**

| PR | Phases (Work Package candidates) | What the PR is | Base |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | P0 (precondition, not a WP), P1, P1a, P1b, P2, P3a, P3, P4, P4b, P5, P6, P7, P8 | Campsite, the stream seam and the ArchUnit gates, the contract models and build spike, the resolved-state reduction, the oracle extension and goldens, the primitives (text-read port, read fence, redaction), the decoders and the Work Package index, the Java harness, the four inbound interfaces with the gate, the 21 read-model records and the typed identifiers. **No route is served** | #5529's last slice |
| B | P9, P10, P11, P12, P13 | The four route phases (`getMission`; `getWorkPackage` with `promptMarkdown`; `getWorkPackageDetail`; the two artifact routes) and the closing phase (CI, native, nightly, README, perf, full-flow tests) | PR A |

**Each PR is self-contained and green at its own tip (E-8, D-153).** The plan statements that would have broken one of them are fixed as follows.

- *What PR A needs from P13.* Nothing is deferred to P13 that PR A's tip needs: the D-145 equality comparison of the two JaCoCo lines is also P8's gate; a reflection-hint miss is a named edit of P6 or P8 (shared-file table), proven by the local native run of E-7; the native floors (`--floor` 3, `--request-floor` 49) and `requests.json` stay as #5529 left them because PR A adds no `@Tag("native")` test, no smoke stage and no workflow line; P13 raises them in the same commits as the tests that need them (D-99).
- *Pin `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py`, clause by clause.* At PR A's tip: the rule-9 literals (P1b, P8), the `adapter/in/http` sub-directory names (`detail` holds `RawQuery` and `IncludePrompt` only, P8; no `dto` directory unless the P2 spike says so, and then in the P2 commit), the `EventsController` needles (P1a), the models list and drift probe (P2), the `@Tag("perf")` rule (P3's class), the tracked-path scan (every phase), the probe list (P1). At PR B's tip: the same set plus the perf classes of P13, the native floors, `requests.json` and the workflow (P13).
- *Files that name later routes.* `ServedRoutes` (P7) loads its four providers by name and treats a missing class as an empty route, so with no provider at PR A's tip `DetailGoldenSuiteTest` compares nothing and is not red; its non-vacuity is the planted self-test provider; `ServedRoutesCompleteTest` is P13 (PR B) and must not appear in PR A. `OutOfScopeRoutesTest` is unchanged in PR A (the five routes still answer 404) and P10 to P12 each remove their own path in PR B. `S/README.md` documents no served route in PR A (P5's allowlist section only, with its parity test); the routes, scope and prompt sections are PR B's.
- *Counts and floors in files owned by later phases.* `R/jacoco-class-floor.properties` carries, at PR A's tip, exactly the counts of P1a to P8; PR B starts from those numbers. A fold commit in PR A that adds a class after PR B has started is recounted by PR B's next phase (R-38). `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` is 16 at PR A's tip (P8). The manifest classes, the census (`DETAIL_CLASS_FLOORS`) and the `resolved-state` floor are P4 and P4b, both in PR A.
- *Rule 15's non-zero scanned count at PR A's tip.* The production subject `adapter.in.http.detail..` holds `RawQuery` and `IncludePrompt` (P8), so the count is non-zero without a controller; the conforming skeleton controller covers the rest.
- *The beans.* `DetailInfrastructureWiring` (`TextSource`, `AdmissionGate`) and `DecoderWiring` are constructed at start-up with no consumer until PR B; no `MissionDetailQuery` bean exists in PR A, and no controller injects one.
- *AC-6.6.* The stub leg is P6 (PR A); the three-route child-JVM leg is P13 (PR B).
- *The Mission record at each tip (cutover-guard, mission-state audit, corpus-blocking).* At each PR's tip `M/meta.json` is complete and `M/status.json` is the materialisation of `M/status.events.jsonl` as committed (`spec-kitty agent status materialize --mission <slug>`; never hand-edited, and no untracked CLI state is committed blind: compare with the coordination copy first). At PR A's tip the Work Packages of P1 to P8 are in their recorded lanes and P9 to P13 are `planned`; the mission-state audit (`spec-kitty doctor mission-state --audit --fail-on teamspace-blocker --json`) is run at both tips before the operator publishes each ref.
- *The frozen archive root.* Once PR A is on `main`, every file of `M/**` is on `main`, and `tests/architectural/test_archive_root_byte_identical.py` fails any modification of it. PR B's record (status events, `status.json`, tracer appends, review files, `tasks/`) cannot be modified in place after the retarget. This is not a choice the plan can make: **fork E-12 (section 14)**.

### Requirements-to-plan traceability

### Test strategy (layers, red-first, revert-fails)

Layers: **domain unit** (no Spring: reducer, phase, readiness, title rows, kind classifier, path rule, redaction), **application** (index, walk, gate, services over the in-memory sources), **adapter** (filesystem faults, link refusal, cap race, HTTP framing, raw-query decoding), **ArchUnit** (rules 1 to 16 each with a planted-violation control and a non-zero scanned count), **contract validation** (networknt, every response), **goldens** (port-level subset on both sources; adapter suite on the real adapter), **native** (tagged tests; smoke digest; CI legs only). **Red-first is stated per phase (D-87), not claimed for all:** "red on the phase base, green on its tip" is reserved for P3, P5, P6, P7, P8 and P9 to P13 (P1a and P1b carry their own red-first first commits, above). P1 is behaviour-preserving, so its form is "every existing test unchanged and green on the base and on the tip, no assertion changed"; P1a is behaviour-preserving at the HTTP boundary and starts with the red `PortLeakCanaryTest` (every HTTP stream test unchanged and green on base and tip, and the ten edited files change types, never an expected value); P1b is test-only and starts with each planted fixture red against the rule it targets; P0, P4 and P4b are "the record and golden tests are red on a copy with a row, module or byte removed"; P2 is "the drift-probe mutation target and the skip-report test are red without their change"; P3a has no behaviour. The reviewer verifies the form of the phase's own row and never signs a vacuous red.

**Revert-fails rows.** Every row is a **standing** named test that fails when the change is made, except the rows marked (M), which are one-off mutations run once in a scratch copy of the tree before each PR (never by editing the working tree under review) with their commands and results in the PR's "Tests run" section. Rows that only exercise a comparator say so ("comparator control") and are not counted as a reverted behaviour.

| AC-1.1 to AC-1.4 | comparator control: change one phase `status` in a golden; alter `nextAction` in the detail path only; test the Completed event before the file in `PhaseDeriver` (the `phases-matrix` class goes red); reverse the list rule for a ledger-only Work Package |
| AC-1.5, AC-1.7 | accept a `mid8`/slug/number selector; stop the lookup at the first faulted sibling (D-39 reverted): `bad-sibling-before` turns red; take the fault of a later sibling into the answer |
