**Discovered-count guards (defect class: an iteration over a glob, directory or list that silently matches nothing).** Each of these asserts a named floor so that zero matches fails: the fixtures per class of `CLASS_FLOORS` and `DETAIL_CLASS_FLOORS` (at least the Count column of FR-014 and the amendments; the Python map equals the union of the two Java maps), the detail fixtures served by a route provider (`ServedRoutesCompleteTest`), the divergence entries (V1 to V19, V20 where D-97 needs it), the ArchUnit rules and the classes each scans, the JaCoCo patterns, the corpus Work Package files and Missions of the nightly job, the ids of `portlevel-tests.txt`, the requests `nativeSmoke` replays, the digest files the gate downloads, the `@Tag("native")` tests `nativeTest` ran, the tracked service paths in the NFR-007 check, the rows of `baseline-red.yaml`, the `@ProjectionRow` cases (26 pairs over 18 rows), the production `@PermissionCase` floor of four ids (P13), the `@PermissionCase` ids (D-101), the discovered `adapter.in.<x>` packages of rule 3 (pinned to `{http}`, each with a non-zero scanned count), the annotated controller classes of rule 3b (floor 5 plus the allow-listed one), and the classes of `application.query.port.in` scanned by `PortExposesNoLedgerRowTest`. Each guard has a revert row above or a planted-empty control.

## 12. Native release matrix

The four legs, runners, GraalVM setup, strict dependency verification, the digest comparison and the release job are #5529's and are **unchanged** (`mission-status-api.yml`: `ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `macos-15`, `windows-2025`; runner labels remain as unverifiable offline as in #5529, Unverified 7). This mission changes only the numbers and the nightly job's environment, all in phase P13, and pins them with the existing tests. **No environment line is added to the native legs**: the smoke harness sets the locale for the binary it launches (D-90).

| Item | Today (verified) | After this mission |
|---|---|---|
| `native-tag-floor --floor` (both the Linux/macOS and the Windows step) | 3 | **8**, if exactly five plain `@Test` methods are added (D-99): decoder valid and decoder rejected, content 200 and content 422, detail assembly. The true pin is `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py`: `native_test_count()` counts the `@Test` lines of the files under `S/src/test/java/.../config/nativetags` that contain `@Tag("native")` (a `@ParameterizedTest` counts 0 there while the gate counts every executed case, so **only plain `@Test` methods are allowed in that directory**), `native_floor_violations` requires both `--floor` values to equal that count, and `test_the_native_floor_is_the_number_of_native_tagged_tests` asserts the literal `native_test_count() == 3`. **P13 alone** adds the five tests under `T/config/nativetags/**` and edits, in one commit, everything of the pin and the workflow that hard-codes 3 (PLAN-FRESH3-013): the two `--floor` literals of the workflow; the needle `"--floor 3"` in the native-job check of the pin (the `for needle in (...)` tuple); the literal `native_test_count() == 3`; and the controls that `replace("--floor 3", ...)` and pass `3`, `4` and `2` as the count. Those controls are rewritten relative to `native_test_count()`, because a `replace` of a literal that no longer occurs is a no-op and a control that asserts a violation after a no-op replace would pass for the wrong reason. The value written is whatever `native_test_count()` returns after the five are added. P6, P11 and P12 add no native-tagged test, so `tests/ci` stays green in every phase between them (the first draft had them add tests with the pin edit left to P13). The gate's own semantics: `native_tag_floor` fails when the executed count is **below** the floor |
| `requests.json` stages | `base` 37, `hostOrigin` 12 (sum 49) | new stage `detail` with `requestCountFloor` **14**, one request per line: `d01` `getMission` 200; `d02` `getMission` with a `mid8` 404; `d03` `getWorkPackage` 200 without a prompt; `d04` `getWorkPackage` 200 with `includePrompt=true`; `d05` `getWorkPackage` with `includePrompt=1` 400 `invalid_parameter`; `d06` `getWorkPackage` of a ledger-only Work Package 404; `d07` `getWorkPackageDetail` 200; `d08` `getWorkPackageDetail` with a legacy `lanes.json` 500 `source_unreadable`; `d09` `listArtifacts` 200 (**one** request, over a tree whose names pin the UTF-8 byte order); `d10` `getArtifactContent` 200 redacted; `d11` 422 (a credential file assembled at start-up from fragments, D-48); `d12` 415 (a NUL file assembled at start-up); `d13` 400 (repeated `path`); `d14` 404 (a missing file). N = 14, so the `--request-floor` of the `digests` step becomes **63** (49 + 14); the existing pin already checks it equals the sum of the stage floors, and `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py -q` after editing both numbers is the P13 exit command |
| Environment of the smoke and nightly processes | inherited | **Smoke:** `NativeSmokeTest` (which builds the `ProcessBuilder` for the binary) puts `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` into the child's environment on non-Windows hosts, so no workflow line is needed and no workflow line outside the C-004 list is written (D-90); macOS defaults to UTF-8; the rule is not applied on Windows (FR-004). **Nightly:** `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` on the `nightly-corpus` job, which C-004 names (S-16) |
| Fixture modification times and links | n/a | set at provisioning by `ProvisionPlan` (P7) so listing bytes agree across legs (R-9); a leg that cannot create a link or a junction reports `not run: link unsupported` in the job summary and never a pass |
| `nativePerf` and the floor binary | `NativePerfTest` against the built binary and the same-leg floor binary | unchanged workflow steps; P13's write scope covers `T/nativesmoke/**`, so if `NativePerfTest` runs the NFR-002 scenario it is extended there with the ten concurrent prompt requests (S-13); a `nativePerf` miss is classified under the ADR, never retried |

The smoke tree is the same committed `tree:` conformance trees plus a start-up provisioning step, so no second copy of any golden exists. The nightly job's `--mission-status.port=8787` start is unchanged.

```text
                       (changed, P1a: StreamRefused[+ detail])

```

**Ports.** *Inbound* (`application.query.port.in`), parameters and results as designed and nothing Spring, servlet, JSON or generated: `MissionDetailQuery#mission(MissionId missionId)`; `WorkPackageQuery#workPackage(MissionId missionId, WpId wpId, boolean includePrompt)`; `WorkPackageDetailQuery#workPackageDetail(MissionId missionId, WpId wpId)`; `ArtifactQuery#list(MissionId missionId)` and `#content(MissionId missionId, ArtifactPathInput path)` (`MissionId` and `WpId` are records of `domain.model`). Results are the `domain.readmodel.detail` records; refusals are `DetailRefusal`, a `final` unchecked exception (`extends RuntimeException`: a `Throwable`, as rule 13 clause c requires of every class in `port.in.exceptions`, and unchecked, so the no-checked-exception clause of rule 16 holds) thrown by the four interfaces, carrying its nested `Kind` and nothing else: no message text beyond the kind's wire word, no HTTP status, no path (kinds NOT_FOUND, INVALID_ARTIFACT_PATH, ARTIFACT_TOO_LARGE, ARTIFACT_NOT_TEXT, ARTIFACT_SECRET, ARTIFACT_UNREADABLE, ARTIFACT_LISTING_UNREADABLE, SOURCE_UNREADABLE) and the existing `MissionStatusUnavailable` (carrying the diagnostic) for the generic 500s of `getMission` and `getWorkPackage` (DC-2 option A). **The identifiers are typed values with a lenient parse (operator ruling E-5, 2026-10-13, D-150; it supersedes the string wording of D-78 and D-95).** `MissionId.parse(String)` and `WpId.parse(String)` are static factories in `domain.model` that return an `Optional` (empty for an ill-formed value: a ULID of 26 characters for the Mission, the Work Package id pattern for the Work Package) and never throw; they have unit tests (`MissionIdParseTest`, P8; `WpIdTest`, P3). Each adapter (HTTP now, MCP later) calls the factory on the raw path value and turns an empty result into `DetailRefusal.notFound()`, a static factory of the `NOT_FOUND` kind; the one kind-to-status mapping (404) is `ProblemMapper`'s, so rows C2 and W1 are decided once, no adapter chooses between 400 and 404, and the port signatures carry no raw `String` identifier (rule 16 clause c). A `mid8`, a slug or a number is not a ULID, so it parses empty and answers 404 as before (AC-1.5). `ArtifactPathInput` is sealed: `Values(List<String>)` (an MCP adapter passes one element) or `Undecodable`. *Outbound* (`application.query.port.out`): the existing `MissionEventSource` (ledger, meta, listing), the new `TextSource` (section 7), `FrontmatterDecoder` (`FrontmatterResult decodeWorkPackage(byte[])`, `ReviewCycleMeta decodeReviewCycle(byte[])`: bytes in, a typed domain record or a rejection out, no path and no file access) and `LanesDecoder` (`LanesResult decode(byte[])`: `Manifest`, `Corrupt` or `Legacy`). All three result types (`FrontmatterResult`, `ReviewCycleMeta`, `LanesResult`) are `domain.model` records or sealed interfaces, so the codec classes may return them (rule 12a) and the application may consume them (rule 2b). The diagnostics consumer stays the `java.util.function.Consumer<Diagnostic>` handed in by `config`. *Stream (ruling A3, section 15)*: `MissionStatusQuery#subscribe(StreamScope, Optional<StreamCursor>)` returns a `Subscription` whose `next(Duration)` yields `StreamSignal.Events(List<StreamEvent>)`, `Dropped`, `Closed` or `Idle`; it throws `StreamRefused` (with an optional fixed detail), `MissionStatusUnavailable` or `StreamCapacityReached`; `changesSince` returns `StreamBatch`; `endStreams()` ends every open subscription; `StreamRequest.parse(String, String)` decodes the two raw query values in the legacy order. No type in this list names a raw ledger row (`PortExposesNoLedgerRowTest`).

**Scope views of the fence (D-77).** `AllowlistAccess#scoped(ReadScope)` returns a view that tags every access with the scope; `FilesystemTextSource` holds one `SafeFiles` per scope (`AUTHORED`, `ARTIFACT`) so the existing callers (`Listing`, `ReadDirResolver`, the event source) are unchanged and stay AUTHORED. `Allowlist.classify(scope, root, path)`: under AUTHORED the existing specific-first classifier applies and `ARTIFACT_DIR`/`ARTIFACT_FILE` never match; under ARTIFACT only those two classes apply and `PLANNING_FILE`, `TASKS_MD`, `WP_FILE`, `WP_CYCLE_FILE`, `MISSION_META`, `LANES_JSON` are not consulted (so a content read of `spec.md` is ARTIFACT_FILE READ, while a READ of `spec.md` under AUTHORED is PLANNING_FILE READ and fails the fence); under ARTIFACT the root `status.events.jsonl` and `status.json` (any case) are unclassified; under AUTHORED `status.json` (any case) is unclassified while the root `status.events.jsonl` **stays class LEDGER at its exact name** (`Allowlist.CLASSIFIERS` lists it, and the three ledger routes and #5529's routes read their ledger there); everything outside the primary directory is unclassified under both. The AC-11.4 control gains the green twin: a READ of the root `status.events.jsonl` under AUTHORED is permitted, and the same READ under ARTIFACT fails (S-21). The primary directory is `MISSION_DIR` (STAT, REALPATH) under AUTHORED, where the containment check `requireContainedDir` runs, and `ARTIFACT_DIR` (STAT, LIST) under ARTIFACT, where the first listing of the walk runs. `Access` keeps its 3-component form for existing tests and gains a scope; the recorder keeps `(route, scope, class, mode)`, the route being supplied by a `Supplier<String>` on the recorder (P7's request-scoped `RouteMarker` is what a harness installs). A fence test asserts against the quadruple, never the path alone. **Bounded listing (D-92).** `AllowlistAccess` also gains `listNames(Path dir, int limit)` returning `AllowlistAccess.NameWindow`, a record declared in the interface as `NameWindow(List<String> names, boolean limitReached)` (the names and a more-exist flag, taken lazily from one `DirectoryStream` and stopping at `limit + 1`), implemented in `AllowlistPassThrough` and logged by the recorder as LIST; `FilesystemTextSource` uses only this variant, `NoUnboundedListingTest` (beside `NoUncappedReadTest`) plants a call to the unbounded `listNames(Path)` from it and requires red, and a test lists a directory of `limit + 2` entries and asserts at most `limit + 1` names were pulled. Without it `limitReached` and `trustworthy` could only be produced by materialising the whole directory, which defeats the bounded-memory claim of AC-4.9 and NFR-002.

**Rules 13 to 16 (new; `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` 12 to 14 at P1b, to 16 at P8). Rules 13 and 14 are the operator's two layout rules (P1b: ports are interfaces only, D-130; the query side never depends on the command side, D-131); rules 15 and 16 are FR-013's former 13 and 14 (renumbered twice, S-25).** Arithmetic: 12 existing + 2 (P1b) = 14; 14 + 2 (P8) = 16; `ArchitectureRulesTest` and the contiguity guard compare the rules run with `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT`, and `ArchitectureControlsTest` requires a control for every id from 1 to the count. A nested class is judged by its outermost class (ArchUnit `JavaClass#getTopLevelClass`), so the nested `Kind` enums of `StreamRefused`, `SourceFault` and `TextFault` are judged as the exceptions they sit in.

| # | Rule | Subject (non-zero scanned count asserted) | Positive control |
|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | **Ports are interfaces only (operator 2026-10-13, D-130, D-131).** The one definition, referred to by section 15.4: over `application.query.port..`, each class judged by its outermost class, (a) a class residing directly in `port.in` or `port.out` has an interface as its outermost class; (b) a class in `port.in.dto` or `port.out.dto` has an outermost class that is not a `Throwable`; (c) a class in `port.in.exceptions` or `port.out.exceptions` has an outermost class that is a `Throwable`; (d) every class of `port..` resides in one of `port.in`, `port.out` and the four `dto` and `exceptions` subpackages (no other subpackage) | `application.query.port..`; the production run asserts a non-zero count for each of the two roots and the four subpackages | `fixture.application.query.port.in.DtoInRoot` (a record in a root: a), `fixture.application.query.port.out.ExceptionInRoot` (a), `...port.in.dto.ExceptionInDto` (b), `...port.in.exceptions.RecordInExceptions` (c), `...port.in.helpers.Stray` (d), each red on rule 13 only. The conforming skeleton gains `port.in.dto.Value` and `port.in.exceptions.Refusal` with a nested enum `Kind`, which exercises the outermost-class judgement the production `StreamRefused.Kind` and `SourceFault.Kind` rely on |
| 14 | **`application.query` never depends on `application.command` (operator 2026-10-13, D-131).** No class of `application.query..` depends on a class of `application.command..` | `application.query..` (non-zero in production). The target package does not exist in production by design; the rule keeps it from being reached before the write module exists, and the planted control is what shows that it bites | `fixture.application.query.ReadsCommand`, depending on `fixture.application.command.Writer` (the fixture tree only), red on rule 14 only; the skeleton's `fixture.application.query.Service` is the conforming scanned member |
| 15 | The controllers, parameter converters and contract mappers of the five operations depend, among project packages, only on `application.query.port.in..` (the root, `dto/` and `exceptions/`), the records and enums of `domain.model`, `domain.readmodel.overview` and `domain.readmodel.detail`, `adapter.in.http` itself and the generated model/dto packages; not on `domain.readmodel.reducer`, `domain.redaction`, `domain.readmodel.derive`, a non-record class of `domain.readmodel.overview`, or `application..` other than `application.query.port.in..` | `adapter.in.http.detail..` (rule 3, generalised to every `adapter.in.<x>`, stays the broader bound; the events adapter is outside the subject, D-50) | `fixture.adapter.in.http.detail.R15Controller`, calling the non-record `fixture.domain.readmodel.derive.R15Deriver` (red on rule 15 only); the conforming skeleton gains `fixture.adapter.in.http.detail.DetailController` (it calls only the skeleton's `port.in.MissionDetailQuery` and returns its `DetailValue` record), so the skeleton test scans a class (PLAN-FRESH3-006) |
| 16 | The inbound interfaces of the new operations and the result types they return import no `org.springframework..`, `jakarta..`, `org.openapitools..` or generated package (clause a, **which includes the stream vocabulary**: PLAN-FRESH4-009), and no public method of the interfaces declares a checked exception (clause b), and no parameter of a public method of the four new interfaces is a `String` (clause c, E-5: an identifier is a `MissionId` or a `WpId`, the path is an `ArtifactPathInput`, the prompt flag a boolean) | clause a: every class of `application.query.port.in..` (the stream vocabulary included) plus every class of `domain.readmodel.detail..`; clause b: the same set except `MissionStatusQuery` and `Subscription`; clause c: only the four interfaces `MissionDetailQuery`, `WorkPackageQuery`, `WorkPackageDetailQuery` and `ArtifactQuery` (the stream vocabulary and the read-model records are outside it: `StreamScope.parse(String)` and `StreamRequest.parse(String, String)` take raw text by design) (`Subscription#next` declares `InterruptedException`, D-50; the exemption now belongs to that one clause and no longer to the whole rule). The subject is structural, not a list of names, so the fixture root evaluates the same predicate | `fixture.application.query.port.in.R16IoException` (a method declaring `IOException`; red on rule 16 only), `R16StringId` (a method with a `String` identifier parameter; red on rule 16 only, clause c) and `R16Spring` (imports Spring; red on rules 2, 8 and 16, the co-reds pinned) and `StreamSpring` (an interface whose simple name starts with `Stream` and that imports Spring: red on rules 2, 8 and 16 like `R16Spring`, which shows that the stream vocabulary is inside clause a); the conforming skeleton gains `fixture.application.query.port.in.MissionDetailQuery` and `fixture.domain.readmodel.detail.DetailValue`, which the rule scans |

**Rule 3 is generalised by the operator's ruling A3 (P1b, section 15.4):** every `adapter.in.<x>` depends, among project packages, only on `application.query.port.in`, `domain` and itself; a part 3b confines the Spring controller annotations to `adapter.in..` (the one allow-listed class is `config.ProblemErrorController`); rules 1 and 2 gain a part banning reflective class loading and lose `java.lang.reflect..` and `java.lang.invoke..` from what `java.lang..` allows (with the recorded fallback). Rule 15's subject stays `adapter.in.http.detail..` and rule 3 is its broader bound for every adapter. Rules 3 and 4 keep their meaning and take the re-cut package strings (`application.query.port.in..` and `application.query.port.out..`); rules 1 and 2 (apart from the reflection part), 5 to 12 keep their text, because every new package sits below the package they name. Rule 7 (file APIs only in `adapter.out.filesystem`, codec excluded) is why the decode-bytes class `StrictUtf8` and the two codec classes may not hold a path. `NoUncappedReadTest` is extended to cover `readCapped` (an `int` cap; only `SafeFiles` calls `openRead`).

**Admission gate (FR-022).** `AdmissionGate` (`application.query`): a fair `java.util.concurrent.Semaphore(4, true)`; `<T> T run(Supplier<T>)` acquires uninterruptibly and releases in `finally`; it never refuses, never times out and creates no thread (rule 2c). It exposes a test accessor `int highWaterMark()` of concurrently held permits. It is held by `WorkPackageDetailQueryService`, `ArtifactQueryService` (both operations) and `WorkPackageQueryService` **only for `includePrompt=true`** (S-13, E-1: the operator's ruling of 2026-10-13, D-148, which supersedes spec decision D-64 "reported"). **Four heavy operations are gated.** `getMission`, `getWorkPackage` without a prompt and the three existing operations are not behind it.

**Walk and listing (FR-004).** `ArtifactWalk` lists with `nameLimit = 100000 - counter` through the bounded `listNames(Path, int)` (`limitReached` true means the next name would be the 100001st: 500 `artifact_listing_unreadable`); every returned name counts one (files, directories, links, ineligible names, the root records); a directory counts once as a name in its parent and is entered unless it is a link or its relative path already has 512 characters; the collector is a bounded max-heap of the 1001 smallest paths under `CodePoints.compare` (code point order of a path equals UTF-8 byte order), with a test accessor for its high-water mark; `truncated` is true exactly when the 1001st exists; `readable` is computed only for the final at most 1000 entries by `readText` with the 262144 cap (a size above it is decided from `stat` alone and not opened). `modifiedAt` is `modifiedEpochSecond` (floored) in UTC with `Z`.

**Phases (FR-001).** `PhaseDeriver` takes the lane counts and `wpTotal`, the set of present planning artifacts (`stat` of `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `tasks.md` in the primary directory, a regular non-symlink file; a link writes `path_refused`; a failed non-"absent" stat is M8) and `StatusSnapshot.lifecycleTypes()`. The order inside a planning phase is file, Completed event, Started event, pending. Implement and review use `derive_phase_status`'s rules on the counts alone.

## Docs impact

**None under `docs/`** (C-004 lists no docs file; D-84, S-14). The service `README.md` (under `services/`, outside the docs spelling scope) is the documentation surface: the allowlist section (P5), "Prompt text" (P10), the UTF-8 locale requirement (P12), the routes-and-scope section that says drift and Ops have **no Java owner yet** and that governance is #5533 on these primitives (P13, FR-018), the divergence list V1 to V19 and the note that the ledger and the Work Package files are read at slightly different instants (Reflexivity); one sentence that the stream projection, the stream cap and the shutdown registry belong to the inbound port and serve every adapter (P13 writes it, with the routes-and-scope section; ruling A3; the cap key `mission-status.stream.max-connections` and its README lines are unchanged). No `docs/changelog/CHANGELOG.md` line is added here: #5529 adds the entry that the service exists; whether this mission adds one is a C-004 amendment the operator may ask for (E-4). No `toc.yml`, page-inventory or Divio change is triggered, so the docs freshness gate has nothing to bite.

## Risks and mitigations

| Risk (plan ids R-n; the spec's R-1 to R-13 stand) | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| R-14 `sun.jnu.encoding` under the native image and under a child JVM test; the forked test JVM of Gate G itself | `NameEncoding` is injected (unit leg under any locale); one child-JVM leg with `LC_ALL=C`; the smoke harness starts the binary with `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8` (D-90); the native behaviour of the property is a P5/P13 spike item (Unverified 5). **Gate G requires a UTF-8 file-name locale and says so (section 5); the tests that create non-ASCII names skip with a recorded `not run: precondition unmet` through `Utf8Host` when it is not (D-128)** |
| R-15 snakeyaml 1.1 versus ruamel 1.2 (booleans, octals, timestamps), pydantic coercion with `extra="forbid"` | `WpFrontmatterCodec` uses a restricted `SafeConstructor` and **refuses what it cannot decode identically**; the 28-case corpus (`wpm-01..28`, S-19; `wpm-14`, `wpm-15`, `wpm-16`, `wpm-17` pin the dialect cases) and the nightly all-file comparison decide; a need for a new library is escalated (E-6), not added |
| R-16 Open PRs #6122 (frontmatter block scalars), #6127 (ownership validators), #5953 (skipped subtasks and scoped mark-status in `status/models.py`, `store.py`, `core/subtask_rows.py`) change Python the oracle imports. **Only #6127 is plausibly golden-only.** #6122 changes what `FrontmatterManager.read` returns and #5953 adds a `skipped` subtask state, in the very code P3 and P6 mirror: regenerating goldens would move Python's expectation while the Java decoder and the FR-020 subtasks projection diverge (the contract's subtask-state enum may not even have `skipped`) | For #6122 and #5953: a merge **before the P3/P6 freeze** is a spec/plan escalation to the orchestrator (add a fixture `wpm-NN` and a subtask-state row, and decide mirror versus declare a V-row divergence; the Java fix is the owning phase's, P6 for the decoder and P3 for the reduction); **after the freeze** it goes through the `knownDivergence` list and the nightly comparison names it. For #6127: regenerate with `--write` and read the `--check` signal. `gh pr list --state open` is re-run at the start of P3 and P6 (D-106). No phase edits those `src` files (C-007) |
| R-17 JSON map order | `ORDER_MAP_ENTRIES_BY_KEYS` sorts by UTF-16 order; the open `subtasks` map is emitted through a code-point-ordered serializer and pinned by `unicode-subtask-ids` (S-15) |
| R-18 `getWorkPackage?includePrompt=true` memory (about 11 MiB each) | admission gate (S-13); NFR-002 arithmetic amended |
| R-19 `SafeFiles.readRegular` can read one 64 KiB chunk past the cap | `readCapped` bounds the final read to `cap + 1 - read`; `readRegular` delegates; AC-7.1 and AC-5.4 assert the exact byte count (S-17) |
| R-20 Lookup cost: 500 `meta.json` reads on every request | priced in NFR-001 and AC-1.8; the counts are pinned (FRESH3-002); a future cache keyed by directory stamp is not built (D-21) |
| R-21 `Listing.missionNames` stats every `meta.json` on each `listMissions` | existing behaviour (STAT only); included in the NFR-001 measurement; not changed here |
| R-22 A non-corpus module in `tests/contract` runs nowhere | by default (D-108) the baseline-record tests live in the already-registered, corpus-marked conformance module, so no module is added and `test_corpus_marked_modules_match_the_curated_registry` is green at every commit; a dedicated corpus-marked module with its registry entry and Packs token was not added (E-2 ruled 2026-10-13: the default stands, D-149) |
| R-23 `ProblemAdvice` and a controller that reads the raw query: Tomcat's own `InvalidParameterException` fires only when parameters are parsed | `getWorkPackage` calls `getParameterValues("includePrompt")` so a malformed percent-encoding keeps the status-only 400 (W2); the artifact controller never parses parameters and decodes `path` itself (D-28); a test covers both |
| R-24 Stack churn: #5529's fold rounds change a coupled symbol | each phase's first step is the **mechanical C-005 pre-flight** of section 5 (`CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` plus the recorded #5529 tip sha, D-107), not a re-read; the orchestrator-run merge of a new #5529 tip is followed by `--check` and the Java gate; no agent pushes or retargets |
| R-25 Cache growth (NFR-009) | measured against `nfr009-base.md` (taken by P3 at its first commit, section 6 item 5); fallback of D-51 (derive history per request from the ledger lines) is pre-decided |
| R-26 SK-353 (spec R-10): Missions whose Work Packages write `subtasks` as mappings (Mission `058-hybrid-prompt-and-shim-agent-surface`) | refused with `projection_refused` by the D-97 decision, pinned by fixture `wpm-28`, compared per Work Package file by the nightly step (AC-6.4); the refused list gains the Mission with a decision-record entry |
| R-27 Ledger SK-357: overlapping `owned_files` collapse lanes at `finalize-tasks` (and may report `LANE_DEPENDENCY_CYCLE`) | the shared-file table of section 11 gives each shared file one owner and names the other writers; the concurrent phases have disjoint scopes; `finalize-tasks --validate-only` is the authoritative check |
| R-28 The nightly job cannot be dispatched before #5529 reaches `main`, so it is untested in CI at PR time | the recorded local run of the same sequence is the pre-merge evidence; the first dispatch is a post-merge item owned by the orchestrator; the 45-minute timeout stays provisional |
| R-30 The Java golden suite is coupled to the manifest (hard-coded `CLASS_FLOORS`, a tag-equality assertion, an on-disk-directory assertion, several consumers of `ConformanceTree.fixtures()`) | the `surface` partition and `DetailManifestCensusTest` land in P4 with the manifest change (D-109); P4 runs Gate G; the Python mirror test compares the Python map with the union of the two Java maps |
| R-29 Base B of the baseline record is rewritten by compaction and the retarget | the tolerant rule of section 6 (D-89): A is a literal sha on `main`, B carries a tree hash and `ephemeral: true`; the record test is re-run after compaction |
| R-31 The ruling A3 edits #5529 files that are still moving (`EventsController`, `EventData`, `Change`, `StreamSignal`, `MissionStatusQuery`, `MissionStatusQueryService`, `ArchitectureRules`): a later #5529 fold round conflicts with P1a, and the landing squad's tip (`dce114b2d`) is not in this repository | the C-005 pre-flight of section 5 runs first in P1 and P1a against the tip the orchestrator names; the stream symbols join the coupled list (S-22) and `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` pins their shape; a conflict is resolved by the orchestrator-run merge into the P1a lane (never on `main`), followed by `--check` and Gate G; P1a keeps every HTTP stream test unchanged, so a merge that changes their expectations is visible as a test failure and not as a silent semantic drift |
| R-32 The cap moves out of the controller, so the harness that sets `mission-status.stream.max-connections` through Spring properties no longer reaches the service the fixture built | `StreamRig.of` feeds the property to the fixture's service through a `FilesystemFixtureFactory` overload (test-support plumbing, named in P1a); `StreamCapacityTest` is unchanged and is the proof; the production wiring reads the same key through `MissionStatusProperties.Stream` |
| R-33 Excluding `java.lang.invoke..` from rules 1b and 2b may flag `invokedynamic` bootstrap references (records, lambdas, string concatenation) as dependencies of conforming classes; ArchUnit's treatment cannot be tested without a JDK | the recorded fallback of 15.4: P1b narrows to `java.lang.reflect..` plus a ban on `MethodHandles` calls and records the observation; the call ban on `Class.forName`/`ClassLoader.loadClass` stands either way |
| R-35 #5529's re-cut (operator rulings D-130 and D-131) renames `MissionStatusService` to `MissionStatusQueryService`, moves every class into the side-first tree and every port type into `dto/` and `exceptions/`; **every path, class name and package string this plan cites is a re-cut name**, the checkout still shows the flat layout, and class names are also held in string literals the compiler does not follow (readiness facts) | the mapping table of section 1.1 is the one place that relates old and new; P1 cannot start before the orchestrator has merged the re-cut tip into the branch; `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` loads each class by its new name and asserts the old name is gone; the string-literal grep of section 5 covers the literals; a mismatch is an orchestrator item (a decision note in both missions' records), not a phase decision |
| R-34 An `@EventListener(ContextClosedEvent)` that calls `endStreams()` on the primary `MissionStatusQuery` must reach the service the controllers use, also behind the test wrapper | `SpyQuery` delegates `endStreams()`; `StreamLifecycleTest` (graceful shutdown under 20 s) is the proof; a destroy method would run after the graceful wait and is not used (15.4) |
| R-36 The service in `application.query` constructs classes that the re-cut moves into `application.query.stream`, where a package-private class cannot be reached (`ChangeFeed` and its nested records are package-private at the checkout) | the classes P1a adds there are public; for a moved #5529 class the first step of P1a reads the re-cut tip and, if it stayed package-private while the service needs it, widens it as a named edit; rules 3 and 4 keep every adapter off `application.query.stream`, so public inside the application is not reachable from an adapter |
| R-37 The transport-agnostic claim covers type references, not the HTTP status that `StreamRefused.Kind#status()` carries (PLAN-FRESH4-009) | stated in section 1; the new refusal vocabulary (`DetailRefusal`, `TextRefusal`) carries no status; clause a of rule 16 covers the stream vocabulary's imports (planted `StreamSpring`); moving the status is E-11, owned by the plan of #6116 |
| R-38 Concurrent lanes each write a JaCoCo count derived on their own tree, and the merged file understates the class count without any gate noticing (PLAN-FRESH4-008) | the merge rule of the shared-file table (the lane merged second recounts with the command of section 2) and the one equality comparison in P13's gate |

## Unverified (could not be confirmed while planning)

1. Whether any check is a *required* status check on `main` (repository settings; not in the checkout).
2. No JDK on the planning host: nothing was compiled or run in Java. ArchUnit's dependency detection of a `ByteBuffer` local variable (as opposed to a call target) was reasoned from its model, not tested; the planted `A2cByteBuffer` control in P5 settles it. Whether `jacoco` counts the new records' accessors toward the 0.90 class line floor without extra tests (records generate methods) is confirmed by P3's first Gate G run.
3. The exact mapping from the `spec_kitty_events` reduction (`reduce_parsed`, `reduce`) to each FR-020 member (`agent`, `assignee`, `role`, `agent_profile`, `agent_profile_version`, `model`, `provider`, `subtasks`, `review_result`, review override, `actor`, history) was not read in full; P3a writes it into `data-model.md` as its first commit and the oracle gate (AC-20.1) decides.
4. `ReviewCycleArtifact.from_file` parse rules (what makes a cycle file "unparseable"; `reviewed_at`, `reviewer_agent` keys) and `FrontmatterManager.read` edge cases were read by name from the oracle, not by source; the corpus and the nightly comparison decide.
5. `sun.jnu.encoding` as seen by a GraalVM native binary on Linux/macOS, and whether the process-environment `LC_ALL` reaches it; P5 spike item. (`config.AppProcess` already passes an environment, `launch(Path, Path, Map, List, List)`, and the process leg of FRESH3-004 lives in the `config` test package, so that part is settled.)
6. Whether the generated `WorkPackage` model can omit `promptMarkdown` and whether the other generated models write the canonical bytes; decided by the P2 spike with the go/no-go rule of section 11.
7. Runner labels `macos-15`, `windows-2025`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (as in #5529).
8. Whether the generator may validate a golden whose oracle outcome violates the contract (a history stamp that is not an instant): the `knownDivergence` entry stores the oracle outcome without a schema check; P4 confirms the generator's own contract check does not run on that entry.
9. Whether `tests/ci/test_mission_status_routing.py`'s Packs floor (36) needs a raise for the extra `--deselect`: it asserts `>=`, so no; confirmed by reading, to be re-run in P0. Moot: E-2 was ruled with the default (2026-10-13), so no `--deselect` is added.
10. `AC-14.6`: whether the extended generator imports a `src` file the `contract_tools` router group does not name. The detail oracle imports `status.wp_view`, `status.wp_metadata`, `lanes.branch_naming`, `ownership.validation`, `review.cycle`, `workspace.context`, all of which the group names today; the derived-imports test in `tests/ci/test_contracts_workflows.py` is the authority.
11. The nightly job's runtime over 609 Missions and every Work Package (`timeout-minutes: 45` is provisional). It cannot be dispatched before `mission-status-api.yml` is on the default branch (introduced by the unmerged #5529); the first dispatch is a post-merge item for the orchestrator, and the pre-merge figure is the recorded local run.
12. Windows behaviour of junction fixtures and `lstat` on reparse points (reported `not run`, never a pass, when the runner cannot create one).
13. Whether `Release Readiness`'s `cutover-guard` passes on each retargeted PR (its first run is after the retarget; nothing runs it while a PR base is not `main`). Whether `--write-verification-metadata` executes `nativeSmoke`'s `doFirst`, which demands `-PnativeBinary` although the workflow's regeneration hint omits it (section 2 gives the fallback).
14. Whether `finalize-tasks` accepts a mission whose P0 files (`M/traces/baseline-red.yaml` and the tests appended to `test_mission_status_conformance.py`, which P4 owns afterwards) are owned by no Work Package: `--validate-only` decides; if it refuses, the orchestrator commits them before `finalize-tasks` runs and records the refusal in `traces/tooling-friction.md`.
15. The landing squad's tip `dce114b2d` (finding A3 and A4) is not an object of this repository; every symbol the ruling names was verified at the checkout (`4dc35c7d5`), and P1a and P1b re-check the shape facts against the tip they run on (R-31).
16. How ArchUnit reports `invokedynamic` bootstrap references (records, lambdas, string concatenation) once `java.lang.invoke..` leaves rules 1b and 2b, and whether `Class.getMethod` and friends are visible as method calls to `callMethodWhere`; no JDK on the planning host. The fallback is recorded in 15.4 and R-33.
17. Whether the re-cut tip adds a test beyond the ten files of 15.5 that names a symbol the ruling retypes: the compiler-completeness grep of P1a's gate and the compiler find it; it is a named edit of the same kind (15.5), never a reason to edit an assertion. At the flat checkout the grep finds exactly the ten files; from the P1 tip on it also finds `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` (a named edit of P1a) and, in P1a, the new tests.
18. The side-first layout of #5529's re-cut (section 1.1: `application.query` and its sub-packages, `domain.readmodel`, `domain.redaction`, `port/{in,out}/dto` and `port/{in,out}/exceptions`) is not in this repository: the plan cites the paths of the operator's ruling, not paths observed. It assumes that (a) the symbol shapes verified at the flat checkout move unchanged except for the renames of the mapping table, (b) the re-cut keeps `ProjectionCache` and the seven-argument service constructor package-private with their parameter types, and the stream classes the service needs reachable (R-36), and (c) it re-scopes only rules 3 and 4 of `ArchitectureRules` and the string literals of the readiness facts (R-35). **Update, 2026-10-13:** the orchestrator verified (a) to (c) as far as (b) goes on branch `landing/5529` (`ProjectionCache` stays package-private; the moved stream classes become public), and the re-cut itself is still in progress; the paths of the plan remain the operator's ruling until the tip is merged into this branch.

## 15. Operator ruling A3: the inbound port projects (live stream, admission cap, head projection, adapter gates)

### 15.1 The ruling, its source and what it binds

**Attribution (quoted for the decision record, D-118): "operator 2026-10-13, #5529 landing finding A3".** The ruling comes from the landing squad's architect report on the #5529 stack (findings A3 and A4, relayed by the coordinator). It is **binding on this plan: it is implemented, not weighed**, and no phase, review or later round may refute or soften it. The ruling reads, in the plan's words: the leak projection of the live stream lives only in the HTTP adapter and the inbound port hands out raw ledger rows, so a second inbound adapter (the MCP resource adapters of #6116) could bypass the projection, the stream cap and the shutdown registry; the plan must therefore (1) move the stream projection into an application `EventProjection` so the port emits a sealed, projected `StreamEvent` (status-transition, lifecycle, log-truncated) and never `Change` or `LineKind`, (2) move the admission counter into the subscribe path with a port refusal on capacity, (3) move the non-ULID mapping and the unparseable-cursor detail into `StreamScope` and `StreamCursorText`, (4) extract `MissionStatusService.projected` (now `MissionStatusQueryService`) and `withProjectedDates` into a reusable head projection that the #957 detail routes must use, and (5) generalise ArchUnit rule 3 to every `adapter.in.<x>` with a planted sibling-adapter fixture, confine controllers to `adapter.in..` with the one error controller allow-listed, and decide whether `java.lang.reflect` and `java.lang.invoke` leave rules 1b and 2b.

**What the checkout shows (read in full, cited by symbol and by its checkout name and path, the flat layout of the readiness facts; the finding's line numbers are not used; the mapping table of section 1.1 gives the re-cut names).** `EventData` holds the event allow-list (a `WP_ID` pattern `^WP[0-9]{2,}$`, a drop of a non-display target lane, `Instants.parse` of `at` raising `ProjectionError` for a missing or non-instant value, `Projector.strict` of `missionId` and `eventId`, the three actor handles through `Projector.strict(..., true)`) and builds the frame; `EventsController` holds the refusal-to-diagnostic mapping (a `ProjectionError` becomes one `projection_refused` diagnostic with the mid8 and the frame is dropped), the admission counter (`admitted`, `maxConnections` read by `@Value("${mission-status.stream.max-connections:128}")`), the shutdown registry (`live` and `@EventListener(ContextClosedEvent.class) endStreams`), `scope()` (a non-ULID `missionId` becomes `StreamRefused(UNKNOWN_MISSION)`), `parse()` (a `MISALIGNED` cursor text becomes the nested `UnparseableCursor`, which `ProblemAdvice` turns into the fixed detail `StreamCursorText.UNPARSEABLE_DETAIL`) and the nested `StreamCapacityReached`. `application.port.in.Change` (re-cut: `application.query.port.in.dto.Change`) carries a `LineKind` (so `StatusEvent` with `reason`, `reviewRef`, `reasonSource`, `claimAgent` unprojected), `StreamSignal.Forwarded` carries `ChangeBatch`, and `MissionStatusQuery#changesSince` returns it and has no production caller. `MissionStatusService#projected` and `#withProjectedDates` (re-cut: `MissionStatusQueryService`) are private. `ArchitectureRules` rule 3 has the subject `adapter.in.http..` only; rules 1b and 2b allow `java.lang..`. `config.ProblemErrorController` is a `@RestController` in `config`. The finding names the tip `dce114b2d`, which is not an object of this repository; the symbols above exist at the checkout (`4dc35c7d5`, which carries the J3, J6 and J8 folds the finding names), and P1a re-checks them against the tip it runs on (Unverified 15, R-31).

### 15.2 Classification, and the behaviour-preservation contract

**Classification (D-119).** The change is a **behaviour-preserving structural change to #5529 code, ordered as a campsite-style tidy-first step before every functional phase** (charter Standing Order 2, C-011, D-44), and it is **not a stated functional change**: no wire byte, status code, header, event order, diagnostic, cap value, property key or timing of the HTTP stream changes. Standing Order 2 does not by itself require it (this mission's routes do not touch the stream); the operator's ruling does, and it is placed with the campsite because it is the same kind of change and because P8's rules 15 and 16 and `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` are built on its result. Three honest limits, each recorded as an amendment (S-22):

- **C-011 names `MissionStatusService` (`MissionStatusQueryService` after the re-cut and S-26), the `FilesystemMissionEventSource*` classes, `Allowlist`, `ArchitectureRules` and the generator as the campsite surfaces.** Item 4 (the head projection) is on that list and is folded into **P1**, a pure campsite with every existing test unchanged. Items 1 to 3 (stream) touch `EventData`, `EventsController`, `Change`, `StreamSignal`, `StreamScope`, `StreamCursorText`, `StreamRefused`, `StreamSettings`, `ChangeFeed`, `StreamSubscriber`, `Resume`, `ProblemAdvice`: they are not on the list, so **P1a** is a widened campsite, not a pure one (S-22 widens the list for this ruling). Its commits carry the prefix `refactor(campsite)` (the red-first test commit `test(campsite)`); the first commit is a red-first characterisation (15.6).
- **"Existing tests unchanged" holds for what the tests assert, not for every line.** The HTTP stream suites keep every assertion, expected value and golden byte and are not edited at all (15.5). Ten files (eight tests, the helper `StreamFx` and the double `SpyQuery`; the closed list is in 15.5 and is the output of a grep, not a recollection) name a symbol that the ruling retypes; they receive **named, type-level edits** whose diff may change import lines, type names and the accessor an assertion reads from, never an expected value (the review brief greps the diff: a changed expected value, a deleted assertion or a weakened matcher in these files is a rejection).
- **C-005.** The coupled-symbol list gains the stream symbols above (S-22); `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest` (P1) carries their pre-ruling shape and **P1a edits those assertions to the post-ruling shape in the same commit that retypes the symbols** (a named edit; the test stays a lower-bound and presence check).

**The contract that makes it behaviour-preserving, and the tests that pin it.**

| What is preserved | Pinned by (assertions unchanged, green at the P1a and P1b tips) |
|---|---|
| Every SSE frame byte: event name, member order, nulls, `streamCursor`, the `id:` line on a Mission-scoped stream only, `log-truncated` without an id, the heartbeat comment | `adapter/in/http/stream/{StreamGoldenTest,StreamDeliveryTest,StreamTruncationTest,EventDataContractTest}`, the conformance stream trees, the native smoke stage `base` replay (stream requests are in its 37) |
| Every connect-time refusal: status, `code`, `detail`, order of decision (cursor without Mission, then cursor text, then scope, then capacity, then lookup and ledger probe), and a refusal sends no event | `StreamRefusalTest` (`aCursorTextThatDoesNotParseIsMisalignedWithTheFixedUnparseableDetail`, `eachRefusalIsOneRequestAndNoSubscriptionIsLeftOpen`), `StreamCapacityTest`, `RefusalRowsTest` and `SubscribeConnectTest` (type-level edit only: PLAN-FRESH3-002) |
| The cap: `mission-status.stream.max-connections`, default 128, checked before `subscribe()` costs a listing, a refused request takes no place, a finished stream frees its place | `StreamCapacityTest` (source unchanged; its rig reaches the service through test-support plumbing, 15.3 and 15.4) |
| Shutdown: every open stream ends first, so the container's graceful shutdown does not wait | `StreamLifecycleTest` (graceful shutdown under 20 s) |
| One `projection_refused` diagnostic per dropped frame (a refused event, and a refused truncation, which drops the frame and keeps the stream open), path-free, with the mid8, written on the connection's own thread through the same consumer line: in production the Spring `LoggingDiagnostics` bean, which `ServiceWiring` already hands the service; under the harness `FixtureHandles#recorder()` records **and** writes that same line (P1a plumbing, `FixtureHandlesLoggingTest` pins the parity) | `CanaryScanTest` (`aStrictFieldThatCannotBeRepresentedIsRefusedOnTheStreamWithAPathFreeDiagnosticAndNoEvent` and `anAtOrATimestampThatIsNotAnInstantDropsTheEventWithOneDiagnosticEach` read `code=projection_refused mid8=` from the captured log and stay byte for byte; PLAN-FRESH3-001); `StreamFaultTest` pins the other stream diagnostics (faults at connect and mid-stream), **not** this one |
| The pinned `EventsController` needles of `tests/ci` (`new SseEmitter(0L)`, `mission-status.stream.heartbeat-interval`, `.comment(" heartbeat")`, the `Last-Event-ID` and `streamCursor` parameter names, no import of `config`) | `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` (`events_controller_violations`): P1a keeps every needle and runs the file in its gate |

### 15.3 Exact classes per layer

`S` paths as in the header; package root `org.speckitty.missionstatus`. "Moved" means `git mv` plus the package line, with no logic change.

| Layer | Class | Change | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|
| `domain` | none | The projection helpers it needs already live in `domain.redaction` (`Projector`, `LeakClasses`, `ProjectionError`) and `domain.readmodel.overview` (`Instants`). No domain class is added or removed | none |
| `application.query.port.in.dto` | `StreamEvent` | N: sealed interface, records only, **projected values only**: `StatusTransition(String missionId, String eventId, String wpId, String fromStatusLane, String toStatusLane, String at, ActorHandles actor, boolean force, StreamCursor cursor)`, `Lifecycle(String missionId, String eventId, String eventType, String at, StreamCursor cursor)`, `LogTruncated(String missionId, String truncationCause, long detectedAtOffset, StreamCursor resetCursor)` (`truncationCause` holds the existing wire word of `StreamSignal.Truncated.reason().wire()`, which `EventData` writes under the wire key `reason`; PLAN-FRESH3-003), and the nested record `ActorHandles(String tool, String role, String profile)` (each nullable, strict-projected). `resumeCursor()` returns the cursor after the row for the first two and empty for `LogTruncated` (the reset cursor is data, never an id). No member named `reason`, `reviewRef`, `reasonSource` or `claimAgent` exists anywhere in the type, and `PortLeakCanaryTest` asserts those four names without an exception | P1a |

| `application.query.port.in.exceptions` | `StreamCapacityReached` | N: `public final class` (unchecked, no message detail, no stack: the moved body of the nested controller class) | P1a |
| `application.query.port.in.exceptions` | `StreamRefused` | C: optional fixed `detail()`; the existing constructor and the six `Kind` values are untouched (so `PortTypesTest` keeps its exact-set assertion) | P1a |

| `application.query.port.in.dto` | `StreamCursorText` | C: the unparseable case throws `StreamRefused(MISALIGNED, UNPARSEABLE_DETAIL)` (the moved `UnparseableCursor` mapping); `NEGATIVE` is unchanged; `UNPARSEABLE_DETAIL` stays public | P1a |
| `application.query.port.in.dto` | `StreamRequest` | N: `record StreamRequest(StreamScope scope, Optional<StreamCursor> cursor)` with `static StreamRequest parse(String missionIdOrNull, String cursorTextOrNull)`: it applies the legacy order once for every adapter (cursor text without a Mission is `CURSOR_WITHOUT_MISSION`; then `StreamCursorText.parse`; then `StreamScope.parse`) | P1a |
| `application.query.port.in` | `Subscription` | C (kept, retyped): the interface stays (`next(Duration)`, `close()`); its signal type is the port's `StreamSignal` with `Events`, `ProjectedSubscription` implements it, and the raw copy is `FeedSubscription` (PLAN-FRESH3-009) | P1a |
| `application.query.port.in.dto` | `Change`, `ChangeBatch` | **Removed from the port**: moved (`git mv`) to `application.query.stream` as public records (raw feed vocabulary, `LineKind` stays inside; application-internal, unreachable from an adapter) | P1a |
| `application.query.stream` | `EventProjection` | N: public final class over `LeakClasses` and the injected `Consumer<Diagnostic>`: `Optional<StreamEvent> project(Change)` **and** `Optional<StreamEvent> project(FeedSignal.Truncated)`; it holds the moved `WP_ID` pattern, the display-lane drop, the instant rule, the actor-handle projection and the refusal-to-diagnostic mapping: a `ProjectionError`, for a change or for a truncation, writes one `Diagnostic(PROJECTION_REFUSED, mid8, null)` and yields an empty `Optional`, which `ProjectedSubscription#next` turns into `Events(List.of())`, so the stream goes on exactly as `EventsController#send` and `#sendTruncation` did (drop the frame, write the diagnostic, keep the stream open; PLAN-FRESH3-012). `EventProjectionTest` has the truncation-refusal case (a `LeakClasses` that flags the Mission id) | P1a |

Visibility of the moved #5529 classes: R-36 | P1a |

| test support | `StreamRig`, `FilesystemFixtureFactory`, `FixtureHandles` (and `SpyQuery`, counted among the ten of 15.5) | C (plumbing only): `SpyQuery` implements the retyped methods and delegates `endStreams`; `StreamRig.of` reads `mission-status.stream.max-connections` out of its property list and hands it to `FilesystemFixtureFactory` through a new overload that takes a `StreamSettings`, because the fixture's service (not the controller) now owns the cap; **`FixtureHandles#recorder()` records the diagnostic and also writes the path-free line of `config.LoggingDiagnostics`** (logger `org.speckitty.missionstatus.diagnostics`, level WARN, text `diagnostic code=<wire> mid8=<mid8> line=<n or ->`), because the projection's diagnostic is now written by the fixture service, whose consumer is the recorder, and no longer by the controller's Spring consumer; `FixtureHandlesLoggingTest` pins the parity of the two lines, so `CanaryScanTest` stays unchanged (PLAN-FRESH3-001). `FilesystemFixtureFactory#fromTreeWithOrdering` keeps reflecting the package-private seven-argument constructor (kept and pinned by P1) | P1a |

### 15.4 The five items, as decided

**Item 1 (EventProjection and the sealed StreamEvent, D-120).** `EventProjection` is the only code that turns a raw row into something a consumer can see. The projection happens inside `ProjectedSubscription#next` and `MissionStatusQueryService#changesSince`, **on the consuming thread and after the raw signal has been queued**, so the raw feed (its bounded queue, the slow-consumer overflow, the floors, the order of signals) is exactly what #5529 built and `ChangeFeedTest` keeps its scenarios. A ledger row the allow-list does not admit (an invalid work package id, a non-display target lane) yields no event, a projection refusal yields one diagnostic and no event, as before. `StreamEvent` carries strings and booleans and the two `StreamCursor` values; the JSON shaping (sorted keys, explicit nulls, the nested `streamCursor` object, the event names `status-transition`, `mission-lifecycle`, `log-truncated`) stays in `EventData`, the one HTTP-specific piece. An MCP adapter serialises a `StreamEvent` and cannot reach a raw row: `PortExposesNoLedgerRowTest` (P1b) proves it for the types, `PortLeakCanaryTest` (P1a) for a planted ledger.

**Item 2 (admission in the subscribe path, D-121).** `MissionStatusQueryService#subscribe` admits before it resolves (the old controller order: the check ran before `subscribe()` cost a listing), releases on any exception, and hands out a `ProjectedSubscription` whose `close` releases once; the live registry belongs to the same `StreamAdmission`, so one cap and one shutdown registry serve **every** inbound adapter of the service (an MCP subscription and an HTTP stream draw from the same 128 places). The refusal is `StreamCapacityReached`, a port type; `ProblemAdvice` keeps the 503 and the code `stream_capacity_reached` through `ProblemMapper.streamCapacity()` unchanged. `@EventListener(ContextClosedEvent.class)` cannot live in `application` (rule 2a: no Spring), so the Spring event stays in `config.StreamShutdown`, which calls the port's `endStreams()`. A destroy method on the bean would run **after** the web server's graceful shutdown has waited (the context publishes `ContextClosedEvent` and stops the lifecycle beans before it destroys singletons), which is why the listener is kept and not replaced by `AutoCloseable`. The property key and default are unchanged (`mission-status.stream.max-connections`, 128); only the reader changes (`MissionStatusProperties.Stream`). `StreamAdmission` (a refusing cap on open streams, 128) is a different mechanism from `AdmissionGate` (FR-022, P8: a fair four-permit semaphore that queues the heavy detail operations and never refuses); the two share no state and no name. `StreamCapacityTest` keeps its source because `StreamRig.of` now feeds the property to the fixture's service (15.3, test-support row); the HTTP-visible behaviour (the 503, the freed place, a refused request takes no place, `totalSubscriptions` unchanged by a refusal) is identical.

**Item 3 (StreamScope, StreamCursorText, StreamRequest, D-122).** The two mappings leave the controller; `StreamRefused` gains an optional fixed detail instead of a controller-nested exception, so the existing `Kind` set (pinned by `PortTypesTest`) does not change. `StreamRequest.parse` fixes the order of the three decisions once, so a second adapter cannot reorder them.

**Item 4 (reusable head projection, D-123; answers PLAN-FRESH2-003).** The campsite P1 does not only move `projected` and `withProjectedDates` into `OverviewReader`: it extracts them into `MissionHeadProjection` and makes **`OverviewReader#head(ref)` the only way to obtain a projected head**. `head` returns `MissionHead(overview, entry)`: the projected `MissionOverview` and the `ProjectionCache.Entry` (the whole `StatusSnapshot` with its per-Work-Package state) it was derived from, in **one pass** (`readMeta`, the stamp, at most one ledger read). `overview(ref)` (the `/missions` item) delegates to it, so `/missions` and `getMission` share one derivation, one projection and one cache by construction (AC-1.2, NFR-009). The detail services take the per-Work-Package snapshot from `MissionHead#entry`; none of them may call `OverviewReader#ledger(ref, mid8)` (a second stamp, hence a fourth `meta.json` read: 4 warm and 5 cold, which fails the pinned count of AC-1.8(c)). `MissionReadModel` exposes `find` and `head`. Two tests make it required and not optional: `OverviewReaderTest` and `MissionReadModelTest` (P1: `head` records the same reads as `overview` did, 2 warm and 3 cold; `find` followed by `head` records 3 warm and 4 cold, the AC-1.8(c) totals, on the extracted path) and `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest` (P1: an ArchUnit call-site gate with a non-vacuous floor and a planted `application` class `HeadBypass` that calls `OverviewDeriver#derive` or `MissionHeadProjection` directly; red). P9 repeats the 3 and 4 on the **real** `MissionDetailQueryService` (its `MissionDetailMetaReadCountTest`, in its exit gate) and P10 and P11 on theirs.

**Item 5 (ArchUnit, D-124).** Done in **P1b**, after P1a, so each gate runs against the tree it protects and is shown to bite by a planted violation:

| Gate | Definition | Subject and floor | Planted control (turns exactly the named rule red) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rule 3 generalised | For every class in `adapter.in.<x>..`, a dependency on a project class must be in `application.query.port.in..`, `domain..` or the same `adapter.in.<x>..`; a dependency on a sibling `adapter.in.<y>..` is a violation (a custom `ArchCondition` comparing the origin's and the target's adapter segment; the text of rule 3 is "every `adapter.in.<x>` depends, among project packages, only on `application.query.port.in`, `domain` and itself") | every class in `adapter.in..`; the production run also pins the **discovered set of `adapter.in.<x>` packages** to `{http}` with a non-zero scanned count per entry, so the first MCP adapter forces its own entry and its own count | `fixture.adapter.in.other.UsesService` (a sibling adapter depending on `application.query.Service`: red on rule 3 only); the existing `H3aInternal` and `H3bConfig` stay |
| Rule 3b controllers | A class annotated `@RestController`, `@Controller`, `@RestControllerAdvice` or `@ControllerAdvice` resides in `adapter.in..`, **except `config.ProblemErrorController`**, allow-listed by fully qualified name | every annotated class of the import; floor 5 in production (`ProjectController`, `MissionsController`, `EventsController`, `ContractDocumentController`, `ProblemAdvice`) plus the allow-listed one | `fixture.config.RogueController` (annotated, in `config`: red on rule 3 only); the conforming skeleton holds `fixture.config.ProblemErrorController` and stays green |
| Rule 1c and 2d reflection | The domain and the application do not call `Class.forName`, `ClassLoader.loadClass`, or `Class.getMethod`/`getMethods`/`getConstructor`/`getConstructors`/`getField`/`getFields`/`getDeclared*` | `domain..` and `application..` | `fixture.domain.D1cReflect`, `fixture.application.query.A2dForName` (red on rule 1, rule 2) |
| Rules 1b and 2b | `java.lang.reflect..` and `java.lang.invoke..` are removed from what `java.lang..` allows | as 1b and 2b | `fixture.domain.D1cReflect` also names a `java.lang.reflect.Method` field (red on rule 1) |
| Rule 13 ports are interfaces only | The one definition of section 13 (rule 13, clauses a to d, nested classes judged by their outermost class); the stream types of 15.3 are placed accordingly. The "exceptions holds only Throwables" clause is clause c and is judged on the outermost class, so the nested `Kind` enums of `StreamRefused`, `SourceFault` and `TextFault` are not violations (PLAN-FRESH3-005) | `application.query.port..`, non-zero for both roots and the four subpackages | `DtoInRoot`, `ExceptionInRoot`, `ExceptionInDto`, `RecordInExceptions`, `Stray` (each red on rule 13 only) |
| Rule 14 query side independent of the command side | `application.query..` depends on nothing in `application.command..` (section 13) | `application.query..`, non-zero in production | `fixture.application.query.ReadsCommand` (red on rule 14 only) |
| Port exposes no ledger row | No class in `application.query.port.in..` depends on `domain.model.LineKind`, `StatusEvent`, `LifecycleEvent`, `LedgerLine` | `application.query.port.in..`, floor = its class count | `fixture.application.query.port.in.LeakyQuery` (a method returning a `LineKind`) |
| Head projection required | (P1) as in item 4 | `application.query..` outside `OverviewReader`, `MissionHeadProjection` | `fixture.application.query.HeadBypass` |

**The allow-list of rule 3b is a permanent structural exception, not transient debt** (Standing Order 5): the container dispatches an error to `/error`, outside the `/api/v1` prefix that applies to the controllers of `adapter.in.http`, so the error controller cannot live there without changing the path; the entry is by fully qualified name, has no expiry, and a stale-entry guard (the allow-listed class must exist in the import, or the test is red) keeps it from rotting. Rule ids of 1 to 12 do not change: rule 3b is a part of rule 3 (as 1a and 1b are parts of rule 1), 1c and 2d are parts of rules 1 and 2, so `DEFINED_RULE_COUNT` is raised by P1b only for the two new numbered rules 13 and 14 (12 to 14) and becomes 16 at P8; rules 15 and 16 are FR-013's former 13 and 14, renumbered twice (S-25).

**The reflection decision (D-124).** Adopted, in two parts: the two packages leave 1b and 2b (the main sources import neither today, verified by grep), and the call ban closes the example the finding gives (`Class.forName("java.nio.file.Files")`), which a package exclusion alone leaves open because `java.lang.Class` stays allowed. The exclusion of `java.lang.invoke` carries a **recorded fallback**: if ArchUnit reports the `invokedynamic` bootstrap references of records, lambdas or string concatenation (`java.lang.invoke.MethodHandles$Lookup`, `StringConcatFactory`) as dependencies of conforming domain or application classes, rule 1b and 2b go red on the production tree; P1b then narrows the exclusion to `java.lang.reflect..` and bans calls to `MethodHandles` factory methods instead, and records the observation in `traces/tooling-friction.md`. There is no JDK on the planning host, so this cannot be settled here (Unverified 16).

### 15.5 Test edits, closed list

**Unchanged (no edit at all):** `adapter/in/http/stream/{StreamGoldenTest,StreamDeliveryTest,StreamTruncationTest,StreamCapacityTest,StreamLifecycleTest,StreamFaultTest,StreamRefusalTest,EventDataContractTest,ValidatedEvents,Cursors,StreamRefusalContract}`, `adapter/in/http/flow/CanaryScanTest`, `adapter/in/http/contract/{ContractValidator,ContractValidatorTest}`, the golden suites, every file of `tests/ci`, and every scenario of `ChangeFeedTest` (which changes only the renamed raw types, below).

**Named type-level edits (the ten files below: eight tests, the helper `StreamFx` and the double `SpyQuery`; at the checkout this list is the output of `grep -rlE '\b(Change|ChangeBatch|StreamSignal|Subscription)\b|changesSince' S/src/test`, and the compiler finds any further file the re-cut adds, Unverified 17):** `application/{PortTypesTest,SubscribeConnectTest,StreamFx,BlankFinalLineCursorTest,ChangeFeedTest}`, `conformance/port/Ac3ResumeAndBatchTest`, `adapter/out/filesystem/{FilesystemMissionEventSourceStampTest,FilesystemMissionEventSourceAllowlistFlowTest}`, `adapter/in/http/rest/RefusalRowsTest`, `testsupport/http/SpyQuery` (test packages mirror the re-cut main packages, section 1.1). **`RefusalRowsTest` (PLAN-FRESH3-002):** `anUnexpectedExceptionIsAGeneric500ProblemWithoutDetail` builds an anonymous `MissionStatusQuery` whose `changesSince` returns the old `ChangeBatch` and which has no `endStreams()`; the edit changes the return type to `StreamBatch`, adds an `endStreams()` method with an empty body and nothing else (the `IllegalStateException` bodies of `project()` and `missions()`, the `UnsupportedOperationException` bodies and every assertion stay); P1a owns the file. `ChangeFeedTest` changes only the renamed raw types (`FeedSignal`, `FeedSubscription`); the others read `StreamEvent` components where they read `LineKind` and `Change` before. The two `PortTypesTest` cases that validate `Change` and `ChangeBatch` stay in the test package of `application.query.stream`, where those records now live. **Plumbing edits, three test-support files and no assertion in them:** `FilesystemFixtureFactory` (the `StreamSettings` overload), `FixtureHandles` (`recorder()` also logs), `StreamRig` (the cap property).

### 15.6 Gates, red-first and revert-fails

- **P1 (item 4).** Red-first form: every existing test unchanged and green on the base and the tip; the new tests are new. Gate G; `OverviewReaderTest`, `MissionReadModelTest`, `MissionHeadProjectionTest`, `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest`, `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest`. Revert: make a second class derive its own head (or make `head` re-read `meta.json`) and `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest` or the read-count assertion goes red.
- **P1a (items 1 to 3).** First commit (ATDD, C-011): `PortLeakCanaryTest`, a port-level test that plants a ledger whose status rows carry a credential-shaped `reason`, a path-shaped `reviewRef` and an address in `reasonSource` and `claimAgent`, subscribes and reads `changesSince` through `MissionStatusQuery` only, and asserts that nothing the port returns (a record-component walk and `toString` of every signal and event) holds a canary and that no returned type has a member named like a raw field. **It is red on the base** (the port returns `StatusEvent`) and green at the tip. Then, one concern per commit: `EventProjectionTest` (every branch of the moved allow-list, with the same cases `EventData` had through HTTP, and the truncation whose projection is refused: one diagnostic, an empty `Optional`, the stream open), `StreamAdmissionTest` (port level: the cap-plus-one `subscribe` throws `StreamCapacityReached`; a closed subscription frees its place once; a failed `subscribe` frees its place; a refused call takes none; `endStreams` closes all and is idempotent), `StreamScopeTest`/`StreamCursorTextTest`/`StreamRequestTest` (the moved mappings and the order of the three decisions), `StreamSettingsTest` (`maxConnections < 1`, the three-argument constructor), `PlainStreamCallerTest` (the AC-13.3 plain-Java caller extended to the stream: no HTTP type, strings in, `StreamEvent` out), `FixtureHandlesLoggingTest` (the recorder's log line equals the `LoggingDiagnostics` line). Gate G, plus the unchanged HTTP stream suites and `CanaryScanTest` of 15.2, `tests/ci/test_mission_status_api_build.py` (the `EventsController` needles) and `-k "events_controller or http_adapter_packages"`. Revert: move the `WP_ID` check back into `EventData` and `EventProjectionTest` and `PortLeakCanaryTest` go red; put the counter back in the controller and `StreamAdmissionTest` goes red (no HTTP is involved); drop the order of `StreamRequest.parse` and `StreamRequestTest` goes red.
- **P1b (item 5).** Red-first form: each planted fixture is red on the rule it targets before the rule is generalised (the fixture is committed first and shown red against the old rule 3, which does not reach `adapter.in.other`), green on the tip with the production tree. Gate G; `ArchitectureControlsTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`, `PortExposesNoLedgerRowTest`. Revert: restore the old subject of rule 3 and `UsesService` stops turning red (the control fails the build); delete the allow-list entry and the production run is red on `ProblemErrorController`; add a `@RestController` to `config` and rule 3b is red; put a record in a port root, an exception in a `dto/` package, a record in an `exceptions/` package or a class in another port subpackage and rule 13 is red; make `application.query` reach `application.command` and rule 14 is red.

### 15.7 JaCoCo, native image, coupled symbols, shared files

- **JaCoCo class counts** (`R/jacoco-class-floor.properties`, `application` key; `domain` is unchanged by this ruling). P1 adds `MissionLookup`, `OverviewReader`, `MissionReadModel`, `MissionHeadProjection` and `MissionHead` (and their nested classes). P1a adds `StreamEvent` with its four nested records, `StreamBatch`, `StreamRequest`, `StreamCapacityReached`, `EventProjection`, `ProjectedSubscription`, `StreamAdmission`, `FeedSubscription`, `FeedSignal` with its five nested variants, and `StreamSignal$Events` (the port's `StreamSignal` gains it), and drops the nested `StreamSignal$Forwarded` and `StreamSignal$Truncated` (PLAN-FRESH3-013); `Change` and `ChangeBatch` move within the `application.*` pattern and do not change the count. Each phase writes the number its own command prints (section 2, row on `jacoco-class-floor.properties`); a class that a phase removes **lowers** the number it must write, because `jacocoScopeCheck` fails when the recorded count exceeds the classes found. The side-first re-cut (D-130, D-131) moves no class out of the `domain.*` and `application.*` patterns, which cross package separators (section 1.1), so neither the moves nor the `MissionStatusService` rename changes a count; the nested and new classes land in `port/{in,out}/dto`, `exceptions` and `application.query.stream` and are counted there. Every new application class meets the 0.90 per-class line floor through the tests of 15.6 (`EventProjection` is branch-complete by `EventProjectionTest`); P1b is test-only and changes no count.
- **Native image.** No new reflective access: `StreamEvent` is not serialised by Jackson (the adapter writes sorted maps), `EventProjection` and `StreamAdmission` are plain Java, `StreamShutdown` is an ordinary Spring bean with an `@EventListener` method that AOT processes, and the new `maxConnections` component of the `@ConfigurationProperties` record binds like `queueCapacity` does. `MissionStatusApplicationHints` stays unchanged unless `nativeTest` proves a miss (the existing rule). P1a adds no `@Tag("native")` test (the pinned count stays 3 until P13, D-99); the native smoke stage `base` is the byte-for-byte evidence on the four legs.
- **Shared files.** `MissionStatusQueryService`, `ServiceWiring` (P1 owns both; P1a is a named edit, so the "edited exactly once" sentence of section 9 now reads "twice, both before P3"), `ProblemAdvice` (P1a owns; P8 named edit), `ArchitectureRules`, `ArchitectureControlsTest`, `ArchitectureRulesTest`, `CoupledSymbolsShapeTest`, the stream test doubles: all in the table of section 11.
- **Naming hygiene.** No new package or fixture directory is named `build` (`SOURCE_PACKAGES_NAMED_LIKE_IGNORED_DIRECTORIES`; the service `.gitignore` anchors `/build/` and re-includes `!/src/test/java/org/speckitty/missionstatus/build/`). `/out/` is anchored at the service root and `!/src/**/out/` re-includes any `out` directory below `src`, so `application/query/port/out` with its `dto/` and `exceptions/` subpackages, and the fixture trees below them, are not ignored (the existing `port/out` already relies on this; the pin's probe list covers the new directories after P1's edit, section 1.1 step 3a); `adapter/in/other`, `application/query/port/in/helpers` and `application/command` (fixture tree) are fine.

### 15.8 Consistency with section 7 (#5533) and the MCP claim

The live-stream projection and the admission cap are now **seams for #6116** exactly as the text-read port is a seam for #5533: section 7.5 lists them, with their commits. They are **not** primitives #5533 imports (governance routes read no live stream), so "primitives complete" for #5533 stays the P8 commit; but the stability promise of section 7 binds these names too, and the transport-agnostic claim of section 1 (type references; its stated limit on the HTTP status of the stream refusal, E-11, stands) is now true of the stream and not only of the five new operations: an MCP adapter calls `StreamRequest.parse`, `MissionStatusQuery#subscribe` and `Subscription#next`, receives only `StreamEvent`, shares the one cap and the one shutdown registry, and duplicates no rule. The head projection (item 4) is likewise a seam, but an **application-internal** one: every implementation of an inbound port derives its Mission head through `MissionReadModel#head` (enforced by `HeadProjectionOwnershipTest`), and a future adapter reaches a head only through the inbound ports, because rule 3 bars it from `application.query.MissionReadModel` and the accessors are package-visible (PLAN-FRESH3-008). The stability promise of section 7 binds the application-side names only.
